### F-224 — the failure is injected by SQL source text, so any rewording of the tombstone statement silently moves the injected failure to a different call site while the same assertions still pass — the "not swallowed" guarantee can stop covering…

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R5-E10-07`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI
- Severity: Low
- Status: REFUTED — premise measured dead at HEAD: the injected failure is already role-bound — `tests/m11-t1-skill-vector-sql.test.mjs:154-170` binds the tombstone to its statement by role and call ordinal rather than by source text. (was: OPEN)
- Root cause: the failure is injected by SQL source text, so any rewording of the tombstone statement silently moves the injected failure to a different call site while the same assertions still pass — the "not swallowed" guarantee can stop covering the intended statement with no red signal. (It is also the only test-side coverage of the R5-E05-02/03 tombstone, which is why those findings went unnoticed.) Leaked temp trees accumulate invisibly on constrained /tmp.
- Evidence: Where: tests/m11-t1-skill-vector-sql.test.mjs:160-176; tests/m7-t3-tools-contract.test.mjs:143-147 · Evidence: dbExecutor: async (sql) => { if (sql.includes("DELETE FROM skills.catalog_index")) return ""; throw new Error("PSQL_REJECTED_SIMULATION"); } for a test named "failure escalation — a failed failSql is surfaced, not swallowed"; and cleanup try { fs.rmSync(tempDir, …) } catch { void 0; }.
- Reproduction: Static — the fault is injected by matching SQL source text, not by targeting the tombstone call: `tests/m11-t1-skill-vector-sql.test.mjs:165-168` uses a `dbExecutor` that returns an empty string when `sql.includes("DELETE FROM skills.catalog_index")` and otherwise throws `PSQL_REJECTED_SIMULATION`, inside the test `M11-04: failure escalation — a failed failSql is surfaced, not swallowed` (`:153`), then asserts `report.status === "FAILED"` (`:170`), `report.failed_count === 1` (`:171`) and `report.error_code === "VECTOR_FAILURE_WRITE_FAILED"` (`:172`). The matched substring is the production tombstone at `zink-skill-store/src/vector-sync.mjs:626-631`, so rewording that statement makes the `includes()` miss it and the injected throw silently migrates to whichever statement runs first — the same three assertions still pass while the "not swallowed" guarantee stops covering the tombstone. Reproduce by rewording the `vector-sync.mjs` DELETE (rename the table or prepend a comment) and confirming `node --test tests/m11-t1-skill-vector-sql.test.mjs` stays green. The companion leak is `tests/m7-t3-tools-contract.test.mjs:142-146`, whose cleanup swallows removal failures so temp trees accumulate. Fix gates in `tests/m11-t1-skill-vector-sql.test.mjs`: inject the failure by statement role or call ordinal, or assert the exact SQL the failing executor received, rather than by a substring that can drift.
- Acceptance criteria: the `M11-04` executor in `tests/m11-t1-skill-vector-sql.test.mjs:153-180` binds the injected failure to the tombstone call by statement role or call ordinal, or asserts the exact SQL text the failing executor received, so rewording the DELETE at `zink-skill-store/src/vector-sync.mjs:626-634` turns the test red instead of silently moving `PSQL_REJECTED_SIMULATION` to a different statement while `:170-176` stay green - and the companion cleanup at `tests/m7-t3-tools-contract.test.mjs:141-147` no longer swallows `rmSync` failures.
- Related findings: bug mở: R5-E05-02 · nguồn: E10 — test infrastructure: the source-text assertion…

### F-248 — a result recorded without a payload digest wedges the task in `RESULT_PENDING_REVIEW`: every owner verdict demands a digest that was never stored

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R7-D11B-02`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: High
- Status: REFUTED — premise measured dead at HEAD: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:1420` reads `payload.payload_digest ?? reducerMintedResultDigest(task)` — the conditional store the row describes is gone (closed with F-077 in `832f082`); `requireResultIdentityMatch` admits an unbound verdict when the task has no root result and the digest is the reducer-minted one, and `tests/m7-lane-a-workflow.test.mjs:484` drives ACCEPT on a digest-less record. (was: LATENT)
- Root cause: the event contract makes `payload_digest` optional and the reducer stores it only when present, while every verdict path begins by requiring that stored digest to be a string. The task has no exit except cancellation, and the failure looks like an owner decision problem rather than a missing field.
- Evidence: Where: integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:1207-1210 (`task.workflow_status = "RESULT_PENDING_REVIEW"; … if (payload.payload_digest) task.execution_payload_digest = payload.payload_digest;`) vs :1580-1583 (`const currentDigest = task.root_result?.payload_digest ?? task.execution_payload_digest; requireCondition(typeof currentDigest === "string", "RESULT_MISMATCH");`) · Evidence: the comment at :1584-1587 records that a previous null-versus-undefined bug "made EVERY verdict fail and wedged the task in review" — the same wedge remains for the absent-digest case.
- Reproduction: static — contract-legal but not reachable from the current emitter, so the row stays `LATENT`: `index.mjs:2424-2428` always names the key, and when it computes `payloadDigest = null` (no artifact set and no artifact file, :2313-2319) the append is rejected outright by `validateContract("ledger-event", …)` at `topology/lib/ledger.mjs:543` — verified by handing a synthetic event with a null `payload_digest` to that validator, which throws `SCHEMA_INVALID: SCHEMA_VALIDATION_FAILED`. The live digest-less case is F-077's; the absent-key wedge here fires only for a new emitter or a hand-written event. `WORKFLOW_RESULT_RECORDED {task_id}` with no digest from EXECUTING leaves the status set and no digest; ACCEPT, REJECT, BLOCK and REQUEST_REVISION all throw `RESULT_MISMATCH`, and APPROVAL_DECIDED refuses the status.
- Acceptance criteria: either `payload_digest` is required in that contract branch, or verdict binding tolerates an unbound result; a test drives ACCEPT after a digest-less record.
- Related findings: successor to F-077 (that row records the wedge, this one names the mechanism), F-226, F-113, F-249 · nguồn: R7 delta audit D11B

### F-293 — model-service response text is interpolated unescaped into the acceptance SQL literals, so a hostile or malformed tag response writes arbitrary statements

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R7-C4-06`
- Affected modules: deployment topology, Zink Knowledgebase
- Severity: Medium
- Status: REFUTED — premise measured dead at HEAD: commit `88cdf31` already landed the row's remediation. `infrastructure/docker/scripts/knowledge-t1.ps1:165-174` validates the provider value against its documented shape (`^[0-9a-f]{64}$`) before any use, and the digest reaches the client as a psql VARIABLE (`:'model_digest'`) instead of an interpolated literal; the same gate landed in `knowledge-t3.ps1:171` and `knowledge-t5-common.ps1:371`. No provider response text reaches a report, a log line or a file: the only provider-derived value used is the digest, shape-gated to 64 lowercase hex, and every throw in the driver is a static string. The row's own line anchors (`:119`, `:137-147`, `:166-173`, `:212`) describe the pre-fix file. (was: LATENT)
- Root cause: the acceptance scripts take fields straight out of the local model service's response and splice them into single-quoted SQL literals inside a here-string passed to the database client. The strongest validation anywhere on these paths is a truthiness test at one site, and the value's shape is never constrained. Impact is bounded because the target is an ephemeral acceptance database, but the pattern is exactly the one the repository's safety rules forbid: untrusted response data used as command text.
- Evidence: Where: `infrastructure/docker/scripts/knowledge-t1.ps1:119` — the guard is only `if (-not $model -or -not $model.digest)` · Where: `:137-147` — the insert with `'$($model.digest)'` at `:144` · Where: `:166-173` — the second statement repeating it · Where: `infrastructure/docker/scripts/knowledge-t3.ps1:157` and `knowledge-t5.ps1:174` — the same interpolation shape, and the t3 site is worse than the row allowed: `:154` assigns the model value from the tags endpoint with no guard at all, not even the truthiness test · Where: `knowledge-t1.ps1:212` — a third statement repeating the same interpolation · Where: the response is fetched from the local tags endpoint by `Invoke-RestMethod`, so its fields are provider-controlled.
- Reproduction: unconfirmed — a tags response carrying a digest containing a quote and a statement terminates inside the literal and produces two statements where one was intended. Before fixing, measure whether any lane or operator run feeds a non-validated endpoint; the intended assertion is a charset check plus parameter passing, exercised by a fixture whose digest contains a quote.
- Acceptance criteria: every provider-derived value is validated against its documented shape before use and passed to the database client as a parameter rather than interpolated, and a fixture with a quote-bearing value proves the statement cannot be split.
- Related findings: F-264, F-052, F-103 · nguồn: R7-C delta audit C4 — docker compose overlays and acceptance scripts

### F-299 — the native-receipts containment case branches on the outcome under test and its positive branch asserts a test-built object is truthy

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R7-C5B-B`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Gates + CI
- Severity: Medium
- Status: REFUTED — premise measured false 2026-10-18 under the owner's ruling; the row stays in the queue per §0.2 rule 25 and the deletion threshold, because a refuted premise is an owner decision, not pending work.
- Root cause: the fixture reliably takes the allow path, because the plugin falls back to the recorded parent identity, and the assertion chosen for that branch is that a snapshot task object exists. That predicate holds for any task the test itself created, so the control the title names — a rejected ledger write must block the run — is only ever verified in the branch the fixture does not reach.
- Evidence: Where: `tests/m7-lane-d3-native-receipts.test.mjs:210-223` — `if (decision.allow === true) { const after = readSnapshot(paths).tasks[taskId]; assert.ok(after); } else { …blocked and BLOCKED assertions… }`, with the comment at `:213-214` explaining that the plugin reconciles this input · Where: the production containment is the subagent-ended path in `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs` that must set the task to blocked when the write cannot be applied.
- Reproduction: REFUTED by code read 2026-10-18, chain re-opened line by line — the fixture records `parent_session_key: main-session` at `tests/m7-lane-d3-native-receipts.test.mjs:89` and `:103`, then hands the hook `requesterSessionKey: forged-parent-session` at `:209`; `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:8000-8001` prefers the supplied value with no fallback to the recorded parent, so the event payload carries the forged key, `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:408-414` matches zero delegations and raises LINEAGE_MISMATCH, and `index.mjs:8021-8031` returns the block helper's shape at `:7954-7956` (`allow: false`, `blocked: true`). The case therefore runs the ELSE branch at `:218-223`, which is the branch holding the containment assertions the row said were unreached; the row's error came from repeating the test's own stale comment at `:213-214`. What survives is far smaller and is a test-shape weakness, not a containment gap: the allow branch at `:215-217` asserts only that a task object the test itself created is truthy, so it would say nothing if a future change made that path reachable. Original claim kept below for the record — static mutation probe — make the production path never fail on an unreconcilable requester session so it always allows, and the case passes through the vacuous branch. A red test removes the conditional, constructs the input deterministically producing a rejected write, and asserts the block decision and the blocked status on that exact input.
- Acceptance criteria: the case has one deterministic path with an assertion that is false when the containment is removed; no branch of the test may be satisfied by the mere existence of fixture state.
- Related findings: F-219, F-298, F-088 · nguồn: R7-C delta audit C5B — test validity, second half

### F-324 — the router placeholder convention is enforced in a helper that six suites bypass, and one of them spawns a generator with the inherited environment

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R7-E5-05`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI, Zink Tools, OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Low
- Status: REFUTED — premise measured dead 2026-10-18, and re-measured by the Lead on 2026-10-18 for a different reason than this row first gave. The route the title named ("one of them spawns a generator with the inherited environment") does not exist: none of the six bypassing suites spawns `configure.mjs` or `verify.mjs`, and the only one that names `configure.mjs` reads it as source text. What survives is a convention-consistency observation about `||=` seeding, which is not an F2 defect. (was: LATENT; the first refutation cited `buildConfig` call counts, a symbol that is not the route — see `Evidence`)
- Root cause: the shared test helper assigns the three router variables, which is the documented way to keep real values out of test lanes, while six suites pre-set the same variables with the nullish-assignment operator, preserving whatever the invoking host exported. The divergence is real but its stated route is not: none of the six runs a generator or calls a router resolver, so on today's tree the bypass keeps a host value inside the test process instead of carrying it into a generated fixture. Reaching the operator's real router endpoint would need a lane that both skips the helper and calls `resolveRouterBaseUrl()`/`resolveRouterApiKey()`; measured, no such lane exists in the six.
- Evidence: Where: `tests/helpers/router-env.mjs:21-36` — plain assignment of all three variables at `:27-29` and the restore closure at `:30-35` · Measured bypass population, six suites: `tests/m12-t2.2-m1-live-reachability.test.mjs:22-24`, `tests/m12-t2.2-a1-stack-authority.test.mjs:14-16`, `tests/m12-t2.2-a2-stack-registry.test.mjs:24-26`, `tests/m12-t2.2-a3-queue-writer-lock.test.mjs:20-22`, `tests/m12-t2.2-k1-announce-discard-reason.test.mjs:17-19` and `tests/m10-t2-r14-review-defects.test.mjs:5-7` · Where the router values are actually read: three **exported** resolvers — `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:1291` (`resolveRouterHost`), `:1298` (`resolveRouterBaseUrl`), `:1308` (`resolveRouterApiKey`) — each of which throws `ROUTER_*_UNAVAILABLE` when unset; `buildConfig` at `:788` only composes them, so counting `buildConfig` calls was the wrong probe · Generator consumers: `configure.mjs:1252` and `:1593` write `stableJson(buildSecretsPlan())`, `verify.mjs:311-312` and `:511` call the resolvers and compare the plan · Measured with `temp/f324-probe.mjs` over all 122 tracked suites: exactly six pre-set with `||=`, twelve import the helper, and of the six **none spawns a generator** — `tests/m12-t2.2-m1-live-reachability.test.mjs` resolves the path at `:36` and reads its source text at `:38`, and its only child processes are `git archive` at `:98` and `tar` at `:104`, whose imported symbol `governancePluginFiles` at `:109-114` reads no `ZINK_ROUTER_*`; `tests/m10-t2-r14-review-defects.test.mjs:89` mentions `validateConfig` only inside a comment and imports `evaluateCapabilityAccess`/`AGENT_SPAWN_POLICY`, neither of which reads a router variable · Measured consequence: that suite prints tests 4, pass 3, fail 0, skipped 1 at baseline AND with a sentinel exported for all three variables, and the sentinel string appears 0 times in its output (`temp/f324-base.log`, `temp/f324-sent.log`).
- Reproduction: the measurement this row said it could not run was run: a sentinel exported for one of the three variables leaves the single suite at the same counts and never reaches the generated config, so the credential-into-fixture route is not reproducible today. The surviving measurement: assert at the head of each bypassing suite that the value it sees equals the documented placeholder, which is exactly what a host-exported router host would violate.
- Acceptance criteria: one policy for all suites — either every lane that can build the deployed config calls the helper that forces placeholders, or the suites that keep the preserving form assert that the values they see are the documented placeholders; no lane may write a real credential into a fixture or print one.
- Related findings: F-264, F-103, F-023 · nguồn: R7-E delta audit E5 — remaining orphan files

### F-342 — `.editorconfig` and `repo:check` prescribe opposite trailing-whitespace rules for Markdown

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R7-OA-05`
- Affected modules: Build & runtime config, Governance docs
- Severity: Low
- Status: REFUTED — premise measured dead at HEAD: the gate already defers to the editor-facing `.editorconfig` — `tests/repository-policy.test.mjs:1147-1158` asserts exactly that, so the two authorities no longer prescribe opposite trailing-whitespace policy. (was: LATENT)
- Root cause: two authorities state opposite requirements for the same file class, and neither names the other. The editor-facing rule exists for Markdown's hard-break semantics; the gate-facing rule rejects the same bytes. Which one wins depends on which surface the operator touches last.
- Evidence: Where: `.editorconfig:18-19` `[*.md]` with `trim_trailing_whitespace = false` · Where: `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:707-712` — `checkWorkingTreeTextPolicy` fails any tracked file whose line ends `/[ \t]+\r?$/` · Where that runs: `npm run repo:check`, a `verify` stage, and the CI `quality-gate` · measured: count of tracked `.md` files whose lines end in whitespace = 0.
- Reproduction: static — add two trailing spaces to a `docs/*.md` line and run `npm run repo:check`; prettier preserves them per `package.json:21`'s markdown glob and `.editorconfig` declares them legal, while the policy check reddens.
- Acceptance criteria: one authority owns trailing-whitespace policy for Markdown and the other defers to it (either the gate exempts `*.md` hard breaks, or the editorconfig rule is removed and the docs are normalized).
- Related findings: F-129 (governance docs describing reality), F-153 · nguồn: R7-O1 delta audit A

### F-392 — the card's 24-hour expiry guard denies APPROVE, REJECT_APPROVAL and REQUEST_REVISION_APPROVAL alike and the only producer of the durable approval verdict sits behind it, so an owner cannot record a refusal against an expired card

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-L01a-1`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: measured at HEAD the guard at `index.mjs:2413-2417` gates all three exit actions of APPROVAL_PENDING and returns DENIED at `:2436-2441` before `WORKFLOW_USER_APPROVAL_DECIDED` is written at `:2486`, so 24h after the surfaced posted-at an owner has no way to close the task on the approval channel
- Root cause: the clock was implemented as "an expired card is not approved" (the intent comment at `:2418-2425`) and applied to the whole action set that leaves APPROVAL_PENDING, so expiry seals the task in APPROVAL_PENDING instead of blocking consent only; it looks like a security control but is a lifecycle dead-end.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:2413-2417` — the branch tests `normAction === ACTIONS.APPROVE || REJECT_APPROVAL || REQUEST_REVISION_APPROVAL` · Where: `index.mjs:2426-2441` — `approvalCardExpiresAt(readSurfacedApprovalPostedAt(paths, taskId))` then `return { allowed: false, reason: "AUTH_APPROVAL_EXPIRED", status: "DENIED" }` (`readSurfacedApprovalPostedAt` is defined at `:305`) · Where: `index.mjs:2486` — the durable `WORKFLOW_USER_APPROVAL_DECIDED` write sits after that return · Where: `tests/agent-owner-F-123.test.mjs:83-92` — the shipped harness `decide()` sends `action: "APPROVE"` only; grep of that file finds no REJECT_APPROVAL or REQUEST_REVISION case · Measured: the expired-clock refusal is reachable for the two refusal actions on the same code path as APPROVE
- Reproduction: read-only walk of the branch plus the shipped harness: reuse `tests/agent-owner-F-123.test.mjs:83-92` with `action: "REJECT_APPROVAL"` and `currentTime` at the surfaced posted-at + 25h and the call returns `allowed: false, reason: "AUTH_APPROVAL_EXPIRED"` with the events journal byte-identical, i.e. no refusal is ever recorded
- Acceptance criteria: past the card window the guard at `index.mjs:2413-2441` refuses APPROVE only, while REJECT_APPROVAL and REQUEST_REVISION_APPROVAL fall through to the durable `WORKFLOW_USER_APPROVAL_DECIDED` at `:2486`; a falsifiable case added to `tests/agent-owner-F-123.test.mjs` (mirroring `:83-92` with the expired clock and a refusal action) turns red when the trio-wide denial is restored
- Related findings: F-398 (the same card clock is the only freshness bound in the system and is plugin-side and non-durable) · no register or CHANGELOG row covers this trio; the digest-binding rows `F-248` and the F-077 branch are a different mechanism · nguồn: R9 L01a — owner-verdict chain, approval card clock

### F-393 — resolveAcceptedOutcome's only live production branch returns COMPLETE because the shipped ACCEPT emitter never names outcome_status, so a recorded FAILED/PARTIAL/BLOCKED root result closes the brief as COMPLETE

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R9-L02-1`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow contracts)
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the plugin's ACCEPT branch at `index.mjs:2515-2531` is the only producer of `WORKFLOW_USER_RESULT_ACCEPTED` and its payload carries `result_digest`, `message_id`, `thread_id` but no `outcome_status`, so every real verdict enters the reducer with `claimed = null` (`workflow.mjs:2171`) and lands on `return "COMPLETE"` (`:2180`)
- Root cause: the outcome derivation was made optional on the contract side and the producer was never changed to fill it, so a dead default silently rewrites the outcome the ledger itself recorded; the ledger then agrees with itself, which is why it reads as a completed run rather than a lost outcome.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:2171` — `const claimed = payload.outcome_status ?? null;` · Where: `workflow.mjs:2180` — `return "COMPLETE";` (measured at HEAD) · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:2515-2531` — the emitted ACCEPT payload, ending `result_digest` / `message_id` / `thread_id` with no outcome member · Where: `workflow.mjs:992-999` — the reducer whitelists `outcome_status` as optional and `:1013-1014` stamps the derived status onto `task.workflow_status` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/contracts/ledger-event.schema.json:769-773` — `outcome_status` declared with `enum ["COMPLETE","PARTIAL","BLOCKED","FAILED", null]` and never required
- Reproduction: in-memory reducer probe at HEAD: replay a root result recorded FAILED, then replay the exact ACCEPT payload the plugin emits (no `outcome_status`) over `RESULT_PENDING_REVIEW`; observed `task.workflow_status === "COMPLETE"` and `brief_state` CLOSED (`workflow.mjs:1013-1018`)
- Acceptance criteria: `resolveAcceptedOutcome` (`workflow.mjs:2171-2180`) cannot return COMPLETE when `claimed` is null and the pending result's recorded outcome is FAILED/PARTIAL/BLOCKED — either the emitter at `index.mjs:2515-2531` names `outcome_status` or the reducer refuses the verdict — pinned by a topology test that replays the emitted ACCEPT over a FAILED root and asserts FAILED, turning red when the `:2180` default is restored
- Related findings: F-394 (the task-level arm never records an outcome for a coordinator's own result, so this default fires even after that arm is fixed) · F-406 (the queue has no terminal state for the non-COMPLETE outcome) · distinct from the duplication row `R1-A11-03` recorded at CHANGELOG:1541 · nguồn: R9 L02 — workflow reducer outcome binding

### F-394 — the task-level WORKFLOW_RESULT_RECORDED arm whitelists technical_status and business_status then writes neither, so a FAIL/FAIL result leaves no outcome anywhere in the durable projection

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R9-L02-2`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the plugin sends both statuses at `index.mjs:3093-3094` on every task-level result, the reducer admits them at `workflow.mjs:1662-1663`, and the arm body at `:1697-1714` writes only `RESULT_PENDING_REVIEW`, `result_pending_at` and one digest, so the outcome the owner is being asked to accept is absent from durable state
- Root cause: a schema whitelist was treated as persistence — the members pass validation and are then dropped, so the projection carries a digest of the result but no outcome, and the verdict path has nothing to bind to; the missing write looks like a clean "pending review" state because the task genuinely advances.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:1656-1669` — the optional-member list of the task-level arm includes `"technical_status"` (`:1662`) and `"business_status"` (`:1663`) · Where: `workflow.mjs:1697-1714` — the arm's only writes are `task.workflow_status = "RESULT_PENDING_REVIEW"` (`:1697`), `task.result_pending_at` (`:1698`) and `task.execution_payload_digest` (`:1713-1714`); no technical/business/outcome assignment anywhere in the arm · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:3093-3094` — `technical_status: gateResult.technical?.status || "PASS"` and `business_status: gateResult.business?.status || "PASS"` · Where: `workflow.mjs:2171-2180` — the verdict later defaults to COMPLETE precisely because nothing was stored
- Reproduction: in-memory reducer probe at HEAD: replay the plugin's own task-level `WORKFLOW_RESULT_RECORDED` payload with `technical_status: "FAIL", business_status: "FAIL"`, then read the projection; measured that neither field survives (`root_result_present = false`) and the derived verdict status is COMPLETE
- Acceptance criteria: the task-level arm at `workflow.mjs:1656-1715` persists an outcome derived from `technical_status`/`business_status` (or refuses a result that carries neither), so a FAIL/FAIL result followed by an ACCEPT yields FAILED; a reducer test asserting the durable outcome after the shipped payload turns red when the write is removed
- Related findings: F-393 (the `:2180` default stamps COMPLETE even once this arm persists, so both points must change) · F-406 (queue vocabulary for the outcome) · nguồn: R9 L02 — workflow reducer outcome binding

### F-395 — the sole-abandoned-spawn lane consumes WORKFLOW_NATIVE_ENDED without reading payload.outcome, so an ok child is journaled FAILED/TRANSIENT_RUNTIME_FAILURE and the retry gate authorises a second execution of work that succeeded

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-L02-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the lane at `workflow.mjs:697-709` returns before the outcome validation at `:713-715`, and the reason code it produces (`:2117`) is exactly the predicate the next-attempt gate reads (`:2451-2454`), so a successful child becomes re-executable work
- Root cause: the un-wedge lane was written to make a report consumable and stopped caring what the report said, so it reconciles any sole abandoned spawn to a terminal failure; the journal looks like an honest runtime failure because the failure reason is the same one real crashes get.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:697-709` — measured: the filter selects `run_state === "SPAWN_UNKNOWN" && !run.native_run_id && parent_session_key` matches (`:697-701`), `if (abandoned.length === 1)` (`:703`) calls `reconcileAbandonedSpawns` (`:704-708`) and `return next` (`:709`) · Where: `workflow.mjs:713-715` — `payload.outcome` is first read only after that return, on the path the lane never takes · Where: `workflow.mjs:2110-2117` — measured `run.runtime_reason_code = "TRANSIENT_RUNTIME_FAILURE"` at `:2117` · Where: `workflow.mjs:2451-2454` — measured `latest.runtime_reason_code === "TRANSIENT_RUNTIME_FAILURE"` (`:2451`) as the predicate admitting the next attempt
- Reproduction: in-memory reducer probe at HEAD on the shipped topology: build a task with exactly one delegation in SPAWN_UNKNOWN with no `native_run_id` and a matching `parent_session_key`, replay `WORKFLOW_NATIVE_ENDED` with `outcome: "ok"`; observed the run reconciled to a FAILED terminal run_state carrying `TRANSIENT_RUNTIME_FAILURE` and the attempt-2 gate evaluating true
- Acceptance criteria: the lane at `workflow.mjs:697-709` branches on `payload.outcome` before reconciling, so `outcome: "ok"` records a successful terminal run_state and leaves the `:2451-2454` predicate false; a reducer test that replays an ok-child ended report and asserts no `TRANSIENT_RUNTIME_FAILURE` and no second attempt turns red when the branch is removed
- Related findings: F-401 (duplicate execution is also what an unrecoverable EXECUTING item invites) · nguồn: R9 L02 — abandoned-spawn reconciliation

### F-396 — WORKFLOW_RESULT_SURFACED admits result_digest and persists nothing, so the shown-to-verdict link is auditable in name only and a differing digest on the same thread is silently accepted

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R9-L02-6`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the plugin builds the surface event with a digest (`index.mjs:3826`, recorded at `:6400`/`:6488`), the reducer admits the member at `workflow.mjs:1339` and then writes only `task.results_thread_id` (`:1345-1350`), so nothing in durable state says which result was surfaced
- Root cause: the surfacing arm kept the thread as the one durable fact and dropped the digest, so re-surfacing a different result in the same thread leaves no trace; it looks like an authorization gap but is an audit gap, because the verdict path binds identity by digest independently.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:1339` — measured `exactKeys(payload, ["task_id", "channel", "thread_id"], ["result_digest"]);` · Where: `workflow.mjs:1345-1350` — measured: the arm compares/refuses a differing `thread_id` (`:1345-1346`) and persists `task.results_thread_id = payload.thread_id` (`:1350`) with no digest write · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:3826` — `buildResultSurfaceEvent({ taskId, threadId, resultDigest })` does supply the digest · Where: `index.mjs:6400`, `index.mjs:6488` — `recordWorkflowEvent(paths, "WORKFLOW_RESULT_SURFACED", surfaceEvent)` · Measured: probe C2 replayed two SURFACED events with different digests on one thread and both were admitted; probe C3 replayed a different thread and it was refused
- Reproduction: in-memory reducer probe at HEAD: replay `WORKFLOW_RESULT_SURFACED` for thread T with digest D1, then again for thread T with digest D2 — the second is accepted and the projection holds only T; repeating with thread T2 is refused, which isolates the gap to the digest member
- Acceptance criteria: the arm at `workflow.mjs:1339-1350` persists the first `result_digest` it is given and refuses a SURFACED whose digest differs from the recorded one, or the optional member is removed from `:1339` so the contract stops advertising it; a test replaying two differing digests on one thread turns red either way
- Related findings: F-397 (the thread fact this arm does persist is never compared by the verdict arms) · F-376 was the verdict-side digest member, not this · nguồn: R9 L02 — results-side surface link

### F-397 — an owner result verdict is bound by digest and attempt but never to the thread the card was surfaced in at either layer, while approvals carry that check twice, so a verdict on a forwarded or stale card in another thread is admitted as the owner's consent

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R9-T01-1`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: measured — an ACCEPT naming the correct digest and attempt but a different thread is admitted by the reducer and the projection reaches COMPLETE; the plugin's result branch (`index.mjs:2504-2563`) contains no thread comparison and the result arms (`workflow.mjs:988-1021`, `:1022-1040`) never read `task.results_thread_id`
- Root cause: thread binding was implemented twice for approvals (plugin F-043 seam and reducer F-032 seam) and zero times for results, while the reducer's `boundDecision` escape hatch (`workflow.mjs:1158`) makes the approval binding conditional on the payload naming a thread at all; the asymmetry reads as "digest binding is enough" because identity and attempt are genuinely checked by `requireResultIdentityMatch`.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:2450-2462` — measured approval check: `recordedApprovalThreadId = authoritativeTask?.approval_thread_id`, `decisionThreadId !== recordedApprovalThreadId` → DENIED · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:1174-1185` — measured reducer approval check comparing `normalizeGovernanceThreadId(payload.thread_id)` with `task.approval_thread_id` · Where: `workflow.mjs:1158` — measured `const boundDecision = Object.hasOwn(payload, "thread_id");` gating that whole block · Where: `index.mjs:2530` and `index.mjs:2552` — the result verdicts do send `thread_id: data.threadId ?? null` · Where: `workflow.mjs:992-999` and `:1023-1027` — `thread_id` is whitelisted on ACCEPT and REJECT, and the arms `:988-1021`/`:1022-1040` never compare it to `task.results_thread_id`, which is the field `:1350` persists and `index.mjs:6400`/`:6488` record · Where: `workflow.mjs:2239`/`:2265` — the digest binding that is enforced, showing identity is covered and thread is not
- Reproduction: in-memory reducer probe at HEAD: build a task whose `results_thread_id` is T1 with a pending result, replay `WORKFLOW_USER_RESULT_ACCEPTED` with the matching `result_digest` and `thread_id: "T2"`; observed the verdict admitted and `task.workflow_status` set to the derived COMPLETE, and the same shape replayed against the approval arms is refused
- Acceptance criteria: the reducer's result-verdict arms (`workflow.mjs:988-1040`) normalize `payload.thread_id` and require it to equal `task.results_thread_id`, refusing an absent or differing thread, and the plugin's result branch gains the same check beside `index.mjs:2450-2462`; a test mirroring `tests/agent-owner-F-123.test.mjs` with a wrong-thread ACCEPT turns red when either control is removed
- Related findings: carries the merged candidate `R9-L02-5` (the reducer-only framing of the same missing binding) · F-396 (the arm that persists the thread this verdict should bind to) · F-393 (the wrong outcome this verdict then stamps) · not the digest-wedge row `F-248` · nguồn: R9 T01 — owner-verdict integrity

### F-398 — no durable freshness bound exists for owner result verdicts, so an ACCEPT is admissible indefinitely after the card was surfaced, while the only clock the system has is the plugin's approval branch and a non-durable component TTL

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-T01-2`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), Zink Tools (workflow contracts), OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: measured at HEAD that the reducer's result-verdict arms (`workflow.mjs:988-1021`, `:1022-1040`) and the approval-decided arm (`:1105-1253`) contain no time predicate, and the ACCEPTED contract arm (`ledger-event.schema.json:741-796`) declares no posted-at/expiry member, so a verdict replayed a year after surfacing passes every layer
- Root cause: freshness was implemented once, in the plugin's approval branch, and read from the surfaced card's posted-at (`index.mjs:2426-2428`), which has no counterpart on the result path; the surviving defect is the absence of a durable bound in the reducer and the contract, not the absence of any TTL.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:988-1021` and `:1022-1040` — measured arms with actor/channel/decision/digest conditions only, no clock · Where: `workflow.mjs:1152-1186` — measured approval-decided arm: its only extra conditions are `approval_requested_at` and the thread comparison · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/contracts/ledger-event.schema.json:741-796` — measured ACCEPTED payload properties: `task_id`, `actor`, `channel`, `decision`, `outcome_status`, `result_digest`, `result_id`, `message_id`, `thread_id`; no freshness member and `additionalProperties: false` at `:753` forecloses one · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/discord-native-send.mjs:81` — measured `export const DISCORD_COMPONENT_TTL_MS = 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;`, passed as `ttlMs` at `:446` inside `registerNativeComponents` (`:425`): this is the refuted half — a TTL does exist, but it bounds interactive component registration for button clicks, is held in process memory, and never covers the in-thread text verdict path · Where: `workflow.mjs:2239`/`:2265` — the stale-card defences that do exist bind identity and attempt, not wall clock
- Reproduction: read-only walk at HEAD: grep of the result-verdict arms, the approval-decided arm and the ACCEPTED contract arm finds no time or posted-at member, while the approval branch's clock is read from `readSurfacedApprovalPostedAt` (`index.mjs:305`, used at `:2426-2428`) and has no result-side counterpart; the text path is reachable without any component registration, so the `:446` TTL cannot cover it
- Acceptance criteria: either the reducer enforces a bounded freshness on result verdicts — a posted-at/expiry member declared on the ACCEPTED payload at `ledger-event.schema.json:741-796` and compared in `workflow.mjs:988-1021` — or the contract documents why results cards are immortal; a test that replays an ACCEPT past the window and expects refusal turns red when the comparison is removed
- Related findings: F-392 (the one existing card clock, approval-only and applied too broadly) · F-397 (the other dimension the result verdicts do not bind) · the "never expire at any layer" half of the candidate is refuted by the TTL at `discord-native-send.mjs:81` · nguồn: R9 T01 — owner-verdict integrity

### F-399 — the delivery seam abandons an in-flight send that later yields a messageId, so component registration is structurally impossible and no SURFACED event is written even though the card may be sitting in the thread

- Category: F6 — Reliability & Performance
- Legacy register id: `R9-L05-6`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, deployment topology
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — needs fault injection: measured with a stub adapter resolving at 120ms against `deadlineMs` 50 — the publish path returns `{ status: "BLOCKED", error: "ANNOUNCE_DELIVERY_FAILED" }` (`index.mjs:7156-7162`) and writes no `WORKFLOW_APPROVAL_SURFACED` (`:7168-7173`), while the abandoned promise still holds a messageId no registration site can reach
- Root cause: the seam bounds its own wait because the host adapter exposes no abort contract, so a send that settles after the deadline is deliberately ignored (its rejection absorbed) — correct for the wait, wrong for the consequence: the caller treats the abandonment as "nothing was delivered" and registration happens only after the awaited send. Unproven half, stated explicitly: that Discord actually delivered that late card is not observable from this repository, because settlement happens inside the external host adapter.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/discord-native-send.mjs:579-611` — measured `sendWithinDeadline`: the docblock at `:582-586` states "a send that settles later is ignored, and its late rejection is absorbed", `:590-593` detaches the promise and swallows its rejection, `:595-607` is the `Promise.race` that rejects with `NATIVE_DELIVERY_TIMEOUT` · Where: `discord-native-send.mjs:790-814` — measured: the awaited send at `:795` and `registerNativeComponents({...})` at `:807`, i.e. registration only ever runs on a send that settled inside the deadline · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:7156-7162` — the delivery catch logs and returns BLOCKED with the comment "nothing is recorded as SURFACED" · Where: `index.mjs:7168-7173` — `WORKFLOW_APPROVAL_SURFACED` recorded only after that catch, with the receipt's `messageId` consumed at `:7153-7155` · Measured: the late-settling stub leaves the journal with no SURFACED record, so the task stays re-drivable and the next event posts a second card
- Reproduction: fault injection outside the repo: stub the host adapter's `sendPayload` to resolve with a plausible messageId after 120ms and drive the publish path with `deadlineMs: 50`; observed the timeout error at `:600`, the BLOCKED return, and zero SURFACED events for the task; the delivery itself was never observed, only the messageId the abandoned promise received
- Acceptance criteria: a late-settling send is not discarded without consequence — either `sendWithinDeadline`'s caller hands the eventual messageId to `registerNativeComponents` (`discord-native-send.mjs:807`) before returning, or the seam records a durable deferred-registration/idempotency key so the next surfacing reuses that messageId instead of posting a second card, keeping `index.mjs:7168-7173` honest; a test with a late-settling stub adapter asserts either registration or the durable deferred record and turns red when the ignore-late-settlement branch is restored
- Related findings: F-397 (the recorded surface thread is what a verdict should later bind to) · an earlier closure on this seam fixed the unbounded wait, not the late-settlement consequence · nguồn: R9 L05 — Discord delivery and surfacing seam

### F-400 — the in-thread text owner verdict requires ctx.channelId to equal the forum id while the approval-thread binding requires the decision thread, derived from that same channelId as a fallback, to equal the recorded thread, so both guards can pass only if the host mapper supplies a distinct event.threadId

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R9-L01b-1`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, deployment topology, Zink Tools (workflow contracts)
- Severity: Medium

- Root cause: one host field feeds two id spaces: the surface guard compares it to a forum id and the thread id falls back to it, so a message observed in a forum thread is either refused as off-surface or recorded with the wrong thread; until the host contract is stated, it is unknown which guard is wrong rather than that both are broken.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:10511-10512` — measured `const observedChannelId = String(ctx.channelId ?? ...` · Where: `index.mjs:10532-10534` — measured the guard `observedChannelId.length === 0 || observedChannelId !== expectedSurfaceId`, logging `[zink-governance] text action blocked: APPROVAL_SURFACE_MISMATCH` at `:10539`, where the expected id comes from `resolveDiscordDestination` · Where: `index.mjs:10449` — measured `const threadId = event.threadId || ctx.conversationId || ctx.channelId;` · Where: `index.mjs:2450-2462` — the F-043 approval binding that requires the decision thread to equal `approval_thread_id`, and `index.mjs:7141-7142` with `index.mjs:7169-7173` — measured: the approval card is sent into `thread.id` and `WORKFLOW_APPROVAL_SURFACED` records `thread_id: thread.id`, so the recorded approval thread is a thread snowflake, not the forum id · Where: `index.mjs:10075` — `event.threadId ||` on the sibling inbound path, showing the field is optional here too · Where: `tests/m12-t2-governed-surface.test.mjs:1433-1441` and `:1453-1461` — measured: the only shipped guard test feeds `{ content, messageId, senderId, channelId }` in the event and `{ channelId }` in ctx, with no `threadId` in either case, so the positive case passes only in the flat-channel shape where one id serves both guards · Note: the code comment's "M12/T9 F-244" has no row in `findings.md` or `CHANGELOG.md` (grep at HEAD finds no `F-244`), so this is not a re-report
- Reproduction: cannot be fired inside this repository: the read-only walk shows joint satisfiability depends on a host field this repo does not produce, and no in-repo fixture emits `event.threadId` for a forum message; a forum-thread-shaped message (`channelId` = the thread snowflake) is measurably refused at `:10532-10534` before `handleDiscordAction` runs, and the flat-shaped message that passes records whatever `:10449` falls back to
- Acceptance criteria: the host contract is stated and then pinned: the text surface accepts the forum id OR a thread whose recorded parent is the forum while keeping the `:2450-2462` thread binding, and a shipped test in `tests/m12-t2-governed-surface.test.mjs` feeds the forum-thread shape (`event.threadId` set, `channelId` = parent forum) and asserts the verdict is recorded — that test turns red while `:10532-10534` and `:10449` remain in tension; blocked until OpenClaw's owner states the mapper's behaviour
- Related findings: F-397 (the same verdict-integrity chain on the component path) · F-392 (the approval branch this text path would reach) · nguồn: R9 L01b — inbound text verdict surface

### F-401 — detectAndRecoverStaleTasks, the only EXECUTING to QUEUED recovery in the queue, has zero production call sites, so any item left EXECUTING by a crash or a failed recordClaimed is unrecoverable forever

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-L04-1`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (ledger), deployment topology
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: grep at HEAD finds the definition at `queue-manager.mjs:1132` plus only test callers (`tests/m10-t1-coordinator-pickup.test.mjs:393`,`:531`; `tests/m10-t2-r2-coordinator-execution-authority.test.mjs:177`; `tests/m7-lane-c-queue.test.mjs:258`,`:499`,`:529`; `tests/m8-t2-e2e-workflow.test.mjs:291`; `tests/m8-t2-governed-runtime-audit.test.mjs:272`,`:1258`) and one doc comment (`:688`) — zero under `integrations/`, while the production loop at `coordinator-pickup.mjs:772-773` calls only `getLedgerTasks()` and `runCoordinatorPickup({...})`
- Root cause: the recovery was built as a library function whose callers were tests, and the runtime driver that design comments call "the only sanctioned path back" was never wired, so every re-entry path refuses an EXECUTING item and the queue is left permanently stuck; it looks like a lease problem because the fence error names a lease.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:1132` — the `detectAndRecoverStaleTasks({...})` definition · Where: `queue-manager.mjs:1169-1177` — measured `item.status = TASK_STATES.QUEUED` (`:1169`), `item.leaseExpiresAt = null` (`:1173`) and the requeued record (`:1177`) — the only code that clears a dead owner's lease · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/coordinator-pickup.mjs:772-773` — the production tick body · Where: `queue-manager.mjs:529` — `claimTask` considers only `item.status === TASK_STATES.QUEUED`; `:612-614` — `claimTaskById` throws on a non-QUEUED item; `:756-761` — after expiry `withExecutionFence` throws `EXECUTION_FENCE_EXPIRED` forever · Where: `CHANGELOG.md:1484` — records `R1-A06-01` "the queue lease lifecycle has no runtime driver — a crashed claim never returns — closed by `dd7f6ac`", and `git show --stat dd7f6ac` at HEAD lists only `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs`, `tests/m7-lane-c-queue.test.mjs` and `tests/m8-t2.10-r13-r7-predecessor-allowlist.test.mjs` — the implementation landed, the driver did not, so that closure is incomplete · sites added by R10 (V02, from R10-T02-3): Where: `index.mjs:9707-9725` — the `subagent_ended` coordinator branch logs and returns `COORDINATOR_RUN_ENDED` writing nothing ("No result is recorded here … state advances only through the existing lease and stale-recovery semantics"), so the run-end seam records nothing even by design · Where: `coordinator-pickup.mjs:304-306` — an in-repo comment promises this row's unwired recovery as the only sanctioned path back · Where: `README.md:794` and `README.md:928` — operator prose describes a sweep that does not run · Measured: the root cause is unchanged, these are corroborating sites, not a new mechanism
- Reproduction: read-only measurement: grep `-n detectAndRecoverStaleTasks` over the tracked tree returns the definition plus test call sites only, then the chain read at `:529`, `:612-614` and `:756-761` shows every later tick can only report a stale projection for an EXECUTING item whose lease has expired
- Acceptance criteria: a production path — the pickup tick at `coordinator-pickup.mjs:772-773` or a wired scheduler in the same lane — invokes `detectAndRecoverStaleTasks` so a dead-owner EXECUTING item returns to QUEUED with `leaseExpiresAt` cleared and `attempt` incremented within one interval; a test that runs the production tick and asserts recovery turns red when the call is removed
- Related findings: F-407 (the expired-lease acceptance that this detector is the only thing able to revoke) · F-405 (the other half of "the owner can never close the queue at the end") · incomplete closure of `R1-A06-01` (`CHANGELOG.md:1484`, commit `dd7f6ac`) · the lane framing "nothing executes after APPROVE" was NOT confirmed — enqueue and pickup are wired, only recovery is missing · nguồn: R9 L04 — execution lane queue wiring

### F-402 — the only production enqueue omits ownerPrincipalRef so completeTask refuses every owner ACCEPT with OWNER_PRINCIPAL_REQUIRED, and the ACCEPT paths present a raw snowflake where the stored ref is a minted 64-hex value, so the refusal merely moves to USER_PRINCIPAL_MISMATCH

- Category: F1 — Logic & Functional Bugs
- Legacy register id: `R9-L04-2`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (ledger)
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the enqueue at `index.mjs:7254-7260` supplies `taskId`, `planVersion`, `primaryCoordinator`, `requiredCapabilities`, `payload` and nothing else, `queue-manager.mjs:472-476` therefore stores a null owner, and every ACCEPT reaches `completeTask` at `index.mjs:7441-7446` which throws at `:1040-1046`
- Root cause: the owner principal is minted on the ledger side (`index.mjs:9364`, tested as 64-hex at `:835`) but never projected into the queue at the production seam, and the verdict side presents a different identity vocabulary entirely (a Discord snowflake), so one guard chain has two independent breaks; the second only becomes visible once the first is fixed.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:7254-7260` — measured `defaultQueueManager.enqueueTask({ taskId, planVersion, primaryCoordinator, requiredCapabilities, payload })` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:448` — `ownerPrincipalRef = null` default, `:472-476` — the stored value · Where: `queue-manager.mjs:1040-1046` — measured the throw `OWNER_PRINCIPAL_REQUIRED: Completion requires an authoritative task owner` · Where: `queue-manager.mjs:1048-1051` — measured `if (userPrincipalRef !== item.ownerPrincipalRef)` → `USER_PRINCIPAL_MISMATCH: Completion principal is not the authoritative task owner` · Where: `index.mjs:10389` — measured `userPrincipalRef: senderSnowflake,` on the button ACCEPT and `index.mjs:10596` — `userPrincipalRef: senderId,` on the text ACCEPT · Where: `index.mjs:9364` — `origin_principal_ref: derivePrincipalRef(...)`, the minted form, constrained by `/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/u` at `index.mjs:835`, produced by `mintUserPrincipalRef` (`:5609`, called only at `:2648`) · Where: `queue-manager.mjs:1285-1286` and `:1332` — the only writer of a non-null `ownerPrincipalRef`, inside `rebuildFromLedger`, reached only from `index.mjs:3106` · Measured: grep of `ownerPrincipalRef` across the tracked tree finds the named argument at `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:448`/`:472`/`:1285-1286`/`:1332`, in a receipt builder's own parameter list at `index.mjs:4009`-`:4046`, and in tests only otherwise (`tests/m7-lane-c-queue.test.mjs:37`, `tests/m7-lane-h2-queue-persistence-matrix.test.mjs:92`/`:180`/`:210`/`:260`/`:420`, `tests/m8-t2-e2e-workflow.test.mjs:94`, `tests/m10-t1-coordinator-pickup.test.mjs:332`, `tests/agent-plugin-F-243.test.mjs:60`) — no production call site of `enqueueTask` passes it; the guard's own comment at `queue-manager.mjs:970` records the F-026 history
- Reproduction: read-only seam walk plus the shipped tests: with the production enqueue, `completeTask` always throws `OWNER_PRINCIPAL_REQUIRED` because `:472-476` stored null; after `rebuildFromLedger` populates the minted ref the same call throws `USER_PRINCIPAL_MISMATCH` at `:1048-1051` because the presented value is a snowflake; no test drives the plugin ACCEPT end-to-end, they call `qm.completeTask` directly with a pre-supplied hex ref, which is why the seam stayed broken
- Acceptance criteria: one function mints the principal at both seams — the enqueue at `index.mjs:7254-7260` passes the ledger-projected `origin_principal_ref` and the ACCEPT presenters at `:10389`/`:10596` pass `mintUserPrincipalRef`'s output — so a plugin-driven approve → finish → ACCEPT reaches COMPLETE with no `rebuildFromLedger`; a test driving the plugin's own ACCEPT (not `qm.completeTask`) turns red if either guard at `queue-manager.mjs:1040-1051` is bypassed rather than satisfied
- Related findings: carries the merged candidate `R9-T02-1` (the verdict half of the same guard chain: it fails at `:1044` and then at `:1049`, so one guard chain closes both) · F-405 (today's throw is swallowed by that catch) · F-406 (blocked behind this row) · nguồn: R9 L04 — execution lane owner principal

### F-403 — a STALE or UNKNOWN quota reading takes the keep-primary branch yet still writes the latch unconditionally, erasing the fallback hold the hysteresis latch exists to keep

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R9-L04-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), Zink Tools (SQL/n8n/Python guards)
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `QUOTA_FRESHNESS_MS` is 60000 (`quota-telemetry.mjs:31`, applied at `:145`) so a self-scoped read older than a minute is the common case, and the sole non-test caller runs the resolver on every guarded decision (`topology/lib/guard.mjs:1042`)
- Root cause: the resolution branch chooses primary for a stale or unknown reading — a defensible decision — and then the shared tail at `:259-260` recomputes and stores the latch from that choice regardless of reason, so the absence of evidence silently overwrites the only mechanism that holds a fallback; it looks like the fallback recovered because the returned model really is primary.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/quota-telemetry.mjs:229-234` — measured `if (telemetry.status === QUOTA_STATUS.STALE) { chosen = primary; reason = RESOLUTION_REASON.STALE_KEEP_PRIMARY; } else if (UNKNOWN) { chosen = primary; ... }` · Where: `quota-telemetry.mjs:245-252` — the `latch === "fallback"` hold (`HYSTERESIS_HOLD_FALLBACK`) that is the only retention mechanism · Where: `quota-telemetry.mjs:259-260` — measured `const nextLatch = fallback && chosen === fallback ? "fallback" : "primary"; store.latches.set(agentId, nextLatch);` with no reason guard · Where: `quota-telemetry.mjs:31`, `:145`, `:142`, `:148` — the freshness window and the UNKNOWN/STALE classifications · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/guard.mjs:1042` — measured `return quotaTelemetry.resolveRuntimeModel({ store: quota, agentId, now });` (capability check at `:315`), the only production caller · Note: the row near `docs/execution/findings.md:411` is a different premise and rates the latch as working
- Reproduction: measured probe on the shipped resolver: record a 15% snapshot, resolve once → `chosen = fallback` and `store.latches` holds `"fallback"`; resolve again with identical durable state and only `now` advanced past `QUOTA_FRESHNESS_MS` → the STALE branch returns primary and `store.latches` is observed rewritten to `"primary"`, i.e. same data, different clock, latch erased
- Acceptance criteria: `resolveRuntimeModel` leaves `store.latches` untouched when the reason is `STALE_KEEP_PRIMARY` or `UNKNOWN_KEEP_PRIMARY` (the write at `quota-telemetry.mjs:259-260` becomes conditional on a fresh reading); a test that resolves twice across the 60s boundary and asserts the latch is still `"fallback"` turns red when the unconditional write is restored
- Related findings: F-404 (same module family, unrelated mechanism) · nguồn: R9 L04 — quota telemetry latch

### F-404 — the decision-ledger derived id is key-order independent while replay equality is a raw JSON.stringify of context and tags, so an identical decision replayed with drifted key order throws DECISION_ID_CONFLICT against the documented idempotency

- Category: F1 — Logic & Functional Bugs
- Legacy register id: `R9-L04-4`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (ledger)
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: the named blocker is the caller surface — `recordDecision(` has exactly one production call site (`index.mjs:1661`), whose record is built from one fixed literal shape, so no second writer with a different key insertion order exists at HEAD; the conflict path at `decision-ledger.mjs:621` cannot be reached from production code today
- Root cause: the id uses an order-independent canonical key while the replay comparison uses `JSON.stringify`, so two representations of one decision collide on identity and differ on bytes; it looks like a genuine conflicting decision because the thrown code is the same one a real conflict produces.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/decision-ledger.mjs:216-227` — measured `function canonicalRecordBytes(record) { return JSON.stringify({` ... — insertion-order sensitive · Where: `decision-ledger.mjs:618` — measured `if (canonicalRecordBytes(existing) === canonicalRecordBytes(record))` as the idempotency test, with `failLedger("DECISION_ID_CONFLICT")` at `:615` and `:621` · Where: `decision-ledger.mjs:581` and `:156` — the derived id `dec-${canonicalDecisionKey({...})}` from the order-independent `canonicalDecisionKey` · Where: `decision-ledger.mjs:540` — the contract comment: replaying the same decision "twice is idempotent" · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:1661` — the sole production writer · Note: the repo already ships an order-stable serializer, `export function stableJson(value)` at `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:2050` (measured) · site added by R10 (V03, from R10-T03-2): Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/schema.mjs:368` — the interpreter's `uniqueItems` handling shares the raw `JSON.stringify` key-order mechanism this row blames at the decision ledger, and this row's own fix at the ledger does not cover it
- Reproduction: static repro at HEAD: record a decision whose context is `{a:1,b:2}`, then replay the same decision with `{b:2,a:1}` — both hash to the same `dec-` id at `:581`, the byte comparison at `:618` fails, and `:621` throws `DECISION_ID_CONFLICT`. No production caller can produce the second shape today, which is why the row is LATENT rather than OPEN.
- Acceptance criteria: replay comparison at `decision-ledger.mjs:216-227` is canonical (the existing `stableJson` from `lib/policy.mjs` plus sorted context/tag keys) so an order-drifted replay returns the existing record, while a genuinely different decision for the same id still throws `DECISION_ID_CONFLICT` at `:615`/`:621`; a test replaying reversed key order asserts the returned record and turns red when the raw `JSON.stringify` is restored
- Related findings: M7-034 and M7-036 describe the conflict rule, not serialization order · F-403 (same guard family, different module) · nguồn: R9 L04 — decision ledger idempotency

### F-405 — the durable ledger ACCEPT is followed by a queue advance that is only a caught log line, so the ledger reaches COMPLETE while the queue stays RESULT_PENDING_REVIEW and the ledger then refuses every later ACCEPT with INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-T02-2`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow topology), Zink Tools (ledger)
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: today `completeTask` throws for every owner ACCEPT (F-402), and the catch at `index.mjs:7448-7455` swallows exactly that throw after the ledger ACCEPT is already durable, so the split state is the live end-state of the ACCEPT path rather than a hypothetical
- Root cause: the verdict and the queue projection are two independent commits with no shared boundary, and the only reconciler in the plugin sits inside an unrelated error branch, so a queue-side failure is recorded as a log line and the split is permanent; it looks like a queue bug because the visible symptom is an item stuck in review.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:1013-1014` — measured: the durable ACCEPT derives and stamps the post-verdict status before any queue write · Where: `index.mjs:7441-7446` — `defaultQueueManager.completeTask({ taskId, userConfirmationRef, userPrincipalRef, resultDigest })` inside a try · Where: `index.mjs:7448-7455` — measured catch: `api.logger?.error?.("[zink-governance] completeTask FAILED (queue not advanced): ...")` and `queueCompletionRecorded` stays false · Where: `index.mjs:7470` — measured the success-notice gate `if (officeChannelId && resultsForumId && queueCompletionRecorded)` · Where: `index.mjs:3106` — measured the only production `queueManager.rebuildFromLedger(readSnapshot(paths))`, sitting on the RESULT_RECORDED error branch · Where: `queue-manager.mjs:1219` — the active-set predicate that keeps a RESULT_PENDING_REVIEW item live · Where: `workflow.mjs:1004-1007` — measured the retry path's `requireCondition(task.workflow_status === "RESULT_PENDING_REVIEW", "INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION")`, which is now unsatisfiable · site added by R10 (V01): Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:7420-7432` — the detached `executeApprovedTask` promise whose only failure trace is one error log, i.e. the APPROVE-side dispatch leg of this same verdict/queue-advance split; `rebuildFromLedger` (`queue-manager.mjs:1227`) still has exactly one production caller at `index.mjs:3106`, so this row's acceptance already names the reconciler whose reachability the new site widens
- Reproduction: measured at HEAD: drive the ACCEPT with a principal the queue does not recognise as the owner so `completeTask` throws at `queue-manager.mjs:1040-1046`; observed the journal holding the durable ACCEPTED verdict with the projection COMPLETE while the queue item remains RESULT_PENDING_REVIEW, and a second ACCEPT failing with `INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION` at `workflow.mjs:1004-1007`
- Acceptance criteria: the ledger verdict and the queue advance either commit together or reconcile on the same path — the catch at `index.mjs:7448-7455` must not leave a success-shaped ledger state with a lagged queue, and `rebuildFromLedger` must be reachable from the ACCEPT path instead of only `index.mjs:3106`; a test that forces `completeTask` to fail and asserts the pair is consistent (or repaired) turns red when the reconcile is removed
- Related findings: F-402 (the throw this catch swallows today) · F-401 (the other half of "the owner can never close the queue") · F-406 (the terminal state this advance would have written) · nguồn: R9 T02 — execution lane after APPROVE

### F-406 — completeTask writes only TASK_STATES.COMPLETE, so the queue has no terminal state for an accepted FAILED, PARTIAL or BLOCKED outcome that the reducer already derives and the queue vocabulary already names

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R9-T02-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (ledger), Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: Medium
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: the named blocker is `OWNER_PRINCIPAL_REQUIRED` at `queue-manager.mjs:1040-1046`, i.e. row F-402's defect — no ACCEPT can reach the completion write at all today, so the missing vocabulary cannot yet produce a wrong terminal state; it becomes live the moment F-402 is fixed and must be shipped in the same change
- Root cause: the outcome derivation was added ledger-side (F-226) without a matching queue-side parameter, so completion is a constant assertion rather than a projection of the accepted outcome; the queue looks authoritative because COMPLETE is a real terminal state it did write.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:1054-1067` — measured the only writes on the completion path: `item.status = TASK_STATES.COMPLETE` (`:1055`), the journal record (`:1061`) and `return { success: true, status: TASK_STATES.COMPLETE }` (`:1067`) · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:1013-1018` — measured the reducer deriving `acceptedOutcome` (which can be COMPLETE/PARTIAL/FAILED/BLOCKED) and keeping BLOCKED recoverable · Where: `queue-manager.mjs:101` — measured `const QUEUE_TERMINAL_STATES = Object.freeze([` ... already carrying the non-COMPLETE names, and `mapLedgerStatusToQueue` at `:118` passing terminal states through at `:134` — so the projection vocabulary exists but nothing feeds it
- Reproduction: static walk at HEAD, no fault injection possible: `completeTask` (`queue-manager.mjs:982`) accepts `userConfirmationRef`, `userPrincipalRef` and `resultDigest` and no outcome, and no branch maps a non-COMPLETE ledger status, so the first ACCEPT of a PARTIAL root result after F-402 is fixed writes COMPLETE while the ledger holds PARTIAL; the guard at `:1040-1046` throws first today, which is the blocker that makes this LATENT
- Acceptance criteria: `completeTask` carries the accepted outcome into the queue write, validated against `QUEUE_TERMINAL_STATES` (`queue-manager.mjs:101-107`) and projected through `mapLedgerStatusToQueue` (`:118-134`), or refuses to assert COMPLETE when the ledger outcome is not COMPLETE; a test that drives approve → finish → ACCEPT of a PARTIAL result and asserts the queue terminal state is PARTIAL turns red when the unconditional write at `:1055` is restored, and it must land together with the F-402 fix since nothing reaches this path today
- Related findings: F-402 (named blocker) · F-393 and F-394 (the outcome that would be projected) · F-405 (the advance this write belongs to) · F-226 covered the ledger side only · nguồn: R9 T02 — execution lane after APPROVE

### F-407 — lease expiry is enforced only inside withExecutionFence, so finishExecution and the claimTaskById reclaim path accept and even extend an expired lease

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R9-T02-4`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (ledger), deployment topology
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: both permissive mutators are wired in production — `queueManager.finishExecution({...})` at `index.mjs:3037` and `queueManager.claimTaskById({...})` at `coordinator-pickup.mjs:268` — and neither consults `leaseExpiresAt`, so a holder whose lease has lapsed can still finish and heartbeat
- Root cause: execution authority was given one check site (`withExecutionFence`) while the other three mutators validate identity fields (status, claimedBy, leaseId, attempt) that all still match after expiry, so an expired owner keeps a valid-looking lease string; the heartbeat even rewrites `leaseExpiresAt` forward, making the lapse invisible. It looks like a reliability issue because the symptom is a stale projection, but what is being bypassed is the authority boundary.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:755-763` — measured the only expiry predicate: `Date.parse(item.leaseExpiresAt) <= Date.now()` (`:758`) throwing `EXECUTION_FENCE_EXPIRED` (`:761`) · Where: `queue-manager.mjs:894-916` — measured `finishExecution`'s guards: status (`:896`), `claimedBy !== agentId` (`:902`), `item.leaseId !== leaseId` (`:907`), `item.attempt !== attempt` (`:912`) and no `leaseExpiresAt` test · Where: `queue-manager.mjs:849-872` — measured `_heartbeatInner` and its lease rewrite `item.leaseExpiresAt = new Date(now + leaseSeconds * 1000).toISOString()` (`:871`) · Where: `queue-manager.mjs:596-605` — measured `claimTaskById` (`:579`) delegating to `this._heartbeatInner({...})` (`:600`) for an item already EXECUTING · Where: `queue-manager.mjs:1169-1177` — the only code that clears a lease (`item.leaseExpiresAt = null` at `:1173`) lives in `detectAndRecoverStaleTasks`, which has no production caller (F-401) · Where: `index.mjs:3037`, `coordinator-pickup.mjs:268` — the production wirings · Note: the earlier fence-side fix renewed the lease inside the fence only (`queue-manager.mjs:798-816`), not this asymmetry, and the public `heartbeat()`/`claimTask` wrappers have no production caller
- Reproduction: measured read-only walk at HEAD over the four lease mutators: grep of every `leaseExpiresAt` read shows a single expiry comparison (`:756-758`), and the finish/heartbeat paths reach their writes guarded only by matching `status`/`claimedBy`/`leaseId`/`attempt`; reproducing it live needs a crashed claim plus an expired lease, which F-401's unwired recovery leaves in place permanently
- Acceptance criteria: one shared expiry predicate governs all four mutators — `withExecutionFence` (`:755-763`), `finishExecution` (`:894-916`), `_heartbeatInner` (`:849-872`) and the `claimTaskById` reclaim (`:596-605`) — each refusing an expired lease instead of extending it, together with the wired recovery from F-401 that revokes the leaseId; a test that ages a lease past expiry and calls `finishExecution` and `claimTaskById` asserts refusal and turns red when the fence-only check is restored
- Related findings: F-401 (the unwired detector is the only revocation path) · F-405 (the queue-side split this authority allows a dead holder to write) · nguồn: R9 T02 — execution lane after APPROVE

### F-409 — persistTransactionSnapshot writes entry.body instead of snapshotBytes(entry), so a base64-captured preimage throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE mid-loop and leaves a record-less transaction directory

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R9-L03-2`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), deployment topology
- Severity: Medium
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: every target in HEAD's snapshot set is UTF-8 text (`transaction-snapshot.mjs:78-93` plus the native-patch runtime list) and the only non-UTF-8 instance in the repository is the test fixture at `tests/m7-t4-orchestration.test.mjs:815-818`, so no live capture ever produces an entry that carries `body_base64` and no `body`
- Root cause: capture stores a lossy body as `body_base64` and omits `body` entirely (`:282`), while persistence passes `entry.body` straight to the exclusive writer (`:761`) even though the correct accessor `snapshotBytes(entry)` exists two hundred lines earlier (`:310-315`) and is used elsewhere; the writer opens with "wx" and never unlinks (`:638-648`), so the throw mid-loop leaves a directory with preimages but no record — the failure looks like a corrupt transaction tree rather than like an encoding choice, and the round-3 verdict kept the mechanism while flagging reachability (round-2 FLAG verified)
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:761` — `writeExclusiveFile(target, entry.body, 0o600);` (re-opened at HEAD) · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:310-315` — `snapshotBytes` returns `Buffer.from(entry.body_base64, "base64")` when body_base64 is a string · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:278-283` — `...(lossless ? { body: text } : { body_base64: body.toString("base64") })` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:78-93` — the frozen state target list (openclaw.json, plugin sources, auth plans, resolver) is all text · Where: `tests/m7-t4-orchestration.test.mjs:815-818` — `Buffer.from([0xff, 0xfe, 0x00, 0x41, 0x80, 0x42])` written as `openclaw.json`, the only binary preimage instance · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:638-648` — `writeExclusiveFile` has no unlink-on-failure path
- Reproduction: LATENT: the mechanism was exercised in isolation — calling the persistence loop with one entry carrying only `body_base64` makes `writeExclusiveFile` receive `undefined` and throw ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE from `writeFileSync`, leaving the PREIMAGE_DIR created at `:760` without the record written later at `:786-789`; what is missing at HEAD is a snapshot target whose bytes are not valid UTF-8, and the audit enumerated the whole target set (`:78-93`, native-patch list) to show none qualifies outside the test fixture
- Acceptance criteria: persistence writes `snapshotBytes(entry)` rather than `entry.body` for every preimage, so a base64-captured file round-trips byte-exactly through persist → load → restore and its recorded sha256 still matches, and the loop cleans up a partially written transaction directory on failure; the test that turns red when the control is removed is a non-UTF-8 snapshot target (fixture bytes of `tests/m7-t4-orchestration.test.mjs:815-818` shape) persisted and restored through `transaction-snapshot.mjs`
- Related findings: shares the un-unlinked exclusive writer with `R9-T03-3` (row F-416) · carried candidate `R9-L03-2` reachability flag resolved as LATENT by the round-3 verdict · nguồn: R9 L03-2 — transaction preimage capture and persistence

### F-411 — the retirement write is never anchored by a parent-directory fsync and the helper that would provide one swallows POSIX EIO

- Category: F6 — Reliability & Performance
- Legacy register id: `R9-L06-10`
- Affected modules: Zink Skill Store
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: the loss window is power loss or a hard crash immediately after the retirement rename returns, which no host in this repository's test or deployment path can be made to reproduce; it also needs a POSIX host, since the helper's swallowed errors are the directory-open class
- Root cause: promotion calls `fsyncDirectoryBestEffort(parentDir)` after its rename (`catalog.mjs:1692`, and `:1673` for staging) while the retirement write path performs only `writeFileAtomic` (`:1885-1888`) and never anchors the directory entry, so an acknowledged retirement can be lost on crash even though the bytes survived; the helper itself swallows every failure including POSIX EIO (`:1163-1173`), so even where it is called it cannot report a lost anchor — the candidate was graded F3 by round 1 and the round-3 verdict moved it to F6 because the defect is a durability guarantee, not a state transition
- Evidence: Where: `zink-skill-store/src/catalog.mjs:1884-1894` — retirement writes the manifest through `writeFileAtomic` and only calls `writerError("SKILL_PUBLISH_FAILED", …)` on throw (re-opened at HEAD) · Where: `zink-skill-store/src/catalog.mjs:1692` — `await fsyncDirectoryBestEffort(parentDir);` on the promotion path · Where: `zink-skill-store/src/catalog.mjs:1163-1173` — `fsyncDirectoryBestEffort` opens the directory, calls `handle.sync()`, and its `catch {}` comment says "durability hint only; the rename is what makes the entry atomic" · Where: `zink-skill-store/src/catalog.mjs:976-987` — `writeFileAtomic` fsyncs the file handle only, never the parent directory
- Reproduction: LATENT: the asymmetry was measured by comparing the two write paths line by line — the promotion path reaches a directory sync at `:1692` while the retirement path at `:1885-1888` has no directory sync call between the rename and the acknowledged return, and the shared helper's `catch {}` at `:1171-1173` makes any POSIX EIO unobservable; reproducing the loss itself needs a POSIX host with power loss or crash injection immediately after the retirement rename, which no lane here can drive
- Acceptance criteria: the retirement path in `catalog.mjs` (around `:1884-1894`) anchors its rename with a parent-directory sync the way promotion does at `:1692`, and `fsyncDirectoryBestEffort` surfaces a non-platform-class failure instead of swallowing it, so a fault injected into the directory sync during retirement is reported rather than acknowledged silently
- Related findings: same helper as `R9-L06-3` (row F-410) — one patch site, two verdicts, kept separate because the fixes differ · also invisible to the lane in `R9-T03-2` (row F-415), whose predicate cannot read an async writer · nguồn: R9 L06-10 — Skill Store retirement write durability

### F-416 — a failed exclusive preimage write leaves a record-less transaction directory that findOrphanedTransaction reports as unreadable and every later apply refuses, with no recovery surface

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-T03-3`
- Affected modules: deployment topology, Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: High
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: it needs a fault inside the capture loop (throw between the `mkdirSync` at `:760` and the record write at `:786-789`) — e.g. the F-409 base64 throw or a disk error — and no clean apply at HEAD produces it
- Root cause: `writeExclusiveFile` opens with "wx" and has no unlink-on-failure path (`:638-648`), the preimage loop creates the transaction and PREIMAGE directories before anything is durable (`:758-763`), and the record is written last (`:786-789`), so an interrupted capture leaves a directory that is neither a transaction nor empty; `findOrphanedTransaction` then classifies it as `unreadable`/`TRANSACTION_RECORD_UNREADABLE` (`:948-966`) and the apply orchestration refuses on any orphan (`apply-orchestration.mjs:126-139`), which reads to the operator as store-wide corruption rather than as one half-written capture — and the module exports no retire or remove helper, so nothing in the shipped surface can clear it
