- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:638-648` — `writeExclusiveFile` uses `openSync(file, "wx", mode)`, fsyncs and closes, and never unlinks on failure (re-opened at HEAD) · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:758-763` — per-entry `mkdirSync(path.dirname(target), …)` inside the capture loop · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:786-795` — the record is written last, after the loop, and only then verified by `loadTransaction` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:948-966` — `findOrphanedTransaction` pushes `{ status: "unreadable", reason: "TRANSACTION_RECORD_UNREADABLE" }` and its comment states it "never deletes, resumes or rolls anything back" · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/apply-orchestration.mjs:126-139` — `stage = "orphan-check"; if (orphans.length > 0) return { status: "FAIL", … reason: ORPHANED_TRANSACTION }` · Measured: `grep -n "^export "` over the module lists no retire/remove helper; `operator-apply.mjs` contains zero occurrences of "orphan" and `apply-orchestration.mjs:39`/`:125-138` mention it only as read-only reporting/refusal, so the no-recovery-surface claim stands
- Reproduction: LATENT: the ordering was measured by reading the capture function — the first durable action is the directory/preimage write at `:760-761` and the record only appears at `:786`, with no try/finally cleanup around the loop — and the refusal was confirmed by the orchestration gate at `:126-139` plus the unreadable classification at `:948-966`; demonstrating it end-to-end needs fault injection into the write loop (an abort after one preimage lands) in a scratch state root, which no lane drives
- Acceptance criteria: either the capture is reversible and journalled (write the record first, or remove partially created directories on any throw, so no record-less transaction directory can ever exist), or an explicit operator flag retires a record-less transaction directory by ID with a named reason code; the regression is a mid-loop fault injection asserting a subsequent apply still runs, and removing the cleanup/refusal carve-out turns it red
- Related findings: the same writer as `R9-L03-2` (row F-409), whose base64 throw is a concrete way to trigger this residue · refuses the apply the ceremony in `R9-T03-1` (row F-414) depends on · nguồn: R9 T03-3 — preimage capture crash residue and apply gate

### F-417 — queue-manager rethrows a post-rename directory-fsync failure as QUEUE_STORAGE_WRITE_FAILED after the rename already committed, so a durable record is reported as a failed write

- Category: F6 — Reliability & Performance
- Legacy register id: `R9-T03-4`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: Medium
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: it needs a POSIX host whose directory fsync fails for a non-tolerated reason after the rename committed — the dev host is win32, where the EPERM/EISDIR/EINVAL/ENOTSUP class is deliberately swallowed at `:410-414` — and no gate injects such a fault
- Root cause: the catch at `queue-manager.mjs:417-431` treats any throw from the durability block as a write failure: it removes the temp name that has already been renamed away (`:428`) and raises `QUEUE_STORAGE_WRITE_FAILED` (`:429`), even though `renameSync` at `:399` had already committed the record, so the caller rolls its in-memory state back behind a disk that holds the newer record; the ledger's snapshot writer shares the same policy (`ledger.mjs:1625-1638`), while the third writer in the family already implements the correct classification — `decision-ledger.mjs:455-493` marks the error `committed = true` and reloads from disk — so this looks like a queue storage fault rather than as a fix that landed in only one of three copies
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:399` — `renameSync(temp, storagePath);` commits the record before any directory sync (re-opened at HEAD) · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:402-415` — the post-rename `openSync(dir,"r")`/`fsyncSync` block rethrows anything outside the win32 tolerance set · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:417-431` — the outer catch calls `removeWriterResidue(temp)` at `:428` and throws `QUEUE_STORAGE_WRITE_FAILED` at `:429` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/ledger.mjs:1625-1638` — the snapshot writer's identical rethrow policy after `renameSync` at `:1624` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/decision-ledger.mjs:455-493` — the sibling's `LEDGER_COMMIT_UNCERTAIN` with `uncertain.committed = true` and reload-from-disk, whose comment names the queue-manager and ledger paths as owing the same treatment
- Reproduction: LATENT: the ordering was read at HEAD (rename at `:399` strictly precedes the sync block whose error the catch at `:417` converts into a write failure), and the divergence was measured by comparing `decision-ledger.mjs:455-493`, which classifies the identical fault as a committed-but-uncertain write; firing it requires POSIX fsync fault injection — e.g. an injected EIO from the directory sync after the rename — which no lane in this repository drives, and on win32 the tolerated error codes mask the whole path
- Acceptance criteria: a directory-fsync failure raised after `renameSync` is classified as a commit-uncertain warning that reloads state from disk (mirroring `decision-ledger.mjs:455-493`) in both `queue-manager.mjs` and the `ledger.mjs` snapshot writer, and the temp-residue removal at `:428` runs only while the temp still exists; the fault-injection test asserts the acknowledged record survives and no `QUEUE_STORAGE_WRITE_FAILED` is raised post-rename — reverting to a plain rethrow turns it red
- Related findings: `R9-T03-2` (row F-415) is the lane that should have caught the divergence but reads only the sync-API shape of this writer · the committed-uncertain classification documented at `decision-ledger.mjs:478-490`, and the `QUEUE_STORAGE_WRITE_FAILED` code itself, have zero register rows per the round-3 note · nguồn: R9 T03-4 — queue and ledger post-rename durability error policy

### F-418 — capture resolves Discord channel targets dynamically but restore membership is keyed on the pinned default, so a non-pinned install's channel file resolves against the runtime root and its rollback throws TRANSACTION_RESTORE_TARGET_MISSING

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-L03-1`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), deployment topology
- Severity: High
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: firing requires an install whose Discord channel path resolves differently from the pinned default that `nativePatchedDiscordChannelRelatives()` returns at `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/native-patch.mjs:128-129`; with the pinned hash `rootForRelative` matches the static list and the defect cannot fire
- Root cause: capture addresses channel targets through the dynamic resolver (`transaction-snapshot.mjs:215-230`) while `rootForRelative` (`:156-164`) tests membership against `SNAPSHOT_TARGETS.discordChannel`, built from the PINNED default (`:101`), so any non-pinned relative path falls to the `runtimeRoot` branch and restore demands the file under the runtime root. Round 3 also found the wedge UNDERSTATED, not just a fail-closed refuse: `attemptRollback` leaves the record STARTED (`apply-orchestration.mjs:313-318`) and `markTransactionComplete` has no operator caller (operator-lock-recovery handles only the ledger writer lock), so an orphaned transaction has NO recovery driver.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:156-164` — re-opened at HEAD: `rootForRelative` returns `stateDir` only for `SNAPSHOT_TARGETS.state`/`extensions/zink-governance/`-prefixed/`SNAPSHOT_TARGETS.discordChannel` members, everything else is `runtimeRoot` · Where: `transaction-snapshot.mjs:101` — `discordChannel: nativePatchedDiscordChannelRelatives()`, the pinned constant list (re-opened) · Where: `transaction-snapshot.mjs:362-363` — `if (!stat || !stat.isFile()) throw new Error("TRANSACTION_RESTORE_TARGET_MISSING")` (re-opened) · Where: `native-patch.mjs:128-129` — the pinned install content-hash behind the default · Measured: CHANGELOG.md:1621 (`R2-B08-04`) closed the resolver half but left `rootForRelative` keyed on the constant — this is a residual/incomplete closure, and the round-2 "fails closed and HARD" finding was verified while the orphaned-transaction wedge stands
- Reproduction: LATENT — read-only proof at HEAD: the capture path (`:215-230`) can name a channel relative that the static list at `:101` does not contain, and the `:156-164` membership test then routes it to `runtimeRoot`, where `:362-363` throws; it cannot fire at HEAD because the shipped default state root carries the pinned channel hash. Fault-injection shape: a fixture install `openclaw-discord-99aa00ff` whose channel package relative is absent from `SNAPSHOT_TARGETS.discordChannel` — capture succeeds, rollback throws, and the record is left STARTED with no recovery driver.
- Acceptance criteria: `rootForRelative` records the root per entry (or every resolver-produced path is classified state-relative), so a non-pinned channel target restores from where it was captured; the `openclaw-discord-99aa00ff` fixture turns red the moment membership reverts to the pinned constant, and the wedge is bounded by a recovery driver that can retire a STARTED record after a failed rollback.
- Related findings: residual of `R2-B08-04` (CHANGELOG.md:1621; the resolver closed, `rootForRelative` unchanged) · same rollback-wedge surface as F-422 · nguồn: R9 V04 — apply/rollback ceremony (L03/T04 lane, 10/10 verified)

### F-419 — the post-apply `applied` verdict derives solely from the caller-declared expected list while `changed` is computed and discarded, so a mutation outside the native post-image still reports a verified apply

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R9-L03-4`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), deployment topology
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: every shipped operator apply computes its verdict through `verifyLiveRestore` with `expected` set to only the native post-image (`operator-apply.mjs:315`), so any tracked component that drifted outside that list is reported in `changed` and ignored while `applied` prints true
- Root cause: `applied` is `Array.isArray(options.expected) && options.expected.length > 0 && unverified.length === 0` — a function of the caller's declaration only — and `changed`, which names every tracked component differing from the preimage, has no non-test consumer, so the verdict measures "did what I said happen" instead of "did only what I said happen." The defect LOOKS security-adjacent but caps at Medium because the builder itself admits each state file it writes by digest allowlist (`configure.mjs:2058-2065`, `:2275-2301`), so an undeclared mutation must originate outside the builder.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:476-479` — re-opened at HEAD: `applied` is derived solely from `options.expected` non-empty plus `unverified.length === 0` · Where: `transaction-snapshot.mjs:483` — re-opened: `changed: mismatches.sort()` is computed in the same return and discarded by the apply path · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-apply.mjs:315` — expected list is only `nativeRuntimePostImage` · Where: `native-patch.mjs:1038-1064` — the post-image covers native+bridge only · Measured: the "F-106" prior cited by the candidate exists only in code comments (e.g. `transaction-snapshot.mjs:84`); grep for `F-106` in CHANGELOG.md returns zero rows, so it must not be cited as closed history
- Reproduction: Static consumer measurement at HEAD plus the candidate's state-root repro: corrupt a tracked builder file that is outside the declared expected list, run the apply-verify sequence, and the verdict still returns `applied: true` because only the native entries are checked; the test lane at `tests/m10-t2-r8…:217-228` asserts `changed` is populated but never asserts `applied: false` on an unexpected changed entry, which is why no gate catches this.
- Acceptance criteria: `applied` is false whenever `changed` names any tracked component outside the declared expected list (or the expected list is extended to every tracked component), with a regression that corrupts a non-expected tracked builder file and asserts `applied: false` — red the moment the `changed`-into-verdict link is removed.
- Related findings: deliberately kept separate from F-424 (the lane's KEEP SEPARATE: adding workspaces to the preimage never makes `applied` reject an unexpected `changed` entry, and tightening `applied` never surfaces workspaces) · verdict cross-checks with F-418's resolver accounting · nguồn: R9 V04 — apply/rollback ceremony (L03/T04 lane, 10/10 verified)

### F-420 — historical recovery restores the native baseline to T1 before verifyRuntimeBackup runs as the first statement inside the mutator, so a present-but-failing T1 backup leaves native at T1 with the T2 files still live

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-L03-5`
- Affected modules: deployment topology, Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: High
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: the ordering is proven but reaching it needs a PRESENT-BUT-FAILING backup — a tampered preimage (digest mismatch at `runtime-backup.mjs:451-455`), mode drift (`:476-488`), or a bumped T1_SHA (`:417-418`) — because the authorization precheck is existsSync-only (`operator-rollback.mjs:124-126`, `recovery-authorization.mjs:201-204`) and refuses only when a file is missing
- Root cause: integrity verification is a post-write observer, not a pre-write gate: the driver mutates first (`:134` restoreNativePatch) and the verifier only then runs inside the mutator (`runtime-backup.mjs:554`), so the half-mutation (native at T1, state files at T2) is the first failure the backup check ever sees — and it looks like a backup-validation problem rather than an ordering defect.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-rollback.mjs:124-126` — re-opened at HEAD: `backupPresent` is `existsSync(t1Config) && existsSync(.../manifest.json)`, presence only · Where: `operator-rollback.mjs:134-135` — re-opened: `restoreNativePatch(nativeRoots, nativeBackup);` executes before `await restoreT1RuntimeFiles(...)` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/runtime-backup.mjs:553-554` — re-opened: `await verifyRuntimeBackup({ stateDir, identifiers });` is the FIRST statement inside `restoreT1RuntimeFiles` · Where: `runtime-backup.mjs:451-455` — re-opened: `BACKUP_DIGEST_MISMATCH` on a tampered preimage · Measured: the round-2 merge with `R9-L03-7` (F-422) was REJECTED — driver-level ordering vs mutator-level compensation are distinct fixes
- Reproduction: LATENT — the ordering was proven by re-reading the driver (`:124-135`) and the mutator head (`:553-554`) at HEAD; to fire it in a scratch state root: create a valid T1 backup, then rewrite one preimage byte so `:451-455` throws, run operator-rollback with a valid authorization, and observe native restored to T1 while `restoreT1RuntimeFiles` fails before touching any file — the half-mutation the row describes.
- Acceptance criteria: operator-rollback verifies the full T1 backup (verifyRuntimeBackup plus native preimage checks) BEFORE invoking restoreNativePatch; a fixture with a tampered/absent-failing preimage asserts zero native mutation, and the test turns red the moment the verify call moves back inside the mutator.
- Related findings: `R2-B08-03` (CHANGELOG.md:1709) closed the preimage-install half, the ordering is unaddressed · the merge with F-422 was rejected by round 3 (distinct compensation layers) · same driver sequencing as F-426 · nguồn: R9 V04 — apply/rollback ceremony (L03/T04 lane, 10/10 verified)

### F-421 — quarantine prechecks every destination then performs N independent renames with no undo, so a failure on the second rename leaves the first tree moved and the docblock's all-or-nothing claim false

- Category: F6 — Reliability & Performance
- Legacy register id: `R9-L03-6`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), deployment topology
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: firing needs at least 2 displaced trees AND a fault injected into the 2nd-or-later rename (`:270`), and the existing lane (the agent-final R8 quarantine test cited by the verdict) moves only ONE tree, so the multi-element claim is untested above one element and no shipped run has demonstrated a mid-loop failure
- Root cause: the destination precheck loop (`:261-267`) validates all moves, then the execution loop (`:269-273`) performs bare `renameSync` calls with no rollback, contradicting the `:240-243` docblock's all-or-nothing claim; the residual data-stranding flavor (first tree quarantined, second still in extensions/ → duplicate plugin id with stale code) is why the candidate filed F5, but the nature is a non-atomic multi-step filesystem operation, so the register grades it F6 (verdict moved Medium→Low and F5→F6 for the narrow fault window and Low impact).
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/runtime-backup.mjs:261-267` — re-opened at HEAD: per-move `noLinks` and `existsSync(move.target)` prechecks throwing `RESTORE_QUARANTINE_CONFLICT` before any move · Where: `runtime-backup.mjs:269-273` — re-opened: independent `renameSync(move.source, move.target)` + two `syncDirectory` calls per iteration with no undo path anywhere in the loop · Where: `runtime-backup.mjs:240-243` — the docblock claiming all-or-nothing · Measured: `R8-M1-02` (CHANGELOG.md:1893) introduced the normal path; this is the failure path it never exercised above one element
- Reproduction: LATENT — read-only proof at HEAD: the precheck and execution are separate loops (`:261-267` vs `:269-273`) with no compensation on rename failure; to fire, place two displaced sibling trees under `stateDir/extensions/`, fault-inject the second `renameSync` (EPERM on the quarantine parent after the first move committed), and observe the first tree in `quarantined-t2` while the second remains live.
- Acceptance criteria: `quarantineDisplacedPluginTrees` either rolls back committed moves on a later rename failure or stages atomically, and a forced second-rename-failure test with two displaced trees asserts the extensions directory is fully before-or-after, turning red when the undo is removed.
- Related findings: the failure-path half paired with F-425's policy-path bypass of the same rename loop · `R8-M1-02` (CHANGELOG.md:1893) introduced the normal path only · nguồn: R9 V04 — apply/rollback ceremony (L03/T04 lane, 10/10 verified)

### F-422 — restoreT1RuntimeFiles commits the config write and the quarantine renames before verification runs last, so a partial failure leaves mixed T1/T2 state plus a permanent refusal on every retry

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-L03-7`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), deployment topology
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: every historical recovery calls this mutator (`operator-rollback.mjs:135`), the re-entry refusals fire on plain sequencing with NO crash required (rollback → redeploy → rollback suffices), and the workspaces/extension-target wedge at `runtime-backup.mjs:612-614` is already demonstrated by a passing (green) test at `tests/m7-t2-openclaw.test.mjs:316-320`
- Root cause: the mutator writes first and verifies last: the config branch commits `writeNew(savedConfig)` (`:640`), `writeNew(temporary, fallback)` (`:642`) and `renameSync(temporary, config)` (`:644`), the quarantine loop renames live targets (`:660-670`), and only then `:687-691` throws `RESTORE_VERIFICATION_FAILED` — with no journal or compensation, the retried recovery refuses at `:612-614` (existing quarantine destination) or `:621` (`RESTORE_TEMP_CONFLICT` for a residue from a death between `:642` and `:644`). The round-2 flag that confined the permanent wedge to the config branch was PARTLY WRONG and this row carries the correction: `:612-614` wedges identically for workspaces/extension targets, so the mixed T1/T2 end state is reachable on more than one branch.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/runtime-backup.mjs:638-644` — re-opened at HEAD: quarantine mkdir, `writeNew(savedConfig, current)`, `writeNew(temporary, fallback)`, `renameSync(temporary, config)` all commit before any verification · Where: `runtime-backup.mjs:660-670` — re-opened: per-target `renameSync(source, destination)` into quarantine · Where: `runtime-backup.mjs:687-691` — re-opened: `restoreMismatches` throws `RESTORE_VERIFICATION_FAILED` only AFTER all mutations · Where: `runtime-backup.mjs:612-614` — re-opened: `RESTORE_QUARANTINE_CONFLICT` on an existing destination for every non-extensions target, so the wedge is not config-only · Where: `runtime-backup.mjs:619-621` — re-opened: fixed-name restore temp → `RESTORE_TEMP_CONFLICT`; the `:646-649` comment shows the caught-path cleanup (the fix registered as an F-163 code comment) only covers the renameSync throw, not a death between writes · Measured: `SNAPSHOT_TARGETS` (`transaction-snapshot.mjs:77-112`, re-opened) contains 16 keys per the verdict's enumeration and none under `operator/`, so no shipped prune can clear `quarantined-t2` · site added by R10 (V04, from R10-T07-5): Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-rollback.mjs:136-146` — the historical recovery restarts the live gateway inside this row's unverified window, and unlike `apply-orchestration.mjs:222-240` this driver has no `attemptRollback` compensation arm · Measured: `native-patch.mjs:1288-1310` verifies a great deal BEFORE the restart, so the claim is the missing compensation arm, not "no verification"
- Reproduction: Fault sequence proven at HEAD without crashing: rollback → redeploy → rollback — the second rollback re-enters `restoreT1RuntimeFiles`, the already-quarantined destinations trip `:612-614` (or `:632-637`) and refuse forever while the runtime sits in mixed T1/T2 state; `tests/m7-t2-openclaw.test.mjs:316-320` already passes by demonstrating exactly this refusal on a non-config target, and a simulated death between `:642` and `:644` leaves the fixed-name temp refusing at `:621`.
- Acceptance criteria: verification precedes commitment or a durable journal drives compensation, so any partial failure either converges the runtime fully to T1 or leaves it fully T2 and retryable; the rollback → redeploy → rollback sequence must end in a non-refused state, and the test turns red when the journal/ordering control is removed. · amendment added by R10 (V04): the acceptance must also require the compensation arm `apply-orchestration.mjs:222-240` already carries, so that a failed post-restart verification in `operator-rollback.mjs:136-146` restores the previous state instead of leaving the restarted gateway as the outcome
- Related findings: merge with F-420 rejected by round 3 (mutator-level compensation vs driver-level ordering) · `R2-B08-03` (CHANGELOG.md:1709) closed the preimage-install half only · the fixed-temp residue fix covers the caught path only (`runtime-backup.mjs:646-649`) · severity RAISED by the verdict from Medium to High · the row carries the round-2 flag correction (the wedge is not confined to the config branch) · nguồn: R9 V04 — apply/rollback ceremony (L03/T04 lane, 10/10 verified)

### F-423 — the OpenClaw runtime root is a machine literal in all three live drivers and is frozen into each durable transaction while verifyLiveRestore discards the caller's roots with `void roots;`, so no shipped command can assert or redirect the tree a rollback writes

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-T04-1`
- Affected modules: deployment topology, Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `operator-apply.mjs:51` hardcodes `const runtimeRoot = "/home/hoang_vinh/.local/lib/node_modules/openclaw";` (a tracked-source machine path, re-opened at HEAD) while `stateDir` is operator input (`:46-50`); the same literal sits in `operator-rollback.mjs:59` and `live-governance-probe.mjs:128`
- Root cause: "the operator names the root" holds only for the state root; the runtime root is duplicated per driver, frozen into the snapshot (`transaction-snapshot.mjs:288-289`), the record (`:776-778`) and the reload (`:849-855`), and `verifyLiveRestore` throws away the caller-supplied roots with `void roots;` (`:432-433`, re-opened) — so a rollback verifies against the snapshot's own roots, not the operator's. The candidate filed F2 because it looks like an authorization seam; the verdict capped it Medium/F3 because no shipped caller diverges at HEAD — it is a state-integrity/contract gap, not a live bypass.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-apply.mjs:51` — re-opened at HEAD: the machine literal, with `stateDir` env/argv input at `:46-50` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:432-433` — re-opened: `export function verifyLiveRestore(roots, snapshot, options = {}) { void roots;` — the caller's roots are discarded · Where: `operator-rollback.mjs:59` and `live-governance-probe.mjs:128` — the other two driver literals · Where: `transaction-snapshot.mjs:288-289,776-778,849-855` — the literal frozen into snapshot, record and reload; `restoreTransactionSnapshot:352-353` and `rootsFrom` resolve from the snapshot's own roots · Measured: the m8-t2.9-r12 test lane self-acknowledges the gap at `:1100-1114`
- Reproduction: Static measurement at HEAD: grep the three live drivers for the literal (all present), read `verifyLiveRestore` (`:432-433`, re-opened) to confirm the caller's roots are voided, and trace `restoreTransactionSnapshot:352-353` resolving from `rootsFrom(snapshot)`; no shipped command path lets the operator pass a different runtimeRoot or asserts caller roots equal snapshot roots before any write — the Medium cap comes from the fact that no shipped caller diverges today.
- Acceptance criteria: the runtime root becomes an operator-named input with the same refusal semantics as the state root, `verifyLiveRestore` asserts caller-supplied roots equal the snapshot's roots before any write (the `void roots;` statement is gone), and a driver fixture with a diverging runtimeRoot turns red when the assertion is removed.
- Related findings: `F-090` (docs/execution/findings.md:220, REFUTED) and `F-373` (CHANGELOG.md:651-661) measure the STATE root only — neither closes this runtime-root half · nguồn: R9 V04 — apply/rollback ceremony (L03/T04 lane, 10/10 verified)

### F-424 — the two backup subsystems enumerate different governed sets, so every apply rewrites seven workspaces AGENTS.md files the transaction never preimages while historical recovery refuses the builder files and prints T1_RESTORED leaving them at T2

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R9-T04-2`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), deployment topology, infrastructure scripts
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the builder rewrites `workspaces/<id>/AGENTS.md` on every apply (`configure.mjs:2049-2051`, content moves on every GOVERNANCE_VERSION bump at `policy.mjs:1662`) while `SNAPSHOT_TARGETS` (`transaction-snapshot.mjs:77-112`, re-opened at HEAD) contains no workspaces entry, so the divergence burns on every real apply
- Root cause: two hand-maintained target enumerations drifted apart: the transaction preimage omits all 7 `workspaces/<id>/AGENTS.md` that the runtime-backup subsystem declares governed (`runtime-backup.mjs:444` accepts them and `:457-460` REQUIRES them — both re-opened at HEAD), while the same backup predicate refuses the 4 F-106 builder files and the systemd drop-in that the transaction DOES restore, so historical recovery completes and prints `T1_RESTORED` (`operator-rollback.mjs:153`, re-opened) leaving those files at T2; `workspaces/<id>/.gitkeep` is in NEITHER set. The defect looks like a coverage gap but is a contract violation between two subsystems that each claim to define the governed surface.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:77-112` — re-opened at HEAD: `SNAPSHOT_TARGETS` keys are state (9 entries incl. the 4 F-106 files at `:89-92`), runtime, discordChannel, systemd — no `workspaces/` anywhere · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/runtime-backup.mjs:444` — re-opened: `AGENT_IDS.some((id) => entry.relative === \`workspaces/${id}/AGENTS.md\`)` is required-governed, `:447` `BACKUP_TARGET_DENIED` refuses what the transaction restores · Where: `runtime-backup.mjs:457-460` — re-opened: `required = ["openclaw.json", ...AGENT_IDS.map(id => workspaces/${id}/AGENTS.md)]`· Where:`integrations/openclaw/configure.mjs:2049-2051`→`integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:2221-2222` — the per-apply workspace rewrite · Measured: the verdict's symmetric-difference probe enumerated 7 workspaces AGENTS.md only in the backup set and the 4 builder files + systemd drop-in only in the transaction set; no CHANGELOG row mentions workspaces/
- Reproduction: Read-only probe the verdict ran: import both modules at HEAD, print each subsystem's governed target set, and take the symmetric difference — measured non-empty in both directions (transaction omits all 7 `workspaces/<id>/AGENTS.md`; the backup's governed predicate refuses the 4 builder files + systemd drop-in). Additionally: bump GOVERNANCE_VERSION, apply, run historical recovery — it prints `T1_RESTORED` while the rewritten AGENTS.md files were never in `snapshot.files`, so `verifyLiveRestore` cannot report them.
- Acceptance criteria: either `SNAPSHOT_TARGETS` is asserted a superset of the runtime-backup governed targets (including workspaces AGENTS.md and .gitkeep) or `runtime-backup` rejects anything the transaction cannot restore; a symmetric-difference check over both module exports turns red the moment either enumeration gains a surface the other lacks.
- Related findings: carries the merged candidate `R9-L03-3` (the identical defect statement's workspaces/AGENTS.md half — builder-rewritten workspace AGENTS.md outside the transaction preimage) · kept separate from F-419 (tightening `applied` never surfaces workspaces) · no CHANGELOG row mentions workspaces/ · nguồn: R9 V04 — apply/rollback ceremony (L03/T04 lane, 10/10 verified)

### F-425 — the preimage delete gate covers deletion only while quarantine moves live sibling extension trees by rename that no preimage records, so rollback verifies restored:true with the plugin loader's candidate set permanently changed

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-T04-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), deployment topology
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: every apply that finds displaced sibling trees runs `createRuntimeBackup` → `quarantineDisplacedPluginTrees`, whose `renameSync` loop (`runtime-backup.mjs:269-273`, re-opened at HEAD) moves `stateDir/extensions/<sibling>` trees that `SNAPSHOT_TARGETS` (`transaction-snapshot.mjs:77-112`, re-opened) never records — its only extensions entries are zink-governance's 4 named files at `:80-83`
- Root cause: `preimageRecreates`/`pruneTransactionPath` (`transaction-snapshot.mjs:527-564`, re-opened) gate DELETION on recreatability only, and the rename path is an equivalent relocation that never consults the gate; `verifyLiveRestore` then iterates only `snapshot.files` (`:436-458`, re-opened), so the moved trees are invisible and a verified rollback reports `restored: true` with the loader's candidate set changed — the plugin-precedence condition the R8-M1-02 quarantine finding describes. The candidate filed this High/F2-adjacent; the verdict capped it Medium because the trees are MOVED not deleted and the change is manually reversible.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:527-540` — re-opened at HEAD: `preimageRecreates` returns false for any path whose children are not all in `snapshot.files` · Where: `transaction-snapshot.mjs:555-564` — re-opened: `pruneTransactionPath` deletes only when recreatable, otherwise `{deleted:false, retained, reason:"TRANSACTION_PREIMAGE_CANNOT_RECREATE"}` — a deletion-only gate · Where: `runtime-backup.mjs:269-273` — re-opened: the displacement loop `renameSync(move.source, move.target)` with no `preimageRecreates` call anywhere in it · Where: `operator-apply.mjs:255` — the apply's prune routes r12 quarantine through the gate, the backup's rename bypasses it · Where: `findDisplacedPluginTrees` (`runtime-backup.mjs:212-236`) selects sibling trees with `:221 continue`, and `discoverExtensionAssets` (`transaction-snapshot.mjs:148-153`, re-opened) enumerates only `extensions/zink-governance/` · Measured: the gate's own comment cites F-106 (`transaction-snapshot.mjs:543`) — a code comment only, zero CHANGELOG rows
- Reproduction: Static proof at HEAD: trace both mutation surfaces — delete goes through `pruneTransactionPath` (gate re-opened at `:555-561`) and rename goes through `quarantineDisplacedPluginTrees` (`:269-273`) with no gate call; a scenario with one displaced sibling tree then apply→rollback returns `restored: true` because `verifyLiveRestore` (`:436`) iterates only `snapshot.files`, never naming the relocated tree whose loader precedence condition changed.
- Acceptance criteria: the quarantine rename is routed through the same recreatability gate or every displaced tree is recorded as a snapshot target, and a scenario that relocates a sibling tree then rolls back must either restore it or name it in the verdict — red the moment the gate call or the target record is removed.
- Related findings: same rename loop as F-421 (this is the policy hole, F-421 the failure-path atomicity) · `R8-M1-02` (CHANGELOG.md:1893) and `R2-B08-03` (CHANGELOG.md:1709) plus the F-106 code comments cover other halves, none this ordering · Medium cap per the verdict: moved not deleted, manually reversible · nguồn: R9 V04 — apply/rollback ceremony (L03/T04 lane, 10/10 verified)

### F-426 — operator-rollback prints installed_mutation:true unconditionally in its single catch, including for pre-mutation failures, overstating a write that never happened while the honest boundary machinery exists next door

- Category: F1 — Logic & Functional Bugs
- Legacy register id: `R9-T04-4`
- Affected modules: deployment topology
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: any non-refusal exception in the try body emits the catch record with `installed_mutation: true` (`operator-rollback.mjs:173-182`, re-opened at HEAD) — and both `identifiers()` (`:108-113`, re-opened, throws `INVALID_IDENTIFIER_FILE`) and `assertNoSymlinkComponents(authorizationPath)` (`:120`, re-opened) throw BEFORE the first mutation at `:134` (re-opened)
- Root cause: the driver adopted none of the mutation-boundary machinery its sibling uses: `apply-orchestration.mjs` tracks the boundary at `:82,104-105,182,277` precisely so reports are honest, and the refusal path in this very file already emits `installed_mutation: false` (`operator-rollback.mjs:100`, re-opened; `recovery-authorization.mjs:172-177` likewise), yet the catch hardcodes true. This is diagnostic honesty in the conservative direction — it over-reports mutation, never under-reports — which is why the register grades it F1/Medium, not a state defect.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-rollback.mjs:173-182` — re-opened at HEAD: the catch's stdout record carries the literal `installed_mutation: true` at `:181` for every non-RecoveryRefused error · Where: `operator-rollback.mjs:108-113` — re-opened: `identifiers()` throws `INVALID_IDENTIFIER_FILE` on a non-0600/oversized file · Where: `operator-rollback.mjs:120` — re-opened: `assertNoSymlinkComponents(authorizationPath)` runs pre-mutation · Where: `operator-rollback.mjs:134` — re-opened: `restoreNativePatch(...)` is the first mutating call, AFTER both throw sites · Measured: `apply-orchestration.mjs:82,104-105,182,277` and `recovery-authorization.mjs:172-177` show the honest machinery already exists on the sibling paths; no register row names `installed_mutation` for this driver
- Reproduction: The verdict's repro: valid authorization + a present-but-unreadable `discord-identifiers.json` at mode 0644 → `identifiers()` throws `INVALID_IDENTIFIER_FILE` at `:112` before any write, yet the driver prints `installed_mutation: true` with `reason: INVALID_IDENTIFIER_FILE` — a report of a mutation that provably did not occur, confirmed at HEAD by re-opening the throw sites (`:108-113`, `:120`) and the first mutation (`:134`).
- Acceptance criteria: a `mutationStarted` flag set immediately before `restoreNativePatch` gates the `installed_mutation` field (plus per-step disclosure of what completed), and the mode-0644 identifiers fixture asserts `installed_mutation: false` — the test turns red when the flag is removed or the literal returns.
- Related findings: same driver as F-420 (that row is the ordering half, this the reporting half) · sources the mutation-boundary pattern from `apply-orchestration.mjs:82,104-105,182,277` · nguồn: R9 V04 — apply/rollback ceremony (L03/T04 lane, 10/10 verified)

### F-430 — the skill-store CLI's deprecate command commits the retirement under the writer lock but triggers no sync and no sync command exists in the CLI at all, so a retired skill stays ACTIVE/READY and searchable until an unrelated plugin approval happens to sync

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R9-L06-5`
- Affected modules: Zink Skill Store, Zink Tools
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `bin/skill-store.mjs:193-219` (measured) calls only `retireSkill` and a grep for `sync` in the CLI returns zero command references (the sole match is the substring in `async` at `:145`), while `skill-adapter.mjs:638` keeps serving ACTIVE/READY rows until some later full sync runs
- Root cause: the CLI's deprecate wraps `retireSkill` in `withSkillStoreWriterLock` and returns the retired record without touching the derived index, and the command inventory is validate/get/deprecate/migrate with no sync — the operator-facing contract says retirement withdraws the skill, but withdrawal of the served row depends on an unrelated approval in the plugin triggering a sync. The violation is the contract/integration seam (F4); the staleness window is the consequence.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-skill-store/bin/skill-store.mjs:193-219` — the deprecate branch: parse, lock at :196, `retireSkill` at :197-204, response returned at :206-219, no sync call (measured) · Measured: case-insensitive grep for `sync` over the CLI file returns only the `async` substring at `:145` · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/skill-store/skill-adapter.mjs:638` — the served-row filter on ACTIVE/READY · Where: the postgres suite calls sync manually after deprecate — the test lane self-documents the gap
- Reproduction: CLI run against a scratch store: `skill-store deprecate` a synced skill, then query through the adapter without running any plugin approval — the retired skill is still served ACTIVE/READY in the derived index; it only disappears once an unrelated approval triggers `syncSkillsThroughDoor`.
- Acceptance criteria: `skill-store deprecate` either propagates the retirement to the derived index or refuses with a documented offline sync path, so a single CLI command leaves canonical and derived state consistent; the regression test retires through the CLI and serves one adapter query without any manual sync, turning red when the propagation is removed.
- Related findings: F-429 is the distinct present-but-unlocked-trigger sibling (this row is the trigger that is missing entirely) · F-095 (closed) made retirement possible under the lock but never wired its propagation · carries no other candidate · nguồn: R9 L06 — Zink Skill Store vector sync (verdict lane V05)

### F-431 — the migration ledger INSERT runs as a separate unlocked statement after the DDL transaction COMMITs with no ON CONFLICT against the version PRIMARY KEY, so two concurrent first-run syncs make the loser throw and stamp INDEX_FAILED even though its canonical promotion committed

- Category: F6 — Reliability & Performance
- Legacy register id: `R9-L06-6`
- Affected modules: Zink Skill Store, OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `applySkillStoreMigrations` is invoked on every approval sync (`index.mjs:1175`, measured) outside any lock, and the loop body is one committed DDL transaction (`migrations.mjs:147`, measured) plus a separate plain INSERT (`:148-152`, measured) into a `version text PRIMARY KEY` ledger
- Root cause: the runner skips files already recorded (`:133-146`) but the check and the insert are not one atomic statement, so two processes on a first run both pass the skip check and both execute the INSERT — the loser hits the primary-key violation, lands in the caller's catch, and the approval stamps INDEX_FAILED (`index.mjs:1515`) after the canonical promotion already committed. The F3 lifecycle consequence (a healthy derived index reported as failed) rides on an F6 atomicity defect.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-skill-store/src/migrations.mjs:147` — `executePostgresQuery("BEGIN;\n${sql}\nCOMMIT;")` — the DDL commits alone (measured) · Where: `:148-152` — the ledger INSERT into `${SKILL_STORE_LEDGER} (version, sha256)` with no ON CONFLICT clause (measured) · Where: `:64-68` — the ledger table declared with `version text PRIMARY KEY` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:1175` — migrations run inside `syncSkillsThroughDoor` on every approval (measured) · Where: `:1507, :1515` — the unlocked call site and the INDEX_FAILED stamp · site added by R10 (V12, from R10-T12-4): Where: `zink-skill-store/src/migrations.mjs:147` and `:148-152` — a process that dies between the DDL COMMIT and the ledger INSERT leaves the file unrecorded, so the next run re-sends committed DDL cold with no recovery · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:2440-2441` and `:2458-2459` — the Knowledgebase runner splits the same way and survives only because of the already-applied heuristic at `:2442-2457`, self-tagged in-source as F-166, an id that does not resolve in this register · Measured: no PostgreSQL was touched, so the crash-window consequence rests on this row's OPEN status rather than on a reproduction
- Reproduction: fault-injected timing, no code change: against a scratch PostgreSQL database with an empty migration ledger, force two concurrent first-run approval syncs so both pass the recorded-digest skip before either INSERT lands — one commits, the other throws a unique-key violation and its proposal is stamped INDEX_FAILED.
- Acceptance criteria: the ledger write becomes atomic with the DDL — same transaction, or an idempotent `ON CONFLICT` form of the INSERT at `migrations.mjs:148-152` — so parallel first-run syncs can never lose on the `version` PRIMARY KEY; a real-database concurrency test turns red when the INSERT is moved back outside the transaction. · widening added by R10 (V12): the acceptance must name both runner sites (`migrations.mjs:148-152` and `database.py:2440-2459`) and must forbid relying on a "was it already applied" guess, because making the INSERT atomic removes the need for that heuristic in both runners
- Related findings: F-094 (closed, CHANGELOG.md:1978) fixed migration ownership only, not this atomicity gap · F-436 shows this exact window is unlocked, which is what makes the collision reachable on an ordinary approval · nguồn: R9 L06 — Zink Skill Store vector sync (verdict lane V05)

### F-432 — the writer-lock guard marks the lock held at call time, before the underlying file lock is acquired, so a process merely waiting for the lock reports itself as its holder and the door's refuse-unless-held control trusts a pending acquisition

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R9-L06-8`
- Affected modules: Zink Skill Store
- Severity: Medium
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: every production door caller binds the gate only inside a completed acquisition (`index.mjs:1434→:1443`, `bin/skill-store.mjs:196→:197` measured), so no door call is reachable while the guard is lying
- Root cause: `withSkillStoreWriterLock` increments the `heldWriterLocks` Map at `catalog.mjs:1054` before `await withFileLock` acquires the mutex at `:1056`, and `isSkillStoreWriterLockHeld` (`:1072-1081`) answers from that Map, so the door's mandatory-lock control accepts a process that merely intends to hold. The round-1 framing "passes for a foreign holder" is overstated and recorded as refuted here — the Map is process-local and self-discloses exactly that at `catalog.mjs:1067-1070`; the surviving defect is the self-reported lie inside the same process, where a pending waiter passes the gate before the lock is real.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-skill-store/src/catalog.mjs:1052-1062` — `heldWriterLocks.set(lockDir, ... + 1)` at :1054 before `await withFileLock(lockDir, fn, options)` at :1056, decrement only in the finally (measured) · Where: `:1064-1070` — the docblock self-disclosure "The answer is about this process only" at :1067 · Where: `:1072-1081` — `isSkillStoreWriterLockHeld` reads the Map · Where: `zink-skill-store/bin/skill-store.mjs:196-197` — deprecate calls the gated path only inside the completed lock callback (measured) · Measured: round-2 probe with a foreign PID holding `<root>.writer.lock` — a process waiting in `withSkillStoreWriterLock` reports `isSkillStoreWriterLockHeld(root) === true` for the whole acquisition wait while the wrapped function never ran (fnRan=false)
- Reproduction: read-only two-process probe: process B blocks in `withSkillStoreWriterLock` while process A holds the writer lock, then evaluate `isSkillStoreWriterLockHeld` in B — measured true with B's callback un-entered; at HEAD no shipped door call executes inside that pending window, which is the named blocker keeping the row LATENT.
- Acceptance criteria: the held count is recorded only after the file lock is acquired — inside the `withFileLock` callback immediately before `fn` runs (`catalog.mjs:1052-1062`) — so the door refuses while an acquisition is pending in the same process; the two-process regression (holder blocks the lock, waiter's door call must be refused) turns red when the increment moves back to the function entry.
- Related findings: carries the merged candidate `R9-T05-2` (same lines, the door-control half with the wait-window measurement) · the door that consumes this guard is `zink-skill-store/index.mjs:61-69`, see F-435 · nguồn: R9 L06 — Zink Skill Store vector sync (verdict lane V05)

### F-433 — lock recovery reads owner.json once and then unlinks the lock directory without re-reading or comparing the owner token, so a writer that acquires between the read and the removal silently loses its live lock

- Category: F6 — Reliability & Performance
- Legacy register id: `R9-L06-9`
- Affected modules: Zink Skill Store
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the TOCTOU needs only interleaving, no fault injection — recovery reads the owner at `:142-144`, checks pid liveness at `:165`, then deletes at `:192-193`, and any acquisition landing inside that window is erased (all lines measured)
- Root cause: the recovery flow shape-checks the owner token for a 32-hex format only (`:153-156`) and never compares it to anything, so by removal time the file may belong to a brand-new holder; the pid check at `:165` vouches for the old holder, not for the current one, and `unlinkSync`/`rmdirSync` at `:192-193` run with no re-read. The tool looks like a safe orphaned-lock cleaner — with a live holder it refuses — but its confirmation is a snapshot, not a compare-before-delete.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-skill-store/bin/skill-store-lock-recovery.mjs:142-144` — `lstatSync`, `readFileSync`, `JSON.parse` of owner.json, one read only (measured) · Where: `:153-156` — token validated by shape (`/^[0-9a-f]{32}$/u`) and never compared to anything later (measured) · Where: `:165` — `holderState(report.owner_pid)` liveness of the pid read earlier (measured) · Where: `:192-193` — `unlinkSync(ownerPath); rmdirSync(lock);` with no re-read (measured) · Where: CHANGELOG.md:1009 and :1016 — closed rows R5-E13-06 and R6-I01-02 about the report stating presence rather than a nonce fragment, a different concern verified in the tree · site added by R10 (V04, from R10-L04-2): Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-lock-recovery.mjs:114`, `:128`, `:175-176` — read-once-then-delete on the orphan writer lock, the OpenClaw-side sibling of this row's site · Measured: the driver DOES shape-check the owner record (`:137-146`, `/^[0-9a-f]{32}$/u` plus a numeric pid), DOES check liveness (`:149-160`, `REFUSED_LIVE_HOLDER`) and DOES refuse without `--recover`, so only the TOCTOU half lands here; severity stays this row's Medium because firing needs the operator's own confirmed `--recover` run
- Reproduction: read-only interleaving on a scratch store, no fault injection: run recovery against a lock whose holder just exited; while recovery is between the owner read at `:144` and the unlink at `:192`, a new writer acquires the lock — recovery deletes the live lock directory and the new holder proceeds believing it is protected.
- Acceptance criteria: recovery re-reads owner.json and compares the owner token (and pid) immediately before `unlinkSync`/`rmdirSync` (`skill-store-lock-recovery.mjs:192-193`) — or removes via an atomic claim keyed to the token — so a lock created after the initial scan can never be deleted; the concurrent-acquisition test turns red when the pre-removal re-read is removed.
- Related findings: the closed rows at CHANGELOG.md:1009 and :1016 (R5-E13-06, R6-I01-02) are the report-sanitization concern, not this removal race · distinct from F-432 (same lock, different tool) · nguồn: R9 L06 — Zink Skill Store vector sync (verdict lane V05)

### F-434 — the adapter's freshness gate aggregates max(synced_at) over the whole ACTIVE/READY table with no correlation to the served rows, so one re-synced skill declares every row fresh and SKILL_INDEX_STALE fires only when the entire table is older than the 7-day SLA

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-T05-1`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools, Zink Skill Store
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the freshness marker at `skill-adapter.mjs:718-722` (measured) is one aggregate over all ACTIVE/READY rows with no join to the rows served at `:723-741` (measured), so any two rows of different ages already produce the false-fresh verdict
- Root cause: the marker SQL is `max(synced_at)` over the whole filtered table and `checkIndexFreshness` (`vector-sync.mjs:524-539`) takes that single stamp, so one successful sync updates the max for every query and rows never re-synced since before the 7-day SLA (`:21`) keep being served; `SKILL_INDEX_STALE` (`:852-858`) fires only when the entire table is older than the SLA. The per-row stamps written by sync (`vector-sync.mjs:633, :648, :1149`) prove rows age independently — the gate was shipped as a staleness control but cannot observe the per-row staleness it claims to guard.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-tools/adapters/skill-store/skill-adapter.mjs:718-722` — the `SELECT 'ZINK_FRESHNESS|' || COALESCE(to_char(max(synced_at) AT TIME ZONE 'UTC', ...), '') FROM skills.catalog_index WHERE status = 'ACTIVE' AND sync_status = 'READY'` marker, uncorrelated (measured) · Where: `:723-741` — the row-serving SELECT in the same script, joined only on segments→catalog with no freshness predicate (measured) · Where: `:714-717` — the docblock claiming the marker row is consumed by the freshness gate before any row is served · Where: `:638` — the ACTIVE/READY served filter · Where: `zink-skill-store/src/vector-sync.mjs:524-539` — checkIndexFreshness takes one timestamp; `:21` — the 7-day SLA; `:852-858` — the SKILL_INDEX_STALE gate; `:633, :648, :1149` — per-row synced_at stamps
- Reproduction: read-only scratch-index repro: two ACTIVE/READY rows with `synced_at` now−9d and now−1d; run an adapter query that serves the old row — the marker reads fresh because max(synced_at) is recent and SKILL_INDEX_STALE does not fire; only ageing the whole table past 7d trips the gate (the closed band's own suite pins the shipped marker, not per-row currency).
- Acceptance criteria: freshness is decided per served row (or per returned identity set) from the minimum `synced_at` of the rows the query actually returns, so a row older than the SLA can never be served while another skill was re-synced moments ago; the two-row test (one 9-day-old, one fresh) turns red when the gate reverts to the table-wide max.
- Related findings: F-430 is the missing-trigger sibling, this row the mis-bounding gate · the closed band recorded at CHANGELOG.md:203 shipped `SKILL_INDEX_STALE` reporting in place of serving stale rows — the graded row is that gate's table-wide bounding · nguồn: R9 T05 — skill store canonical vs derived index (verdict lane V06)

### F-435 — promoteApprovedSkill is exported twice with opposite locking contracts — the store's variant acquires the writer lock while the door's variant refuses unless it is already held — and the plugin's docblock justifies its placement with the variant it does not bind

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R9-T05-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Skill Store, OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: both exports are live at HEAD and measured distinct (`same function identity? false`), the plugin binds the door while `skill-adapter.mjs:20-23` imports store internals directly
- Root cause: one name carries two opposite contracts — `catalog.mjs:1358-1361` exports `promoteApprovedSkill` as the acquiring wrapper (measured) while the public door `zink-skill-store/index.mjs:61-69` refuses the same-named call unless the lock is already held (`:62` measured) — so import-site selection silently chooses whether the call self-serialises or errors; the Unlocked writers stay publicly importable (`catalog.mjs:1364, :1756`) and the adapter bypasses the door by importing store internals. The nature is the integration-contract violation (F4); the F2 authorization concern of the guard itself is the separate F-432 half.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-skill-store/src/catalog.mjs:1358-1361` — `promoteApprovedSkill` returns `withSkillStoreWriterLock(params.skillsRoot, () => promoteApprovedSkillUnlocked(params))` (measured) · Where: `zink-skill-store/index.mjs:61-69` — the door guard `if (!isSkillStoreWriterLockHeld(params?.skillsRoot)) {` refusing unless held, and the same guard shape again at `:81` (measured) · Where: `zink-skill-store/src/catalog.mjs:1364` — `export async function promoteApprovedSkillUnlocked` (measured) · Where: `:1756` — the second public unlocked export · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:1412` — the plugin binds the door while the docblock at `:1427-1429` justifies the placement with the acquiring variant it does NOT bind · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/skill-store/skill-adapter.mjs:20-23` — the adapter imports store internals · Measured: the two `promoteApprovedSkill` exports compared at runtime — same function identity false
- Reproduction: read-only import probe at HEAD: import `promoteApprovedSkill` from `zink-skill-store/index.mjs` and from `zink-skill-store/src/catalog.mjs`, assert identity — false (measured); call the door's variant outside any lock — refused by the guard at `:62`; executed the catalog variant outside a lock — it acquires, then failed only downstream with SKILL_IDENTITY_INVALID, proving the acquisition path runs.
- Acceptance criteria: one name per locking semantics — the door's refusing variant and the store's acquiring wrapper cannot share an importable name, the plugin docblock at `index.mjs:1427-1429` describes the variant actually bound, and `skill-adapter.mjs:20-23` no longer imports unlocked store writers (routed through the door per the AGENTS.md §9 boundary); the regression asserts the identity check between the two exports and turns red if both re-export the shared name.
- Related findings: F-045 (EXCLUDED at `docs/execution/findings.md:168-179`) is a DIFFERENT claim about `promoteApprovedSkill` — constant provenance defaults, verified before cross-referencing and not merged here · F-432 is the guard's own pending-acquisition half · nguồn: R9 T05 — skill store canonical vs derived index (verdict lane V06)

### F-436 — the schema migration runs on every approval inside the same post-lock window as the derived sync, so its DDL mutates the skills schema while another process is inside its writer-lock critical section

- Category: F6 — Reliability & Performance
- Legacy register id: `R9-T05-4`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Skill Store
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `applySkillStoreMigrations` is awaited at `index.mjs:1175` (measured) inside `syncSkillsThroughDoor`, which the approval flow invokes at `:1507` (measured) only after the writer-lock section closed at `:1487` (measured)
- Root cause: the migration DDL transaction therefore serialises against no process's critical section — it mutates `skills.*` while a concurrent promotion can be inside its `withSkillStoreWriterLock` section — and the call sits at the top of `syncSkillsThroughDoor` (measured at :1162-1182) so its ledger INSERT is the same second non-atomic statement as F-431. The runner's idempotence (the recorded-vs-observed digest compare) already guards byte-edited applied files, so what remains unlocked and non-atomic is the serialisation, not the detection.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:1174-1175` — `if (typeof skillStore.applySkillStoreMigrations === "function") { await skillStore.applySkillStoreMigrations(options); }` inside `syncSkillsThroughDoor` (measured) · Where: `:1177-1181` — the derived sync follows the same unlocked call path · Where: `:1487, :1507` — the writer-lock section closes, then the door call runs outside it (measured) · Where: `zink-skill-store/src/migrations.mjs:147-152` — DDL COMMIT then the separate ledger INSERT (measured) · Where: `migrations.mjs:132-146` — the recorded-vs-observed digest compare is ALREADY implemented and raises SKILL_STORE_MIGRATION_DIGEST_MISMATCH (measured) — the candidate asked for it again; the verdict records that half as refuted
- Reproduction: read-only locator trace plus real-DB concurrency plan: two processes — A in a promotion critical section under `withSkillStoreWriterLock` (entry `index.mjs:1434`), B completing an approval whose DDL at `index.mjs:1175` runs after its own lock closed at `:1487`; at HEAD no serialisation can be observed between them, and the scratch-DB race turns red only once the migration call moves inside the lock.
- Acceptance criteria: `applySkillStoreMigrations` is serialised against the store writer lock — entered inside it (or run once at boot before any door call) — so no DDL statement can land while another process is inside its critical section, and a real-database concurrency test turns red when the lock-wrapping is removed; the digest-compare half of the candidate's acceptance is already satisfied at `migrations.mjs:132-146` and is not asked for again.

### F-443 — every cleanup and retention path enumerates PDF content builds only through pdf_source_details, so terminal builds and builds whose link was reclaimed accumulate one build-and-batch set per run per PDF and can never be retired

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-T06-1`
- Affected modules: Zink Knowledgebase
- Severity: High
- Status: PARTIAL — the retirement half is closed by `a22e5d2`: `_retire_terminal_pdf_content_builds` deletes `FAILED` builds with no `pdf_source_details` link from both sweep entry points (`cleanup_superseded` and `recover_interrupted_runs`), and `tests/test_m12_t9_b5_pdf_build_retention.py` proves the four properties that matter (both entry points sweep, `CANCELLED` is never taken because `pdf_content_is_reclaimed` reads it back, and no index id gates the residue). Removing the sweep reddens all four cases (measured here). The acceptance criterion's named proof — a lane that runs two incremental index passes over one PDF and asserts no build-and-batch residue survives — is NOT shipped: the cases build the sweep's call sites directly, so the accumulation itself was not driven end to end. The row stays until that pass pair exists (2026-10-24).
- Root cause: retention is only a lifecycle status on a content-addressed row while both reuse rules read the content address and exclude terminal states, so a second identical build is legal and unenumerable; reclamation deletes the `pdf_source_details` link that is one of the table's two writers (`:1400` superseded re-link besides `:1833` `replace_pdf_source`), which removes the only handle the sweep has. It looks like the owner's `H9` reclamation being implemented because the reclamation itself succeeds (`:1197-1221`), yet the residue it leaves is invisible to every later cleanup path. Correction kept from the verdict: `pdf_source_details` has TWO writers, conclusion unchanged.
- Evidence: Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:2328-2333` — `_pdf_build_ids_for_indexes` returns build ids solely from `pdf_source_details` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:2350-2355` — `_retire_pdf_content_builds` deletes only builds with `NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM knowledge.pdf_source_details ...)` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:2091` and `:2300` — the two callers, `cleanup_superseded` (`:2079`) and `_purge_index_content` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:793` — reuse matches only `status IN ('BUILDING', 'READY')` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:1400` and `:1833` — the two `INSERT INTO knowledge.pdf_source_details` writers · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:1197-1221` — reclamation deletes details/segments/batches then marks CANCELLED · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:2379-2392` — FAILED terminal rows written per build · Where: `apps/knowledge/migrations/002_pdf_ingestion_foundation.sql:20-26` — the two partial unique indexes over `('BUILDING', 'READY')` and `= 'READY'` that make a duplicate identity legal once terminal · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:1177` — a second reclaim returns `ALREADY_RECLAIMED` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/index_lifecycle.py:360-370` — the run reports `SOURCE_INDEXING_FAILED` over the failed builds, the live residue the folded candidate R9-L08-1 described
- Reproduction: Walked every retirement path at HEAD: both cleanup entry points (`database.py:2091`, `:2300`) source candidate builds only from the `pdf_source_details` query at `:2328-2333`, and `:2352-2353` refuses any build the details table does not mention, so a build whose link was deleted by reclamation (`:1197-1221`) or a terminal build never linked is unreachable by construction; the per-run accumulation was then read off `index_lifecycle.py:291` (a build per sidecar per run) against `pdf_pipeline.py:211-215` (each failure committed as a FAILED build), which is the folded candidate `R9-L08-1`'s live residue: one fresh BUILDING→FAILED build-and-batch set per run per PDF plus a run-wide `SOURCE_INDEXING_FAILED`. A row-count proof needs an index run against a real ledger and was not executed.
- Acceptance criteria: an identity-keyed cleanup that retires terminal builds whose published source sha matches the reclaimed copy without depending on `pdf_source_details` (a sweep beside `_retire_pdf_content_builds` in `apps/knowledge/app/database.py`), pinned by a lane that runs two incremental index passes over one PDF and asserts no build-and-batch residue survives and no READY orphan is created — red when the new sweep is removed.
- Related findings: carries the merged candidate `R9-L08-1` (same re-derivation residue; its orphan-READY premise is false because `database.py:1177` returns `ALREADY_RECLAIMED`, and it cannot exist at all while extract_range dies at `pdf_runtime.py:323`) · F-437 is what makes the terminal rows accumulate today · F-444 is the same owner decision defeated by re-keying · closed rows F-093, F-389, F-390 and F-391 are different claims and none names enumeration blindness · nguồn: R9 T06 — knowledge index / PDF retention lane

### F-445 — the runtime schema assertion forbids the only build-identity constraint shape that could make reuse retention-safe, so PDF build accumulation is schema-mandated and the natural fix trips it

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-T06-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Knowledgebase
- Severity: Medium
- Status: REFUTED — premise measured false at HEAD: the assertion at `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:687-707` is a token test that a terminal-inclusive PARTIAL unique index PASSES — the probe reported "retention-safe partial index -> PASS", "HEAD shape -> PASS" and "unconditional UNIQUE -> invalid" — so accumulation does not follow from the schema but from the query filter at `database.py:793`, and the partial shape is a pinned feature at `apps/knowledge/tests/test_pdf_migration_contract.py:70-76`
- Root cause: the candidate read the assertion's `required` predicate (`:687-694`, which asks only that some `pg_indexes` definition contains UNIQUE INDEX, CONTENT_IDENTITY_DIGEST, WHERE, BUILDING and READY) as a prohibition on any stronger or terminal-inclusive constraint, but the raise at `:706-707` fires only when that partial index is missing or a `pg_constraint` UNIQUE naming `content_identity_digest` exists (`:702-705`); nothing blocks a retention-aware index or an application-side identity claim. The filed F5 half (data-integrity correctness) likewise has no surviving claim, since the accumulation is an enumeration problem, which F-443 files as F3.
- Evidence: Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:687-694` — the `required` predicate, satisfied by any partial UNIQUE index naming BUILDING and READY · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:702-707` — the only disqualifier, a `pg_constraint` UNIQUE on `content_identity_digest`, raising `PDF_CONTENT_IDENTITY_SCHEMA_INVALID` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:793` — `WHERE content_identity_digest = $1 AND status IN ('BUILDING', 'READY')`, the actual source of terminal-row accumulation · Where: `apps/knowledge/tests/test_pdf_migration_contract.py:70-76` — `test_failed_identity_can_retry_without_an_unconditional_unique_constraint` asserts the partial shape is present and `UNIQUE (content_identity_digest)` absent · Where: `apps/knowledge/migrations/002_pdf_ingestion_foundation.sql:20-26` — the defended partial index shape the assertion protects
- Reproduction: Measured by evaluating the assertion's own predicate against three schemas: a terminal-inclusive retention-safe PARTIAL UNIQUE index -> PASS, the HEAD shape -> PASS, an unconditional UNIQUE on `content_identity_digest` -> invalid. No shape the candidate called "the natural fix" is rejected, so no accumulation is schema-mandated and there is nothing left to re-run; the row stays only so a later band does not re-file this premise.
- Acceptance criteria: No code change is licensed by this row: the refutation is recorded so no later band re-files the schema-blocking premise, and the retention work that actually survives is F-443's identity-keyed enumeration sweep — reopening this row requires a measured case where `database.py:687-707` raises against a constraint shape that would have made reuse retention-safe.

### F-446 — four provider adapters still store a credential they never read while the no-retention rule is asserted for DagsHub only

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R9-L07a-1`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow contracts), Gates + CI
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the four constructors assign an API-token/API-key-shaped option straight onto the instance (`zink-tools/adapters/mlops/huggingface-adapter.mjs:26` `this.apiToken`, `zink-tools/adapters/compute/databricks-adapter.mjs:29` `this.token`, `zink-tools/adapters/compute/kaggle-adapter.mjs:29` `this.key`, `zink-tools/adapters/analytics/motherduck-adapter.mjs:34` `this.token`) and nothing in their methods reads it, while `tests/provider-adapter-guards.test.mjs:172-181` asserts the rule for a `DagsHubAdapter` instance only.
- Root cause: the F-277 remediation was written as a single-adapter assertion instead of a roster, so the same "constructor retains a credential the class never uses" pattern survived in the four adapters the guard does not cover; the retained field makes every caller that passes a credential believe the adapter authenticates with it, and keeps the value alive in process memory and in any serialization of the instance for a field that has no reader.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-tools/adapters/mlops/huggingface-adapter.mjs:24-27` — the constructor stores `options.apiToken`, while `evaluateCandidateModel` (`:41-94`) never references the field · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/compute/databricks-adapter.mjs:29`, `zink-tools/adapters/compute/kaggle-adapter.mjs:29`, `zink-tools/adapters/analytics/motherduck-adapter.mjs:34` — the same unread credential field, grep-measured as the only four `this.<credential> = options…` assignments under `zink-tools/adapters/` · Where: `tests/provider-adapter-guards.test.mjs:172-181` — the assertion is `Object.hasOwn(adapter, "token") === false` against `new DagsHubAdapter({...})` alone; probe measured `Object.hasOwn` true for all four named fields and false for the DagsHub instance · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/mlops/dagshub-adapter.mjs:24-37` — the class that records why it holds nothing, the standard the other four miss.
- Reproduction: Read the four constructor lines and the DagsHub test at HEAD, then probe each class: instantiate each adapter with a credential option and evaluate `Object.hasOwn(instance, "<field>")` — measured true for `apiToken`, `token` (Databricks), `key`, `token` (MotherDuck) and false for the DagsHub token field, so the existing guard cannot see the other four. Severity is capped at Low because no real secret is written at HEAD: the only importer of the adapter barrel constructs every adapter with no arguments (`tests/m7-t4-orchestration.test.mjs:60-69`), so no credential value ever reaches these fields in the shipped tree.
- Acceptance criteria: `Object.hasOwn(instance, "<credentialField>") === false` (or removal of the field) holds for the HuggingFace, Databricks, Kaggle and MotherDuck adapters in `tests/provider-adapter-guards.test.mjs`, one case per adapter in the style of the existing DagsHub case at `:172-181`, and the assertion turns red if any constructor line is restored.
- Related findings: sibling row `F-449` covers the same roster gap on the fidelity side · nguồn: R9 L07a — zink-tools provider adapters and shared safety helpers

### F-447 — the logical-path validator accepts a leading- or trailing-dot segment, so the storage adapter's "no second spelling can address this object" invariant is false

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R9-L07a-2`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow contracts), Zink Skill Store
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `zink-tools/common/path-safety.mjs:3` allows a dot inside the segment class and `:44-47` rejects only empty, `.` and `..`, so `orders.` validates and the landing key at `zink-tools/adapters/storage/r2-adapter.mjs:140` becomes `landing/orders.`, measured as a distinct Map key from `landing/orders` — the claim written at `:136-137` ("no second spelling can address this object") is therefore false at HEAD.
- Root cause: the segment grammar treats `.` as an ordinary character rather than as a normaliser-visible alias, and the validator normalises separators but not dot padding, so two logical ids that a case-insensitive or trailing-dot-folding filesystem treats as one object produce two keys in the adapter's own bookkeeping; the immutability check then guards one spelling while the other addresses the same stored object once the store is real.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-tools/common/path-safety.mjs:3` — `const SAFE_SEGMENT_PATTERN = /^[a-zA-Z0-9_.-]+$/u;` · Where: `zink-tools/common/path-safety.mjs:44-47` — the loop throws only for `segment.length === 0`, `"."`, `".."` · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/storage/r2-adapter.mjs:136-140` — the F-052/F-171 comment asserting a single spelling, then ``const key = `landing/${artifact_id}`;`` and the`this.storage.has(key)`immutability test at`:141-145`· Where:`zink-tools/adapters/storage/r2-adapter.mjs:108`—`this.storage = new Map()`, i.e. the HEAD double is in-process · Measured: `validateArtifactLogicalPath("landing/orders.")`returns normally and`landing/orders.`is stored as a key distinct from`landing/orders` in the Map.
- Reproduction: Probe the validator and the adapter double at HEAD: call `validateArtifactLogicalPath("orders.")` (passes), then put a landing artifact with `artifact_id: "orders"` and one with `artifact_id: "orders."` and read the Map keys — measured two distinct keys, so the immutability violation is bypassed by the dot spelling. Two halves of the round-1 claim were refuted and are not part of this row: the "`:63` returns the input verbatim" hop is already fixed at HEAD (`:60-63` returns `segments.join("/")`), and an actual object collision needs a Windows- or provider-keyed store, which HEAD does not have (`:108`), so the surviving damage at HEAD is the false adapter invariant plus the real-store exposure.
- Acceptance criteria: `validateArtifactLogicalPath` (`zink-tools/common/path-safety.mjs:12-64`) rejects any segment that begins or ends with `.`, and a test in `tests/` asserts that `orders.` and `orders` are not both admissible ids so `R2StorageAdapter.putLandingArtifact` raises `R2_LANDING_IMMUTABLE_VIOLATION` on the second put; removing the dot check turns that assertion red.
- Related findings: `F-052` and `F-171` are the earlier key-assembly rows, and the register shows `F-171` as REFUTED for traversal/separator only (`docs/execution/findings.md:350`, `:356`), which is why this dot-alias case was never closed · nguồn: R9 L07a — zink-tools provider adapters and shared safety helpers

### F-448 — the skill adapter forwards a raw filesystem error message into the governed envelope, so an absolute operator home path reaches the model-facing sink

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R9-L07a-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Skill Store, OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `zink-tools/adapters/skill-store/skill-adapter.mjs:1146-1149` interpolates `${error?.message}` into the failure text and `:1169-1177` returns `message: error.message` for any non-adapter error, so a Node fs error's absolute path leaves the adapter on every failure the persistence branch hits.
- Root cause: the F-145 rule — fixed canonical failure sentences, details to a trusted sink — was applied to the knowledge adapter but not to the skill adapter, which instead re-serialises whatever the write helper threw; Node's `ENOENT`/`EACCES` messages embed the full absolute path they tried to open, so the envelope carries operator-filesystem topology into text that the plugin hands to the model.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-tools/adapters/skill-store/skill-adapter.mjs:1144-1149` — the catch around proposal persistence builds `Skill proposal persistence failed: ${error?.message}` with code `SKILL_PROPOSAL_PERSIST_FAILED`, the message coming from `writeJsonAtomicSync`/`syncDirectory` (`:261-298`) · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/skill-store/skill-adapter.mjs:1169-1177` — the outer catch returns `governed_status: "CONTROLLED_FAILURE"` with `message: error.message` verbatim · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/knowledge/knowledge-adapter.mjs:774-781` — the sibling path deliberately sends a fixed sentence and cites the F-145/R1-A14-08 reason (raw upstream text must not reach caller, model, cards) · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:5218-5227` — the model-facing sink that renders the returned message.
- Reproduction: Drive the adapter's propose branch against a state root whose target file cannot be opened (fault-injected scratch root, no live state touched) and capture the returned envelope — measured `{"error":"SKILL_ADAPTER_ERROR","message":"ENOENT: no such file or directory, open '<absolute operator-home path>'"}`, i.e. the failure text carries a full absolute path from the operator's home directory rather than the fixed sentence the knowledge adapter emits for the same class of error.
- Acceptance criteria: every non-`SkillAdapterError` exit in `skill-adapter.mjs` (`:1144-1149`, `:1169-1177`) emits a fixed canonical message per error code, matching the `knowledge-adapter.mjs:774-781` pattern, with the raw error text sent only to a trusted log sink; a test asserts the returned `message` contains no `/` or drive-letter path and fails if the interpolation is restored.
- Related findings: `F-158` is the register's REFUTED sibling claim in a different file (`docs/execution/findings.md:311`, `:317`), so it does not cover this adapter · nguồn: R9 L07a — zink-tools provider adapters and shared safety helpers
