### F-454 — the Python deny gate has no production caller and is escaped by library spellings while comment text is refused

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R9-L07b-5`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (SQL/n8n/Python guards), OpenClaw governance plugin, Gates + CI
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `zink_python` is registered with `status: "callable_interface"` (`integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:2414-2417`), not `schema_only`, so the `AUTH_POLICY_DENIED` branch at `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/guard.mjs:1864-1869` does not stop it and the governed call proceeds; `assertNoPythonViolations` (`zink-tools/python/python-validator.mjs:217-223`) has zero production callers, and the live `.py` validation (`integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs:710-741`) computes counts and runs `py_compile` with no deny rule at all.
- Root cause: the deny rules were added to the summariser module and its test, but the artifact path re-implemented Python validation without referencing that module, so the invariant the module documents — "This is the refusal the governed path calls" at `:210-211` — is simply not true of HEAD; meanwhile the rules match on substrings rather than call sites, so a comment or docstring containing `subprocess`/`exec()` is refused while the reachable library spellings that perform the same effect pass.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-tools/python/python-validator.mjs:72-98` — the five deny rules (`exec`, `eval`, `os.system`, `subprocess`, `open`) · Where: `zink-tools/python/python-validator.mjs:15-53` — the benign tag sets that never refuse · Where: `zink-tools/python/python-validator.mjs:207-223` — the docstring claim at `:210-211` and the exported `assertNoPythonViolations` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs:710-741` — the python branch: empty check, line/function/class/import counts, then `validatePythonSyntax`; grep measures no import of the validator module here · Where: grep measurement of `assertNoPythonViolations` across the repo (excluding `temp/`): definition plus `tests/python-and-artifact-guards.test.mjs:8,53,57` only, i.e. 0 production callers · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:2414-2417` and `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/guard.mjs:1864-1869` — the `callable_interface` status that keeps the tool dispatchable.
- Reproduction: Probe the deny rules with the declared threat classes and with benign text — measured: `os.popen("id")`, `pandas.read_csv('/etc/passwd')` and `duckdb.sql("SELECT * FROM read_csv('/etc/passwd')")` all PASS `findPythonViolations`, while `# do not use subprocess here` and `"""never calls exec()"""` and `with open('data.csv') as f:` are REFUSED; then confirm the governed path never calls the refusal at all (grep above), so the false refusals only bite wherever the module is used and the passes are the live result.
- Acceptance criteria: either `assertNoPythonViolations` is wired into the production `.py` path (`artifact-validator.mjs:710-741`) and its rules cover the library spellings that perform the declared effect (`os.popen`, `pandas.read_csv`, `duckdb.sql`/`read_csv` targets) while ignoring comment and docstring text, or the published invariant at `python-validator.mjs:207-216` and the tool contract are narrowed to say the module is advisory only; a test pairing the three escaping spellings (must refuse) with the three benign texts (must pass) turns red if either half regresses.
- Related findings: `R7-D5-02` (`CHANGELOG.md:1286`) fixed only the "no deny rule at all" half, leaving the caller and escape surface unreported · `F-452` and `F-453` are the same mask-vs-raw-text defect class on the SQL side · nguồn: R9 L07b — zink-tools SQL/n8n/Python guard and contract lane

### F-456 — the claim that guard-side contract enforcement has zero live call sites and the registered dispatcher enforces none, measured false because `guard.beforeToolCall` is dispatched in production and holds both `validateContractSchema` sites

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R9-T07-2`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: Ungraded
- Status: REFUTED — premise measured false at HEAD: `guard.beforeToolCall` IS dispatched in production (`integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:8202`, `:8580`, `:8861`, `:8923`, plus `beforeRun` `:7940` and `beforeRunEnd` `:9117`), and both `validateContractSchema` sites live inside that function, so "the only production dispatcher enforces none / guard-side enforcement has zero live call sites" is false; only the plugin's OWN `validateContractSchema` call count is 0
- Root cause: the round-2 premise conflated "the registered handler contains no schema check" with "nothing enforces the contract"; measurement shows `guard.mjs:1892` (tool-call) and `guard.mjs:2282` (tool-result) both sit inside `beforeToolCall` (method span :1518→2304), which the plugin calls on the canonical envelope built from the model's own `liveInput` (`index.mjs:8058`→`:8168`→`:8202`), and the `before_tool_call` lease binds dispatched params by digest (`:8215-8228` vs `:9200-9227`). Only a narrow defence-in-depth residue survives: the registered handler itself contains no schema check, so envelope enforcement depends on the host invoking the hook.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/guard.mjs:1518` — `async beforeToolCall(...)`; its only two `validateContractSchema` sites are :1892 `"tool-call", event.params, TOOL_CALL_ENVELOPE_MAX_BYTES` and :2282 `"tool-result", rawResult`, next sibling method `beforeAnnounce` at :2304 · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:8202` — `const canonicalDecision = await guard.beforeToolCall(canonicalEvent, context, signal);` with further live sites :8580, :8861, :8923 · Where: `index.mjs:8058`/`:8168` — `liveInput` derived from `rawInput` and fed as `input` into the canonical event · Where: `index.mjs:8215-8228` and `:9200-9227` — lease set after `allow:true` and `POLICY_DENIED:LEASE_DIGEST_MISMATCH` on the dispatched params · Measured: plugin `grep -c validateContractSchema` = 0 while `grep -n beforeToolCall index.mjs` returns the four dispatch sites above
- Reproduction: node method-map walk of guard.mjs proving :1892/:2282 lie inside beforeToolCall (1518→2304), plus grep of index.mjs counting the four live `guard.beforeToolCall` call sites; the plugin's own validator count is 0 but the guard on that seam validates the canonical envelope before `allow:true`, so a contract-invalid or substituted envelope cannot reach the dispatcher (the surviving residue is only that the registered handler adds no second schema check)
- Acceptance criteria: no code change is licensed by this row; it records the refutation so no later band re-files "guard-side enforcement has zero live call sites", and the only forward-looking concern is the defence-in-depth observation that the registered handler relies on the host invoking `beforeToolCall`/`before_tool_call`
- Related findings: refutes the round-2 central premise per V08; the live-dispatch fact is the same seam cited as the durability target in `R9-T08-3` (`F-461`) · nguồn: R9 T07 — published contract vs executable code

### F-459 — two SQL scanners with opposite literal/comment semantics disagree in both directions, the artifact scanner refusing benign data inside a string literal while accepting a literal the canonical lexer forbids

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R9-T08-1`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (SQL/n8n/Python guards), OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the artifact SQL scanner runs on the live result-artifact gate (`validateTechnicalArtifact` → `evaluateResultGate` → `index.mjs:3007`) and its raw-text regex disagrees with the canonical validator's tokenizer (verdict downgraded High→Medium because impact fails closed)
- Root cause: `artifact-validator.mjs` scans raw `content` with a `UNSAFE_SQL` regex and a hand-rolled backslash-parity single-quote counter that never skips `--`/`/*`, while the canonical `validator.mjs` lexer rejects comments and backslash-in-literal outright; the two disagree in both directions and the canonical tokenizer already exists but is never imported by the artifact validator (imports only node builtins).
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs:662-663` `const UNSAFE_SQL = /\b(DROP\s+DATABASE|DROP\s+SCHEMA|SHUTDOWN|…)\b/i` applied to raw `content` at :664, `:669` sets `sqlSafety = false`; `:676-688` hand-rolled `if (ch === "'" && (i === 0 || content[i-1] !== "\\"))` backslash-parity scanner, no `--`/`/*` skipping; `:1-17` imports only node:fs/child_process/crypto/path; live surface `:395` `validateTechnicalArtifact` called at `:1381` inside `:1363` `evaluateResultGate`, imported by the plugin at `:186`/`:3007` · Where: `zink-tools/sql/validator.mjs:144-150` rejects `--` comments, `:153-158` forbids backslash escaping, `:160-166` doubled `''` handling · Measured: replication matrix — `"SELECT 'DROP DATABASE' AS note FROM t"` → artifact `[UNSAFE_SQL_STATEMENT]` vs canonical `ACCEPT`; `"SELECT 'a\\b' FROM t"` → artifact `[]` (pass) vs canonical `REFUSE: SQL_UNSUPPORTED_SYNTAX`; `"-- don't …\nSELECT …"` → BOTH refuse · site added by R10 (V11, from R10-T08-3): Where: `zink-skill-store/migrations/004_skill_retirement_state.sql:5` — an apostrophe inside a `--` comment, run through both FILE guards on identical bytes: `validateSqlFileContent` accepts with `statementCount 7` and `mutationKeywords [DROP, ALTER]` while `validateTechnicalArtifact` returns `status FAIL` with `MALFORMED_SQL_SYNTAX` and `checks.sqlSyntax: false` · Measured: this row's refuted half holds only for the canonical QUERY path (`zink-tools/sql/validator.mjs:144-150` rejects `--` comments) and not for the canonical FILE path, which strips comments; `validateSqlFileContent` itself has 0 production callers (`git grep` = 1 definition + `tests/m7-lane-h4-sql-safety-matrix.test.mjs` + `tests/m8-t2-governed-runtime-audit.test.mjs`), so the opposite verdicts are real but fail closed and severity stays this row's Medium
- Reproduction: node replication of the artifact scanner plus a canonical `assertReadOnlyStatement` call on identical strings → the first two inputs give opposite verdicts in both directions; the refuted half: the candidate's chosen `-- don't drop anything` fixture is refused by BOTH scanners (canonical rejects `--` at :145-147) so it does not evidence opposite verdicts, "read the same artifact" is actually query-vs-file not one shared input, and the implied `'it''s'` doubled-quote case balances by parity (`[]`)
- Acceptance criteria: route both the balance check and the keyword decision through the canonical `tokenizeSql`/one exported mask so the artifact scanner and `validator.mjs` share a tokenizer, with a regression that turns red when the raw `UNSAFE_SQL` regex or the hand-rolled quote counter is restored
- Related findings: folded candidate R9-T08-1 is the surviving disagreement of the broader chain in legacy `R9-L07b-3`/`R9-L07b-4`; 0 register hits for `MALFORMED_SQL_SYNTAX`/`UNSAFE_SQL` · nguồn: R9 T08 — guards that over- and under-reject

### F-460 — the same refusal is model-visible as a raw internal code on the tool-result surface and flattened to one fixed sentence on the native surface, while the durable refusal record the boundary docblock promises has no event type in the ledger contract and no writer in the guard

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R9-T08-2`

- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: both surfaces are live, the SQL handler path (`index.mjs:5067`) and the result-artifact gate (`:3007`), and neither emits a durable refusal record
- Root cause: the tool-result path forwards the raw internal `err.code` to the model and discards the reason, the native-gate path discards the canonical code for one fixed `POLICY_REFUSAL_TEXT`, and the boundary docblock claims the code "stays in the ledger" although `ledger-event.schema.json` declares no refusal event and `guard.mjs` writes no refusal record at all.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:5067` `error: err?.code || "DuckDB_EXECUTION_ERROR"`, `:5068` `message: "Governed SQL execution failed closed."` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/native-boundary.mjs:165-169` `console.error(... blocked:", canonical)` then `return { block: true, reason: POLICY_REFUSAL_TEXT }`; `:23` `POLICY_REFUSAL_TEXT = "Action not permitted by workspace policy."`; `:250-258` selects the canonical code then routes it only to `refuse`; docblock `:16-21`/`:155-164` claims the code "stays in the diagnostic channel and in the ledger" · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/contracts/ledger-event.schema.json` — 26-member event enum; the `REFUS|BLOCK|DENI` filter returns only `WORKFLOW_USER_RESULT_BLOCKED` (`:47`, const `:855`) whose description (`:863`) says "This is NOT an execution or reconciliation event" · Where: `guard.mjs:447-448` `function fail(code){ throw new Error(code); }`, grep `appendLedger|recordEvent|writeLedger|EVENT_TYPE` = 0 hits · Measured: node enum walk → `event enum size: 26 | refusal-named events: ["WORKFLOW_USER_RESULT_BLOCKED"]`
- Reproduction: node walk of the ledger-event enum counting refusal-named events and grep locating `index.mjs:5067` and `native-boundary.mjs:169` exactly as cited, establishing that the only refusal-named event is an owner verdict and the guard has no ledger writer
- Acceptance criteria: one refusal contract across both surfaces — either a typed durable refusal event (code + guard id, no user data) added to `ledger-event.schema.json` and written by the guard, or the "stays in the ledger" docblock claims removed; the tool-result path stops emitting raw internal codes while keeping a non-sensitive discriminator, with a regression that turns red when the raw `err.code` is exposed or the ledger claim is reasserted without a writer
- Related findings: keep as sibling to `R9-T09-1` (do not merge); F-463/`R9-L07a-3` are free-text outbound leaks, a different decision; no prior row pairs the two refusal surfaces · nguồn: R9 T08 — guards that over- and under-reject

### F-461 — approval consumption is durable and one-way with no release path, so for the operations that do require approval any post-consumption refusal, including the guard's own post-dispatch validation, permanently burns the approval

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R9-T08-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — needs fault injection: the surviving burn only fires for approval-requiring ops (zink_excel populate/generate, n8n trigger) and was not exercised live because that would write to a state root, which the audit forbids; settled by branch-predicate measurement (verdict downgraded High→Low because the SQL class it was filed against never reaches this path)
- Root cause: `bindInvocationApproval` durably writes to `consumed-approvals.json` under a file lock inside `beforeToolCall`, and no release/unconsume path exists (`grep releaseApproval|unconsume` in guard.mjs = 0 hits), so any refusal after consumption — including the guard's own post-dispatch validation at `guard.mjs:2282` — consumes the owner's approval with no recovery.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/guard.mjs:2167-2185` `if (opPolicy.requiresApproval === true) { approvalExpiryMs = bindInvocationApproval({...}) }` inside `beforeToolCall` (:1518); `:594` `consumeApprovalAtomically(...)` inside `:527` `bindInvocationApproval`; `:827` `consumeApprovalAtomically` → `:857-883` durable write `consumedFile = path.join(paths.root,"consumed-approvals.json")` under `withFileLockSync`, `consumed[approvalId] = { consumed_at, claimScope }` (:877-882) · Where: `guard.mjs:2144-2149` — `operationName` hardcoded to `query` for `zink_sql` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:2341-2343` `zink_sql: { query: { requiresApproval: false } }`; approval-requiring ops at :2323/:2331/:2416-region · Where: `index.mjs:8215-8228` returns `{ allow: true }` after the guard authorized, `:5049-5052` dispatch, `:5057-5076` catch → BLOCKED envelope with no compensating release
- Reproduction: not attempted as a live burn (would write a state root — forbidden); settled by measuring the branch predicates above — the refuted half: the candidate's headline pairing with the over-rejecting content guards is false, because `zink_sql`'s operation is hardcoded to `query` with `requiresApproval:false`, so the TABLE_FUNCTION/HOST_EXECUTION/FILESYSTEM false positives measured under `R9-T08-1` never touch an approval and the claimed mechanism is unreachable for the class it was filed against
- Acceptance criteria: either run content guards before consumption, or release/re-arm on any post-consumption refusal, or emit a durable refusal event naming the consumed approval id so it can be reconciled — checked against the approval-requiring operations and turning red when a post-consumption refusal leaves the approval consumed with no release
- Related findings: durability measured against the live seam `index.mjs:8202` (dispatch of the guard that `R9-T07-2`/`F-456` confirms is production); shares the missing durable-refusal-event remedy with `R9-T08-2`/`F-460`; 0 register hits for `consumed-approvals` · nguồn: R9 T08 — guards that over- and under-reject

### F-462 — the published zink_python safety invariant has no enforcement point on any live path and the orphaned deny set, if wired as written, would refuse `open()`/docstring/comment text while accepting `os.popen` and `read_csv`

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R9-T08-4`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (SQL/n8n/Python guards), OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Medium
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: the live `zink_python` handler executes nothing (`ENGINE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`), so no governed call ever reads or runs a `.py` body and the absent content gate has no reachable victim, while the over-refusal deny rules run only inside two test files (verdict downgraded High→Medium)
- Root cause: the published safety invariant points at `python-validator.mjs`, which has zero production importers, and the production `.py` gate (`artifact-validator.mjs`) is syntax-plus-counts with no deny rule, so nothing enforces the declared class on any live path; the orphaned deny set itself is per-line raw-text regex with no comment/string state, so it both over-refuses benign text and under-refuses real host-execution by library spelling.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-tools/python/python-validator.mjs:72-98` `PYTHON_DENY_RULES` (patterns `/(^|[^.\w])exec\s*\(/`, `/(^|[^.\w])subprocess\b/`, `/(^|[^.\w])open\s*\(/`), `:108-119` per-line `content.split(/\r?\n/)` loop with no comment/string state, `:217` `assertNoPythonViolations` · Where: importers = `tests/m8-t2-governed-runtime-audit.test.mjs:53` and `tests/python-and-artifact-guards.test.mjs:10` — 2 test, 0 production · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:4951-4974` `execute(_id, params)` → `error: "ENGINE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED", tool: "zink_python", implemented: false, message: "No governed Python sandbox is implemented. No script was executed."` (grep confirmed at `:4962`) · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:2414-2417` `zink_python: { status: "callable_interface" }` so `guard.mjs:1865-1868`'s `schema_only` check does not stop the call · Where: `artifact-validator.mjs:709-741` production `.py` gate = EMPTY_PYTHON + lineCount/functionCount/classCount/importCount + `validatePythonSyntax`, zero safety deny rules · Measured: findPythonViolations → comment `["PYTHON_SUBPROCESS"]`, docstring `["PYTHON_EXEC"]`, openFile `["PYTHON_OPEN"]`, `popen` `[]`, `duckRead` `[]`
- Reproduction: `node -e findPythonViolations(...)` matrix proving over-refusal (comment/docstring/`open()` refused) and under-rejection (`os.popen`, `read_csv` accepted); the mechanism is proven but the live impact is not reachable because the handler executes nothing — the blocker is the `ENGINE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` stub at `index.mjs:4951-4974`; the candidate's `schema_only` flag was corrected, `policy.mjs` gives `callable_interface`
- Acceptance criteria: before the sandbox lands, either narrow the published zink_python safety invariant to match what is actually enforced or wire one tokenizing content gate on the live path that covers the declared class and exempts `open()` inside the sandbox root — a regression that turns red when a `subprocess`/`exec` body reaches the stub-free handler or when the orphaned per-line regex refuses a comment
- Related findings: published-invariant view of the same module as legacy `R9-L07b-5` (the Python deny gate, cited by legacy id because that row is not yet on disk); `R7-D5-02`/`F-236` closed "no deny rule at all" only in the orphaned module, and the orphan is still orphaned at HEAD · nguồn: R9 T08 — guards that over- and under-reject

### F-463 — governed plan-step fields render into Discord approval and result bodies through clipField, which applies no BANNED-PATTERN redaction, so paths, session keys and tokens survive into the card body

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R9-L05-1`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: a governed plan field is rendered on every approval card and every result card, so hostile or incidental internal identifiers in plan text reach a Discord-visible body in the live path.
- Root cause: clipField only trims and slices (discord-formatter.mjs:638-642) and the renderers join raw node inputs/constraints/validation_criteria into the body (:667-671, approval :733-735, result :783-785); the plugin's only guard on these bodies is sanitizeApprovalBody (index.mjs:1898-1905), which strips just DISCORD_MENTION_TOKENS (:1891-1896) and never the formatter's BANNED_PATTERNS (:11-30), so a filesystem path, a session key, a bearer-style token or a 64-hex digest survives into the body — and the result body (:6374-6385, no wrapper) and the forum-tag content (discord-forum-tags.mjs:825-826) bypass even the mention strip.
- Evidence: Where: integrations/openclaw/lib/discord-formatter.mjs:638-642 — clipField value.trim()+slice only, no redaction · :667-671 joins node.inputs/constraints/validation_criteria via clipField only · :733-735,:783-785 approval/result body = planSteps.join · contrast :935-937 formatGovernedResultCard DOES call sanitizeDiscordText · BANNED_PATTERNS :11-30 cover the path/session/token/64-hex classes · Where: integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:1898-1905 sanitizeApprovalBody strips only DISCORD_MENTION_TOKENS (:1891-1896) · :1983 approval body wrapped · :6374-6385 result body NOT wrapped · Where: integrations/openclaw/lib/discord-forum-tags.mjs:825-826 content sliced, no allowed_mentions · Measured: hostile plan-field probe — a filesystem path, a session key, a bearer-style token, a 64-hex digest and an @everyone mention all survive into both approval and result bodies.
- Reproduction: render a governed plan whose step inputs/constraints carry sentinel identifiers through renderGovernedPlanSteps into the approval and result bodies; measured in-memory that the raw path/session-key/token/64-hex classes persist into BOTH bodies, while only the @everyone/<@id> mention tokens are removed from the approval body (sanitizeApprovalBody) and nothing is removed from the result body or forum-tag content.
- Acceptance criteria: every free-text plan field in the four Discord renderers passes through the formatter's BANNED-PATTERN redactor before joining into a body, and the result body/forum content additionally pass the mention strip, so a shipped case rendering a sentinel path/session key/token/64-hex/@everyone yields none of them in the approval or result body.
- Related findings: kept distinct from F-470 (the tool-result envelope sink) and F-471 (raw text at rest), which share the same no-redaction boundary RC-T09-1 but are render sinks vs dispatcher vs store · nguồn: R9 L05 — artifact/presentation truth

### F-468 — expected-result PNGs are written to a shared "main" sandbox under a target-keyed filename, so a second same-target task rendering between another task's render and its deferred upload makes the first card attach the wrong image

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R9-L05-7`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable, conditional: fires only when two in-flight tasks for the same targetAgentId overlap the render→await→upload window.
- Root cause: discord-result-preview.mjs writes path.join(normalized, expected_result_${sanitizeTarget(target)}.png) (:146-151), keyed only on target, into the shared resolveAgentSandboxDir("main",...) root, and the card's sendNativePayload({mediaUrl: previewPath}) (index.mjs:8508-8518) runs after the thread-post await (:8490), so a second same-target render overwrites the first task's PNG before that task reads it, with no task/attempt scope in the filename.
- Evidence: Where: integrations/openclaw/lib/discord-result-preview.mjs:146-151 — path.join(normalized, `expected_result_${sanitizeTarget(target)}.png`); writeFileSync(filePath,png) (overwritten) · Where: integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:8357 — previewRoot=resolveAgentSandboxDir("main",…) shared · :8387-8394 render keyed on targetAgentId · :8508-8518 sendNativePayload({… mediaUrl: previewPath}) after the thread-post await at :8490 — no task/attempt scope in the filename · Measured: mechanism proven by shared root + target-only key + write-before-await-upload.
- Reproduction: mechanism traced directly (a concurrent same-target host was not exercised): the preview file lives in a shared "main" sandbox under a target-only name and mediaUrl is resolved after a deferred await, so two overlapping same-targetAgentId renders in the render→await→upload window collide and one card attaches the other's image.
- Acceptance criteria: the preview filename carries task/attempt scope or a content hash, so a test running two same-target renders keeps distinct files and each card's mediaUrl resolves to its own image rather than the last writer's.
- Related findings: shares RC-T12-3 (side-effect evidence keyed without attempt/task scope); kept separate — this is identifier scoping, not a byte budget · nguồn: R9 L05 — artifact/presentation truth

### F-469 — the technical evidenceDigest is stamped before the artifact-set path-security mutation, so a FAIL(artifactSetPathSecurity) result ships a stale PASS-era digest that omits the added check and error

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R9-L05-8`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable, conditional: fires only when the primary technical PASSes AND the ordered set has >1 member AND a secondary is non-absolute/UNC (:1386-1396).
- Root cause: stampProvenanceAndDigest computes evidenceDigest over status:sortedChecks:errors.length (artifact-validator.mjs:290-293) and is the return of validateTechnicalArtifact (:941), but the caller then mutates technical.status=FAIL, checks.artifactSetPathSecurity=false and pushes the INVALID_PATH_SECONDARY_ARTIFACT error (:1396-1404) and returns the same object (:1432), so the digest no longer commits to its own evidence.
- Evidence: Where: integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs:290-293 — evidenceDigest=sha256 over `${status}:${sortedChecks}:${errors.length}` inside stampProvenanceAndDigest · :941 — stampProvenanceAndDigest is the return of validateTechnicalArtifact · :1396-1404 — sets technical.status=FAIL, checks.artifactSetPathSecurity=false and pushes the path error AFTER the digest was stamped · :1432 — returns the same technical object carrying the pre-mutation digest · Measured: a FAIL verdict produced at :1397 carries an evidenceDigest built from the pre-mutation PASS status/checks/errors.
- Reproduction: directly provable from code order — a delivered set whose primary PASSes with >1 member and a non-absolute/UNC secondary trips the :1397 mutation after the :941 stamp, leaving evidenceDigest bound to the pre-mutation PASS state; the conditional reachability matches the round-2 flag.
- Acceptance criteria: the artifact-set verdict (status/checks/errors) is finalized before stampProvenanceAndDigest, or the digest is recomputed after the :1396-1404 mutation, so a FAIL(artifactSetPathSecurity) result's evidenceDigest commits to the added check and error; a test must assert the digest changes when the path-security arm fires.
- Related findings: the opposite ordering error to F-464 (stamp-before-mutation vs cost-before-bound), kept separate per round-2 · nguồn: R9 L05 — artifact/presentation truth

### F-470 — the live plugin tool dispatcher forwards raw error.message and echoes full tool output into the model-facing tool envelope, so internal failure text and unredacted output reach model context while the repo's own convention is symbolic codes

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R9-T09-1`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the registered tool handler's execute-catch and output-schema paths are the live dispatch sink, so any thrown error's raw message and any non-conforming tool output serialize into the envelope returned to the model.
- Root cause: the execute-catch returns the model-facing envelope with error: err?.message || String(err) (index.mjs:5225) and the param-catch errors:[paramErr?.message||String(paramErr)] (:5185), while output-schema failures echo the whole rawOutput (:5280) and pass parsedOutput into blocked() (:5305); the SQL sibling (:5058-5072) returns a fixed message plus a symbolic code, demonstrating the repo's own convention the dispatcher violates — so raw failure text (a class that can embed absolute filesystem paths and internal identifiers) and unredacted tool output leak into model context.
- Evidence: Where: integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:5217-5231 — execute-catch returns the model-facing envelope with error: err?.message || String(err) at :5225 (the sink) · :5182-5186 — param-catch errors:[paramErr?.message || String(paramErr)] · :5276-5280 — echo of full rawOutput on INVALID_OUTPUT_SCHEMA · :5302-5305 — blocked([outErr?.message || String(outErr)], parsedOutput) echoes parsed output · :5056 — echoes JSON.stringify(result) · contrast :5058-5072 — SQL catch returns fixed "Governed SQL execution failed closed." + symbolic code · Measured: string-forwarding provable by reading the catch bodies; the SQL sibling shows the fixed-message convention the dispatcher omits.
- Reproduction: read the dispatcher's catch paths — any thrown error's raw .message and any non-conforming tool output are serialized into the content[0].text envelope returned to the model, while the SQL handler at :5058-5072 emits a fixed message plus a symbolic code; the string-forwarding is directly provable and no live run was required.
- Acceptance criteria: every model-facing envelope from the registered tool dispatcher returns a fixed canonical failure text plus a symbolic code (matching the SQL catch at :5058-5072), never raw err.message and never echoed rawOutput/parsedOutput; a shipped case must assert the banned path/identifier classes are absent from the envelope text.
- Related findings: shares the RC-T09-1/RC-T09-2 no-redaction boundary with the render-sink row F-463 and the durable-store row F-471 (kept separate: envelope vs render sinks vs at-rest); the skill-adapter instance of the same forwarding is filed separately under R9-L07a-3 · nguồn: R9 T09 — disclosure and credential surface

### F-471 — the runtime persists raw error text into durable, untracked trees (the skill-proposal JSON and gitignored logs/backups) while the only credential scanner is tracked-only and silently skips absent scratch, leaving a structurally unscanned surface for raw failure text

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R9-T09-2`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Gates + CI, Governance docs
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the durable-store write is in-repo on the skill-proposal path, so raw failure text is persisted into a tree the credential scan never enumerates; whether a real secret currently sits there is external-dependent and was not demonstrated.
- Root cause: index.mjs stores String(error?.message ?? error).slice(0,500) into vectorSync and persists it via writeJsonAtomicSync into a durable proposal JSON (:1521-1526, also :1513), and console.error warn sites carry raw messages into logs/ (e.g. :8521 mediaErr?.message), while conventions.md:108 declares backups/, logs/, temp/, scratch/ as gitignored generated trees and the F-038 scan enumerates only listTrackedFiles (repository-policy.test.mjs:1476-1478) and early-returns when scratch is absent (:1488-1491) — a producer-to-blind-consumer coverage gap; the disclosure surface is F2 but its root is a verification-coverage weakness (F7) in nature.
- Evidence: Where: integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:1521-1526 — error: String(error?.message ?? error).slice(0,500) persisted via writeJsonAtomicSync(skillProposalFilePath(storeDir,proposalId)) into a durable proposal JSON (also :1513) · :8521 — console.error warn site carrying raw mediaErr?.message toward logs/ · Where: docs/development/conventions.md:108 — declares backups/, logs/, temp/, scratch/ as generated trees never committed (via .gitignore **/logs/_, \**/backups/_) · Where: tests/repository-policy.test.mjs:1476-1478 — F-038 scans only listTrackedFiles · :1488-1491 — scratch test `if (!existsSync(scratch)) return;` silent pass · Measured: the credential scan is structurally tracked-only and the gitignored trees are never enumerated, so raw failure text written there is unscanned.
- Reproduction: register/scan-coverage traced directly — listTrackedFiles never returns the gitignored trees and the scratch test early-returns when the directory is absent, so a raw error string written to the proposal store or a gitignored log is never scanned; whether an actual credential currently sits in those trees is host/fault-contingent (external-dependent) and was not demonstrated.
- Acceptance criteria: the F-038 credential scan enumerates every declared generated tree (backups/, logs/, temp/, scratch/) with scanned === enumerated rather than tracked-files-only and no early-return on absence, and the store/emit sites use canonical fixed messages rather than raw error text — a test planting a sentinel credential in each declared tree must go red if any tree is dropped from the scan.
- Related findings: shares RC-T09-3/RC-T09-2 with the tracked-only roster gap; the credential-scan's internal coverage bug is filed separately under R9-L11b-3 and the forwarded model-envelope instance is F-470 · nguồn: R9 T09 — disclosure and credential surface

### F-472 — the plugin mints artifact-set and payload SHA-256 digests from unbounded full reads guarded only by path containment, so an oversized in-sandbox artifact is fully read and hashed before any size ceiling — including the validator's, which itself reads whole — is ever consulted

- Category: F6 — Reliability & Performance
- Legacy register id: `R9-T12-1`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: result-digest computation runs on every in-sandbox artifact, so the unbounded read + hash precedes the validator's ceiling in the live path.
- Root cause: buildResultDigest computes sha256 = createHash("sha256").update(readFileSync(logicalPath)) (index.mjs:2960-2964) preceded only by existsSync (:2945) and artifactContainmentFailure (:2953-2956, a path-scope guard not a size ceiling), and payloadDigest re-reads readFileSync(artifactPath) (:2980-2984); the only size ceiling (validator :537) runs later in validateTechnicalArtifact, after that validator's own full read at :517, so the ceiling can refuse the verdict but never the cost.
- Evidence: Where: integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:2960-2964 — createHash("sha256").update(readFileSync(logicalPath)), preceded only by existsSync (:2945) and the containment guard (:2953-2956, path-scope not size) · :2980-2984 — payloadDigest re-reads readFileSync(artifactPath) · Where: integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs:537 — the sole size ceiling, consulted later at :540, itself after the full read at :517 · Measured: no size bound exists at either plugin read site before the digest is minted.
- Reproduction: traced directly per the brief — the plugin computes artifactSet sha256 (:2962) and payloadDigest (:2982) from unbounded readFileSync with only a containment check, both before validateTechnicalArtifact reaches its ceiling comparison at :540; no oversized host artifact was exercised but the read-before-ceiling ordering is proven.
- Acceptance criteria: both plugin read sites (:2962, :2982) refuse on stat.size against the FORMAT_MAX_SIZE_LIMITS table before readFileSync/createHash, and the digest is minted only after the bound passes, so an over-limit in-sandbox artifact is rejected without a full buffer; a regression sized >max must fail before hashing.
- Related findings: sibling of F-464 (R9-L05-2) — one rule, two modules: this earlier plugin digest read precedes the validator ordering that row describes, so round-2 ruled keep separate · nguồn: R9 T12 — resource ceilings

### F-475 — the shipped `.editorconfig` `[*]` rule is matched first against every path so the `[*.md]` trailing-whitespace exemption is dead, and `repo:check` rejects the hard breaks the editor declares legal, which re-opens `F-342`

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R9-L10-2`
- Affected modules: Build & runtime config, Governance docs
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `readEditorConfig` pushes rules in file order (`scripts/repository-policy.mjs:1158` `rules.push({ pattern: section, trims: … })`, measured) and `:1177-1179` (measured) returns the FIRST match, while `:1172` (measured) compiles a pattern as `^<source>($|/.*)$` with `*` widened to `[^/]*` — so the `[*]` section at `.editorconfig:3-7` (measured, `trim_trailing_whitespace = true`) matches EVERY path including `note.md` and `docs/x.md`, and the `[*.md]` exemption at `.editorconfig:18-19` (measured, `false`) is never reached; the check that consumes it is `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:903` `if (editorConfig.trimsTrailingWhitespace(repositoryPath))` (measured), run by `repo:check`, a `verify` stage (`package.json:29`, measured) and the CI `quality-gate` (`ci.yml:46`, measured).
- Root cause: the matcher implements neither editorconfig precedence (a later section must override an earlier one) nor basename matching at any depth, so the order inside `.editorconfig` silently decides policy and the exemption is dead even for top-level Markdown. `F-342`'s `Status: REFUTED` is what hides this: `docs/execution/findings.md:512` credits `tests/repository-policy.test.mjs:1147-1158` with asserting the deferral, but the lines I measured there (`:1147` `failures.some((failure) => /no record names/u.test(failure))`, `:1151` `/has no allowlist pattern/u`) belong to the F-339 gitleaks-allowlist tests starting at `:1157`, and the real deferral test is `tests/repository-policy.test.mjs:1241-1263`.
- Evidence: Where: `.editorconfig:3-7` and `:18-19` (both measured) · Where: `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:1144-1185` — `:1149-1159` file-order push (measured `:1156` property regex, `:1158` push), `:1163-1172` the `^…($|/.*)$` compile (measured `:1169`, `:1172`), `:1176-1182` first-match-wins with a `true` default · `:903` the gated trailing-whitespace check (measured) · Where: `tests/repository-policy.test.mjs:1241-1263` (measured) writes a synthetic editorconfig containing only `root = true`, `[*.md]`, `trim_trailing_whitespace = false` (`:1246`) and a top-level `note.md` (`:1248`), so the shipped `[*]` section is never in the denominator, and `:1255-1261` keeps `code.mjs` checked · Measured: the two locators inside `F-342`'s refutation are wrong at HEAD — `tests/repository-policy.test.mjs:1147-1158` is gitleaks, and `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:707-712` now lives at `:903-912`.
- Reproduction: the verifier probed the shipped config through the module's own matcher: `matches("*","note.md")` = true and `matches("*.md","docs/x.md")` = false, so `trimsTrailingWhitespace` returns true for both where the editorconfig says false; `node --test tests/repository-policy.test.mjs` was 67/67 green because no case pairs the shipped `[*]` with `[*.md]`. Adding two trailing spaces to a line in a tracked `.md` file and running `npm run repo:check` reddens, while prettier preserves them (`package.json:21-22` markdown globs, measured). At HEAD no tracked `.md` line carries trailing whitespace, so the gate is not currently red.
- Acceptance criteria: `readEditorConfig` (`scripts/repository-policy.mjs:1144-1185`) resolves the LAST matching section and matches `*.md` by basename at any depth, and a case run against the SHIPPED `.editorconfig` asserts `trimsTrailingWhitespace("note.md") === false` and `trimsTrailingWhitespace("docs/x.md") === false` while `code.mjs` stays true; restoring first-match-wins or the `($|/.*)$` anchor turns that case red, and `F-342` is re-graded against these two measured locators.
- Related findings: re-opens `F-342` (`docs/execution/findings.md:506-517`), whose refutation cites the wrong test range and a stale script locator · the precedence bug is an independent `repository-policy.mjs:1163-1172`/`:1176-1182` logic defect that round 2 deliberately kept out of RC-T10-1 · nguồn: R9 L10/T10 — gates and CI truth

### F-476 — `cancel-in-progress: true` on `push: main` lets a fast second push cancel the earlier commit's run before the merge-gate detector evaluates it, leaving that SHA permanently without a post-merge verdict

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R9-L10-4`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: the mechanism is `ci.yml:16-18` (measured `concurrency:` at `:16`, `cancel-in-progress: true` at `:18`) plus a detector keyed to exactly one SHA (`ci.yml:127-128` measured `if: always() && github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'` with `needs: quality-gate`; `ci.yml:147-148` measured `MERGE_GATE_SHA: ${{ github.sha }}` then `node scripts/merge-gate.mjs --sha "$MERGE_GATE_SHA"`; `scripts/merge-gate.mjs:115` measured `run.headSha === sha`), and firing it needs the external GitHub Actions runner to actually cancel the in-flight run.
- Root cause: the post-merge half of the blocker is per-SHA while the workflow's concurrency group is per-ref, so the SHA whose run got cancelled simply has no verdict and nothing sweeps for it. Round 3 upheld the narrowing FLAG: the cancelled commit still ran its own `quality-gate`, and the next push to `main` is a descendant that re-tests the same tree, so the real loss is only the per-SHA detector record — the row must not be read as "ungated code reached main".
- Evidence: Where: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:16-18` (measured) the concurrency block with `cancel-in-progress: true` · `:127-128` (measured) the detector's push/main condition and its `needs: quality-gate` · `:147-148` (measured) the single-SHA input · Where: `scripts/merge-gate.mjs:115` (measured) the head-SHA filter and `:120` (measured) the reason it emits when no run exists at that SHA.
- Reproduction: not attempted — reproducing it means pushing twice to `main` inside one run's duration and cancelling a remote workflow, which this read-only audit may not do; instead both halves were read at HEAD (`ci.yml:16-18` with `:127-148`, `merge-gate.mjs:115`) and the round-3 verifier recorded the harm as needing the external Actions cancellation, keeping the grade Low and the state LATENT.
- Acceptance criteria: every SHA that lands on `main` carries a detector verdict, so either the concurrency block at `ci.yml:16-18` does not cancel `refs/heads/main` runs or the detector sweeps the commits lacking a record rather than reading one `${{ github.sha }}`; the reason string at `merge-gate.mjs:120` must name the un-gated SHA in that sweep, and a lane that supplies a run state missing one merged SHA must fail closed.
- Related findings: F-474 (the same evaluator the detector calls, which also scores green from one job) · distinct from the closed `F-388` always() fix (`CHANGELOG.md:2010`), which addressed the SKIP path not the CANCEL path · shares RC-T10-1 with F-477, F-478, F-479, F-480, F-481 (here the proxy is "the SHA the concurrency let finish") · nguồn: R9 L10/T10 — gates and CI truth

### F-477 — the skip-expectation gate's must-run and inert-lane refusals execute only under `process.env.CI`, and CI is precisely the host where the must-run `symlink` family does not skip, so local verify is green while the control contributes zero executed assertions

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R9-T10-1`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: re-measured at transcription time on the repository's own report, `CI=1 node scripts/skip-expectation-gate.mjs .skip-expectation.xml --json` printed `SKIP EXPECTATION GATE: FAIL` (exit 1) with one failure entry naming the symlink family as expected to RUN on the CI runner while having skipped 5 case(s), and the same report tallies the symlink family at lanes 5, passes 80, skips 5, maxSkips 0, and the identical command with `CI` unset printed `SKIP EXPECTATION GATE: PASS` (exit 0) with `failures: []` and unchanged tallies.
- Root cause: `scripts/skip-expectation-gate.mjs:257` `if (!process.env.CI) continue;` (measured) sits BEFORE the must-run branch at `:258-269` (measured `:258` `if (family.expectation === "must-run")`, `:265` `if (passes === 0)`), and the inert-lane detection at `:221-230` (measured `:221` `if (process.env.CI) {`, `:223` `entry.passes === 0 && entry.skips === 0`) is CI-gated the same way, so the refusals are enforced only where skips are declared legal and disabled where they are forbidden; no host both skips the control and is forced to enforce it.
- Evidence: Where: `scripts/skip-expectation-gate.mjs:257`, `:258`, `:265`, `:271` (all measured; `:271` `} else if (skips > (family.maxSkips ?? 0)) {`) and `:221`, `:223` (measured) · Where: `docs/contracts/ci-skip-expectations.json:43-46` (measured) the `symlink` family as `must-run` with `maxSkips: 0` and the reason "the CI runner can create symlinks, so the family must execute there; a skip means the case stopped protecting the refusal" · Measured: the symlink family's 5 skips come from this win32 host, and the closed `F-197` lane `tests/agent-owner-F-197-skip-expectation.test.mjs` is wired into `npm test` (`package.json:24`, measured).
- Reproduction: run read-only twice at transcription time against the existing `.skip-expectation.xml` (the script contains zero `writeFileSync` sites, verified before running): with `CI=1` the verdict is FAIL and exit 1 on the symlink family alone; with `env -u CI` the verdict is PASS and exit 0 on the identical input. The ceiling for symlink is `maxSkips: 0`, so the family contributes zero executed assertions locally and is still counted green, while on the ubuntu runner symlinks work and the refusal never has anything to catch.
- Acceptance criteria: the must-run refusal at `scripts/skip-expectation-gate.mjs:258-269` and the inert-lane refusal at `:221-230` evaluate on every host, with a declared `NOT RUN` path when a family truly cannot execute; the existing `F-197` lane must fail when `CI` is unset on a report whose must-run family skipped, and deleting the `continue` at `:257` is what turns that case red.
- Related findings: F-481, F-479, F-480, F-478 (round-2 RC-T10-1 — the same proxy-green root cause, kept as separate rows because round 3 rejected the merge: different fix sites and denominators) · F-474, F-476 (the blocker that consumes this gate's verdict) · the closed `F-197` (`CHANGELOG.md:2002`, `:2005`) never flagged that its enforcement is CI-gated · nguồn: R9 L10/T10 — gates and CI truth

### F-478 — `deploymentEvidence: PASS` needs a posix host with the governed operator tree while family-ceiling enforcement needs `process.env.CI`, so on both configured hosts a green run reports deployment evidence and enforced ceilings that were never produced together

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R9-T10-2`
- Affected modules: deployment topology, Gates + CI
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `integrations/openclaw/verify.mjs:218-219` (measured) stamps `deploymentEvidence: posixModesEnforced && governedStateTreeReachable ? "PASS" : "NOT RUN"`, where `posixModesEnforced` comes from `:206` (measured) `hostEnforcesPosixModes(process.platform)` and `:160-162` (measured) `return platform !== "win32"`, and `governedStateTreeReachable` is `statSync(GOVERNED_STATE_TREE).isDirectory()` at `:209` with `false` on catch at `:211` (measured) — so the ubuntu CI checkout reports `NOT RUN` while the live operator host, which has no `CI`, runs the family ceilings off (`scripts/skip-expectation-gate.mjs:257`, measured).
- Root cause: the two halves bind to different, mutually absent host facts — one to the OS plus the operator's state root (`GOVERNED_STATE_TREE`, `verify.mjs:69-72`, resolved from the operator state-dir environment variables with a default that is an absolute path under the operator's home directory; value not transcribed here), the other to a runner-only environment variable — so the emitted deployment verdict is a statement about host capability, not about an executed deployment control, which is the F5 decision-correctness face of this F7 gap. Round 3 graded it PARTIAL: the co-absence is real on today's hosts, but it is a topology consequence rather than a code invariant.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/verify.mjs:206`, `:207-211`, `:215-219` (all measured) the two flags and the derived verdict · `:160-162` (measured) the win32 predicate · `:69-70` (measured) the env-var chain that resolves the governed tree · `:634` and `:642` (measured) the comment that `deploymentEvidence` is the verdict a reader sees and the field actually emitted · Where: `scripts/skip-expectation-gate.mjs:257` and `:221` (measured) the CI-only enforcement · `.github/workflows/ci.yml:127-128` (measured) the detector that runs on the host where deployment evidence is always `NOT RUN`.
- Reproduction: not executed — `verify.mjs` inspects the live governed operator state root, a read/write target this audit may not touch; the mechanism was read at `:205-221` and the two host facts (CI runner without the operator tree; operator host without `CI`) come from the round-3 measurements. The refuted half: the candidate's "satisfiable ONLY on disjoint hosts" is false as a code invariant — a self-hosted runner with `CI` set and the operator state-dir variable pointed at a real tree co-satisfies both halves in one run, which is why the verdict is PARTIAL and the reachability is external-dependent.
- Acceptance criteria: either one run must be able to emit both `deploymentEvidence: PASS` (`verify.mjs:218-219`) and enforced family ceilings (`skip-expectation-gate.mjs:257`), or `verify.mjs` must state in the verdict which half is missing instead of collapsing to a bare `NOT RUN`; a lane asserts the emitted object names `posixModesEnforced` and `governedStateTreeReachable` separately, and it reddens if either half is inferred from the other or from host capability alone.
- Related findings: F-477 (the CI-gated enforcement half of this row) · F-474, F-476 (the detector that consumes the verdict on the host where it is always `NOT RUN`) · F-481, F-479, F-480 (RC-T10-1 proxy greens) · not the ordering defect carried by candidate `R9-L05-5`, which stamps evidence before the control runs · nguồn: R9 L10/T10 — gates and CI truth

### F-479 — the M1 live-reachability coverage assertion is satisfied by construction wherever the live tree is not mountable, so it prints per-path coverage it never measured

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R9-L11a-2`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI, deployment topology
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — test-only: `tests/m12-t2.2-m1-live-reachability.test.mjs:195` (measured) declares the single coverage entry as `covers: (mountable) => !mountable`, and `:329` (measured) `const mountable = existsSync(LIVE_DIR)` is false on this win32 host and on the ubuntu CI runner, so the assertion at `:345-347` (measured `assert.equal(unasserted.length, 0, …)`) demands exactly the zero the platform predicate produced at `:342` (measured `unasserted.push(rel)`).
- Root cause: the coverage denominator is discharged by a platform predicate rather than by per-path evidence, so the property is falsifiable only on a host where the sibling per-path case self-skips — the green is a proxy for the platform's capability, not for an executed read. Round 3 kept it CONFIRMED and graded it Low for that reason, and rejected folding it into the other proxy-green rows.
- Evidence: Where: `tests/m12-t2.2-m1-live-reachability.test.mjs:195` (measured) the `covers` predicate · `:45` (measured) `LIVE_DIR`, the governed plugin tree under the operator's home directory (path value withheld) · `:329-330` (measured) the mountability flag and the empty `unasserted` list · `:333` (measured) the per-path `readFileSync` inside the try that feeds `:342` · `:345-347` (measured) the equality assertion and its message about paths that "drift with no gate noticing" · `:296` (measured) the comment claiming this proves coverage "on every platform".
- Reproduction: the verifier ran an in-memory coverage probe over the same predicate: on a non-mountable host `unasserted.length` is 0 and the assertion passes by construction; on a mountable host with one genuinely ungated path `unasserted` is 1 and it reddens. Not re-run at transcription because the mountable branch needs the live operator tree; the cited lines were re-opened instead, and the sibling M1-4 per-path case self-skips on this host, which is what the row's "false assurance" refers to.
- Acceptance criteria: coverage is decided per governed path with a non-empty read denominator and may not consume the platform flag, so the `covers` predicate at `:195` is removed or made path-keyed and the case asserts `scanned + declared_not_live_reachable === total` on BOTH hosts; a lane that deletes one governed path from the declaration list must redden even when the tree is absent, and restoring the `!mountable` predicate must redden it too.
- Related findings: F-481, F-480, F-477, F-478 (RC-T10-1 — a green derived from a proxy; round 3 explicitly rejected the three-way merge with this row because the fix sites and denominators differ) · the case this row measures was created by the closed legacy `R2-B16-02` (`CHANGELOG.md:1711`) · nguồn: R9 L11a/L11b — test validity

### F-480 — the repo-wide Discord-tag sweep asserts a denominator of `git ls-files` entries while swallowing unreadable files, so a tracked governed file that cannot be read is silently unscanned and the gate stays green

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
