- Root cause: the assertion proves enumeration, not scanning, and there is no `scanned` counter to compare against, so the more files that fail to read, the greener the lane; it is a data-correctness shape (an unverified result) whose nature here is a verification gap. Round 3 graded it LATENT and Low rather than the candidate's Medium, and kept it separate from the credential lane's identical swallow.
- Evidence: Where: `tests/m12-t2-governed-surface.test.mjs:574-577` (measured) `function trackedGovernedSourceFiles()` invoking `git -c safe.directory=… ls-files -z` · `:598-602` (measured) the enumeration-length assertion · `:604-610` (measured) the per-file loop whose read failure is dropped by a bare `continue` · `:611` (measured) a second uncounted `continue` on NUL bytes · `:612-614` (measured) the patterns the surviving reads are actually checked against.
- Reproduction: not attempted — creating an unreadable tracked file means writing to the index or the working tree, which a read-only audit may not do; the locators were re-opened at HEAD (`:574-577`, `:598-610`) and the candidate's measurement of 0 unreadable tracked governed files is the named blocker.
- Acceptance criteria: the lane counts what it examined — a `scanned` counter incremented only after a successful read and asserted equal to `files.length` alongside the existing `files.length > 50` check at `:599-602`, with read errors and NUL-skips named in the failure message — so a fixture holding one unreadable tracked governed file reddens the case, and reverting the counter to the bare `continue` reddens it again.
- Related findings: F-482 (the same uncounted-read swallow inside the credential-literal helpers, a different gate) · F-481, F-479, F-477, F-478 (RC-T10-1 proxy denominators) · distinct from the closed `F-214` (`docs/execution/findings.md:428`), whose refutation measured that the walk exists, not that its reads are guarded · nguồn: R9 L11a/L11b — test validity

### F-481 — the postgres and docker may-skip ceilings equal their own family denominators (4/4 and 32/32), so 100 % of those lanes may skip and the skip-expectation gate still reports PASS

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R9-L11b-2`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `docs/contracts/ci-skip-expectations.json:49-52` (measured) declares the `postgres` family `may-skip` with `"maxSkips": 4` and the reason that its four cases report NOT RUN on a runner without a Docker daemon, and `:55-58` (measured) declares `docker` `may-skip` with `"maxSkips": 32`, while `scripts/skip-expectation-gate.mjs:271` (measured) fires only on `skips > (family.maxSkips ?? 0)` — so all four postgres cases skipping is tolerated and the SQL statement gate contributes zero executed assertions to a green job. | BLOCKER measured 2026-10-24: applying the abandoned b8 lane gate to this tree reddens the SHIPPED F-197 case - tests/agent-owner-F-197-skip-expectation.test.mjs 7 passed / 1 failed - the refusal names the live-runtime family as one that contributed no pass at all. The acceptance criteria re-grade docs/contracts/ci-skip-expectations.json, and that re-grade needs per-family pass and skip tallies from a real CI runner; this host cannot produce them because the WSL, Docker and PostgreSQL lanes it needs are not reproducible here. Owner decision 2026-10-24: the row stays OPEN carrying this measurement, and no partial gate change ships.
- Root cause: each ceiling was sized from the family's own case count instead of from the maximum NOT RUN the project is willing to accept, leaving the comparison no discriminating range; docker's 32 is additionally dead capacity because its lanes are `readFileSync` + regex scans that structurally never skip. The `passes === 0` refusal at `:265` cannot rescue them because it applies only to `must-run` families, so the green is derived from a declared ceiling — a proxy for execution.
- Evidence: Where: `docs/contracts/ci-skip-expectations.json:49-52` (measured) postgres `maxSkips: 4` · `:55-58` (measured) docker `maxSkips: 32` · `:43-46` (measured) the contrast case, `symlink` as `must-run` with `maxSkips: 0` · Where: `scripts/skip-expectation-gate.mjs:248` (measured) `maxSkips: family.maxSkips ?? 0` and `:271` (measured) the strict-greater comparison, `:265` (measured) the must-run-only zero-pass refusal · Measured at transcription time from the repository's own report: postgres `lanes 1, passes 4`, docker `lanes 6, passes 32` — each family's pass tally equals its declared ceiling.
- Reproduction: re-run read-only at transcription time as `CI=1 node scripts/skip-expectation-gate.mjs .skip-expectation.xml --json`: both families are tallied with `maxSkips` equal to their own case counts and appear with no failure entry, the only failure in that run being the symlink must-run family (F-477), and `env -u CI` on the same report yields `failures: []`. The verifier's logic probe on an all-skipping postgres report (passes 0, skips 4, maxSkips 4) returned `failures: []` — PASS — and only a fifth skip would fire.
- Acceptance criteria: every `may-skip` family declares `maxSkips < (passes + skips)` or becomes `must-run` with a documented `NOT RUN` path, and a lane reddens any declaration whose `maxSkips` equals the family's own case count by comparing `docs/contracts/ci-skip-expectations.json` against the parsed report tallies; restoring 4 or 32 turns that case red, and the docker reason is corrected to state that its lanes cannot skip.
- Related findings: F-477 (the same gate, whose enforcement is CI-gated — different defect, different fix site) · F-479, F-480, F-478 (RC-T10-1 proxy greens, kept separate per the rejected merge) · the closed `F-197` (`CHANGELOG.md:2002`) bounded only "more than declared" and `F-384` (`CHANGELOG.md:2002-2003`) sized duckdb 10/130 the same way · nguồn: R9 L11a/L11b — test validity

### F-484 — the R2-J4 lock-ordering case asserts a constant-true arity check and pins the absence of a string whose identifiers never appear in guard.mjs, so the queue-then-ledger order is unfalsifiable

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R9-L11a-1`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — test-only: the case runs in the `npm test` lane and is green at HEAD while neither of its two order-sensitive controls can fail (`tests/m10-t2-r2-coordinator-execution-authority.test.mjs:1117-1136`, measured by reading the case body)
- Root cause: `assert.equal(taskIdOf.length > 0, true)` at `:1135` compares a function object's declared arity (`:241` is `function taskIdOf(paths) {`, arity 1) against a literal, so it is true by construction and asserts nothing about production state; the ordering check at `:1128-1133` is an `includes(...) === false` pin on a call spelling whose second identifier (`readSnapshotLocked`) does not exist in `guard.mjs` at all, so the absence it claims to police can never be violated by the code it reads.
- Evidence: Where: `tests/m10-t2-r2-coordinator-execution-authority.test.mjs:1135` — `assert.equal(taskIdOf.length > 0, true);` (re-opened at HEAD 2026-10-23) · Where: `:1124-1127` — `coordinatorCommits` from `/commitCoordinatorMutation\(|queueExecutionFence\(/g` then `assert.equal(coordinatorCommits.length, 3)` · Where: `:1128-1133` — the `includes("withExecutionFence(lockDir, () => readSnapshotLocked(paths))") === false` pin with message "the ledger must never take the queue lock" · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/guard.mjs:984` `function commitCoordinatorMutation(owner, commit) {`, `:989` `return queueExecutionFence(`, `:1855` the call site — the production ordering is real and unobserved by this case · Measured: the round-3 probe returns `taskIdOf.length>0 = true | declared arity = 1` and HEAD `{count:3,countOk:true,absentOk:true}`; a grep for `readSnapshotLocked` in `guard.mjs` returns 0 hits (it lives only in `ledger.mjs`)
- Reproduction: node -e mutation probes over copies of the guard text: variant A (ledger takes the queue lock, different call spelling) keeps `{count:3,countOk:true,absentOk:true}` so the case stays green, while variant B (reformatted fence call) yields `{count:2,countOk:false}` — the candidate's blanket "deleting or reformatting the control keeps the suite green" claim is refuted for the occurrence pin, and only the arity-constant plus absence-pin half survives.
- Acceptance criteria: `tests/m10-t2-r2-coordinator-execution-authority.test.mjs:1135` is deleted or replaced by an assertion against production state, and a case acquires the queue lock and the ledger lock in both orders through `commitCoordinatorMutation`/`queueExecutionFence` (`guard.mjs:984`, `:989`) so it turns red when the ordering control is removed; the `:1127` occurrence pin is kept as-is because it is already falsifiable.
- Related findings: same lexical-oracle class as F-487 and F-488 (RC-T11-1; the shared root cause is stated once in R9 T11's chain, not repeated per row) · no register duplicate: round 3 checked `findings.md:412` (the MODEL_ALLOWLIST row) and the closed row behind F-484's `taskIdOf` premise and found neither matches this file or premise · nguồn: R9 L11a — test harness ownership (gate-lane validity)

### F-485 — sixteen shipped lanes create mkdtemp temp trees with no removal of any kind, so every run leaves a growing residue in the OS temp directory

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R9-L11a-4`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — test-only: measured at HEAD across the shipped `tests/` population — 16 lanes create `mkdtempSync` trees and contain no removal API whatsoever, so the residue accumulates in `os.tmpdir()` on every run
- Root cause: no gate owns a lane's resource discipline (`scripts/repository-policy.mjs` carries no `mkdtemp`/`rmSync` rule), so lanes that copy the create-a-temp-root idiom never copy the `finally`/`t.after` removal their siblings use; the round-1 row understated the population as "two lanes" and credited sibling cleanup that does not exist at HEAD, which is why the leak read as bounded.
- Evidence: Where: `tests/m12-t2.2-a3-queue-writer-lock.test.mjs:81` — ``const dir = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), `a3-queue-${name}-`));`` (re-opened at HEAD 2026-10-23) plus a second tree at `:326` (`b12-${label}-`) · Where: `tests/agent-setup-F-265.test.mjs:29`— the`node:fs`import list does not even name a removal function, with trees at`:66`and`:219`· Where:`tests/m11-t3-openclaw-runtime-bounds.test.mjs:88`—`return mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), prefix));`inside`tempRoot`with zero removal calls in the file, so the claim that this lane cleans up is false at HEAD · Measured: a read-only scan of the 262 files in`tests/`→ 160 contain the token`mkdtemp`(the denominator round 1 used; round 3 counted 143`mkdtempSync`lanes), and exactly **16** of them hold at least one`mkdtempSync`call site with no`rmSync`/`fs.rm(`/`rmdir`/`rimraf`/`after(`/`unlink`at all — measured across 16 lanes, including`m12-t2.2-a3-queue-writer-lock`, `agent-setup-F-188/-264/-265/-290/-291/-292`, `agent-lib-F-002/-058`, `agent-plugin-F-243/-F-296`, `m11-t3-openclaw-runtime-bounds`, `m7-lane-a/-c/-d`and`m7-r4-root-fix`— which round 3 sized at 29 creation sites per run (a raw`mkdtempSync`token scan of the same 16 files returns 44 because the token also appears in comments and assertion strings); round-3 corrected siblings:`m12-t2-r4`rmSync=10,`m11-t3-durable-state-integrity`rmSync=6,`m8-t2.3-pg-probe` not present at HEAD
- Reproduction: read-only node sweep over `tests/*.mjs` (no gate executed, no temp tree created by the probe): filter lanes matching `mkdtemp`, keep those with a `mkdtempSync` call site, then re-check each against the removal-API family — all 16 return zero, and the only hits anywhere in them are non-cleanup text (`tests/m12-t2.2-a3-queue-writer-lock.test.mjs:393`/`:415` assertion strings about an unlink and the contract prose in `tests/agent-setup-F-291.test.mjs`).
- Acceptance criteria: each of the 16 lanes removes its temp roots in `finally` or `t.after`, and `scripts/repository-policy.mjs` gains a policy rule that fails any lane file matching `mkdtemp` with zero removal API — red against the current tree, green only once every lane in the population removes what it creates.
- Related findings: same unowned-harness class as F-491 · the round-1 duplicate check found no register row for this population (the closed swallowed-cleanup note names a different file) · nguồn: R9 L11a — test harness ownership (lane resource discipline)

### F-486 — the F-195 acknowledgement case is the only probe spawn in its lane that inherits the ambient environment, so an exported ZINK_PROBE_STATE_DIR silently outranks the argv root the case intends

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R9-L11a-5`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: the blocker is the absence of any HEAD-side exporter — a repo-wide grep for `ZINK_PROBE_STATE_DIR` over `*.mjs`/`*.json`/`*.yml`/`*.md` found only the three probes that read it and two tests that scrub it, no script, `package.json` entry or CI job that exports it, so firing the defect requires a host-exported variable value the register cannot assert
- Root cause: the spawn at `:648-652` omits the `env` key, so the child inherits the parent environment, and the probe resolves its state root as env-then-argv, meaning an ambient value silently outranks the root the case passes on the command line; the assertion then reddens for a reason unrelated to the control under test, and when the outranked root is a real one the probe journals synthetic provenance into it before the acknowledgement refusal stops it.
- Evidence: Where: `tests/m7-t4-orchestration.test.mjs:647-655` — measured at HEAD 2026-10-23: `spawnSync(process.execPath, [probePath, <the hard-coded production state-root literal>], { encoding: "utf8" })` with no `env` key, asserting `PRODUCTION_LEDGER_WRITE_NOT_ACKNOWLEDGED` at `:654` · Where: `tests/m7-t4-orchestration.test.mjs:639-641` — the sibling "no state root named" case does pass `env: { ...process.env, ZINK_PROBE_STATE_DIR: "" }`, so the omission is specific to the ack case · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/live-governance-probe.mjs:59` — `const stateDir = process.env.ZINK_PROBE_STATE_DIR ?? process.argv[2] ?? null;` (re-opened at HEAD; the same precedence line also exists in `operator-token-probe.mjs:13` and `runtime-preflight.mjs:11`) · Where: `tests/agent-last-F-033.test.mjs:41` `delete env.ZINK_PROBE_STATE_DIR;` and `tests/agent-topo-F-219.test.mjs:93` — the scrub hygiene already used elsewhere in the harness
- Reproduction: read-only grep measurement above; the fault-injection that would prove it (export the variable, then run the case) was not executed because it writes through the probe and full gates are forbidden for this band, and a second guard at `live-governance-probe.mjs:116-119` (`PROBE_STATE_DIR_NOT_DISPOSABLE`, reached via `holdsGovernanceHistory(resolvedStateDir)`) blocks the write for any root that holds governance history.
- Acceptance criteria: the ack case's `spawnSync` options pass an explicit `env` that deletes `ZINK_PROBE_STATE_DIR` (the `tests/agent-last-F-033.test.mjs:41` idiom), so the argv root is the only root the probe can resolve; a lane reddens if any probe spawn in `tests/m7-t4-orchestration.test.mjs` inherits that variable.
- Related findings: exploits exactly the default-root mechanism the register already REFUTED for the probe (the "state root must be NAMED (env or argv)" disposition at `findings.md:226`), so this is a new row and not a duplicate; distinct from the `ZINK_ROUTER_*` hygiene row · nguồn: R9 L11a — test harness ownership (probe env hygiene)

### F-488 — the provider-matrix disclaimer guard skips any provider absent from the README and inspects only its first occurrence, so a respelled mention or an un-disclaimed second mention stays green

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R9-L11b-5`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI, Governance docs
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — test-only: the case is green at HEAD while four of the seven roster members already have a second, disclaimer-free mention that the guard never looks at (`tests/m9-t1-functional-truth-remediation.test.mjs:1014-1031`, re-opened 2026-10-23)
- Root cause: the loop iterates a hand-typed roster and computes `readme.indexOf(provider)`, `continue`-ing when the name is absent, so deleting or respelling a mention removes the check instead of failing it, and `indexOf` inspects only the first occurrence inside a ±400-byte window, so a provider advertised as live in any later mention is never examined; nothing asserts that the roster size matched the number of findings.
- Evidence: Where: `tests/m9-t1-functional-truth-remediation.test.mjs:1014-1022` — the seven-provider roster (`BigQuery`, `MotherDuck`, `Databricks`, `Kaggle`, `Streamlit`, `Hugging Face`, `DagsHub`) typed inline (re-opened at HEAD) · Where: `:1023-1024` — `const at = readme.indexOf(provider); if (at < 0) continue;` · Where: `:1025-1031` — the ±400-byte window with the `simulation|test double|not implemented|không phải|chưa triển khai|contract` regex and no `found === providers.length` assertion · Measured: a node pass over `zink-tools/README.md` returned `BigQuery occ=2 guarded=1`, `MotherDuck occ=2 guarded=1`, `Databricks occ=2 guarded=1`, `Kaggle occ=2 guarded=1`, and for all four `idx0 guarded? true idx1 guarded? false`, the un-guarded second mention being the "Tính toán ngoại vi" adapter bullet
- Reproduction: read-only node -e over `zink-tools/README.md` counting every occurrence of each roster name and applying the guard's own window regex to each; the second-occurrence result above is the surviving evidence, and the absent-provider half is proven by the `continue` at `:1024` (no occurrence → no assertion, case still passes).
- Acceptance criteria: the guard asserts `found === providers.length` (or derives the roster from the README headings / `adapters/compute/*-adapter.mjs` filenames) and applies the disclaimer window to every occurrence of every name, so deleting a mention, respelling a name, or adding an un-disclaimed second mention reddens `tests/m9-t1-functional-truth-remediation.test.mjs`.
- Related findings: same lexical-oracle class as F-484 and F-487 (RC-T11-1) and the hand-typed-roster class R9 T11 documents as RC-T11-3 · related to the R9 L07a-4 provider claim but a different mechanism · nguồn: R9 L11b — test validity (doc-truth guards)

### F-489 — operator-lock-recovery.mjs reads neither ZINK_OPERATOR_STATE_DIR nor a positional root and defaults to the homedir state root, while README BƯỚC 1b tells the operator to run its mutating recovery bare

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R9-L12-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), Governance docs
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: README.md:263/:265/:267 are the operator path (BƯỚC 1b) and `:265` invokes this driver with the mutating `--recover --confirm=ORPHAN_LOCK_RECOVERY` and no root argument, while README.md:250 promises every operator driver "dừng lại với `STATE_DIR_NOT_NAMED`" — measured at HEAD the driver resolves a root instead of refusing
- Root cause: the fixture is the one operator driver that never adopted the named-root contract: it seeds `stateDir` from `path.join(os.homedir(), ".openclaw-zink")`, accepts only the `--state-dir` flag, and throws `Unknown argument` for a bare positional root, so the documented "env or first argument" rule cannot be satisfied; because it is absent from both driver rosters that pin the contract, no gate notices the exemption and the README claim keeps reading as though it were covered.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-lock-recovery.mjs:40` — `const DEFAULT_STATE_DIR = path.join(os.homedir(), ".openclaw-zink");` (re-opened at HEAD 2026-10-23) · Where: `:45` `stateDir: DEFAULT_STATE_DIR,` in the parsed defaults, `:51-54` the only accepted root form (`--state-dir`), `:58` ``else throw new Error(`Unknown argument ${argument}`)`` · Where:`README.md:250`the`STATE_DIR_NOT_NAMED`promise and`README.md:263`, `:265`, `:267`the bare invocations · Where:`tests/agent-topo-F-219.test.mjs:155-162`—`EXPECTED_REFUSALS`(6 entries, none for lock recovery) and`tests/agent-setup-F-373.test.mjs:36-38`— the 3-driver roster · Measured: token counts inside the driver are`process.env`0,`STATE_DIR_NOT_NAMED`0,`homedir`1; a grep for`operator-lock-recovery`across`tests/` returns a single hit (`tests/m12-t2-r4-orphan-lock-recovery.test.mjs:52`), i.e. the named-root rosters never list it
- Reproduction: read-only run with HOME redirected to a scratch directory: the bare invocation exits 0 and reports the scratch home's `.openclaw-zink` as `state_dir` with `lock_present:false` and `mutated:false` — no `STATE_DIR_NOT_NAMED` — while a positional root throws `Unknown argument`; the mutating unlink is gated by the driver's `if (!options.recover)` branch, so the delete-the-wrong-root half is host-dependent and was measured as `mutated:false` rather than executed against a real root.
- Acceptance criteria: the driver resolves its root from `ZINK_OPERATOR_STATE_DIR` or its first argument and exits with `STATE_DIR_NOT_NAMED` when neither is given (the homedir default removed), and it is added to `EXPECTED_REFUSALS` in `tests/agent-topo-F-219.test.mjs` and to the `tests/agent-setup-F-373.test.mjs` driver roster — the roster case turns red the moment the default is restored.
- Related findings: F-490 is the same operator-named-root contract on the configure/preflight side, kept separate because the verdict separates them and the patches differ · the closed `F-373` entry (`CHANGELOG.md:651-660`) claims "every driver" reads the root, which is false for this one and is the claim this row corrects · nguồn: R9 L12 — doc truth (operator runbook and state-root naming)

### F-490 — the deploy runbook promises an operator-named state root but configure.mjs hard-refuses any root other than the homedir default, and its refusal string is not in the classifier, so the operator sees an unnamed APPLY_REJECTED

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R9-L12-4`
- Affected modules: infrastructure scripts, Governance docs, Build & runtime config
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: README BƯỚC 1 → `operator-preflight.mjs` → `configure.mjs --preflight --state-dir` is the documented operator path, and re-running it at HEAD against a scratch root produces the hard refusal (`integrations/openclaw/configure.mjs:1559-1561`, re-opened 2026-10-23)
- Root cause: `defaultStateDirectory()` returns `path.join(homedir(), STATE_DIRECTORY_NAME)` with no environment override, so `configure.mjs` compares the resolved root against that single value and throws for anything else; the runbook meanwhile states that the driver "never guesses a machine path" and accepts an operator-named root, and because the refusal's literal is missing from the known-refusal list, the preflight's own rule ("unknown output stays undisclosed") collapses the message into a bare rejected verdict.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/configure.mjs:1559-1561` — `if (stateDir !== path.resolve(defaultStateDirectory())) { throw new Error("OpenClaw state directory must be ~/.openclaw-zink"); }` (verbatim at HEAD) · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:1214-1216` — `export function defaultStateDirectory() { return path.join(homedir(), STATE_DIRECTORY_NAME); }`, no env override (transcribed) · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-preflight.mjs:44-48` + `:55` — the spawn of `configure.mjs --preflight --state-dir <root>` and `:65` "Classify only known static diagnostics; unknown output stays undisclosed." · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/refusal-classifier.mjs:47` — `"BACKUP_NESTED_LEDGER_ROOT_PRESENT",` present while `KNOWN_REFUSAL_REASONS` (`:26-65`) has none of this literal · Where: `docs/runbooks/deploying-the-m12-t9-build.md:69-72` — "The operator drivers take the state root as an **input** (F-219): they read `ZINK_OPERATOR_STATE_DIR` or their first argument, and refuse with `STATE_DIR_NOT_NAMED` … the driver never guesses a machine path" (re-opened at HEAD) and the refusal table at `:133-138` · Measured: a grep of `refusal-classifier.mjs` for `OpenClaw state directory` returns 0 hits
- Reproduction: `node integrations/openclaw/configure.mjs --preflight --state-dir <scratch root outside the homedir default>` writes "OpenClaw state directory must be ~/.openclaw-zink" to stderr, and the classifier grep above shows that no reason code wraps it, so the operator's report degrades to APPLY_REJECTED/PREFLIGHT_REJECTED with no named reason.
- Acceptance criteria: either the runbook and `configure.mjs` agree on an operator-named root (an env override in `defaultStateDirectory()`) or the runbook states the homedir-only constraint plus its refusal text in the table at `docs/runbooks/deploying-the-m12-t9-build.md:133-138`; in both cases the literal joins `KNOWN_REFUSAL_REASONS` in `refusal-classifier.mjs` (and the duplicated list in `operator-preflight.mjs`) so the refusal is disclosed by name, and a lane reddens when the thrown literal is absent from that list.
- Related findings: F-489 is the driver-side half of the same named-root contract (the verdict keeps them separate because the fix sites differ) · the closed `F-383` entry (`CHANGELOG.md:2022`) is the identical shape on a different literal whose fix IS present at `refusal-classifier.mjs:47`, so this is a fresh unclassified instance, not a duplicate · nguồn: R9 L12 — doc truth (operator runbook and state-root naming)

### F-492 — the skip gate recognises only two case states, so a failed or errored case is counted as a pass, satisfies the must-run tripwire and is published as execution evidence

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R9-T11-2`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI
- Severity: Medium
- Status: PARTIAL — the failed/errored half is closed by `7e13dcb`: a `<failure>` or `<error>` case is now its own count and refuses the gate on every host, pinned by `tests/agent-owner-F-197-skip-expectation.test.mjs`. The childless zero-assertion half of the widened acceptance criterion is NOT closed: this row's own measurement says the junit reporter emits only `name`, `time`, `classname` and `file` per case, so a case that returns before any assertion is byte-identical to a pass and no predicate over this report can separate them; the criterion's named lane `tests/skip-expectation-gate.test.mjs` does not exist. Owner decision needed: re-scope the criterion to the two observable states, or name the signal the gate must read (2026-10-24).
- Root cause: `reportCases` parses only `/<skipped\b/u` from a testcase body, so `<failure>` and `<error>` children are indistinguishable from passing ones; the must-run tripwire therefore fires only at `passes === 0`, which a family that failed outright satisfies, and the ceiling test compares against the same inflated denominator — so the published "N passed" line is a count of report entries, not of executed assertions.
- Evidence: Where: `scripts/skip-expectation-gate.mjs:132` — `skipped = /<skipped\b/u.test(body);` (verbatim at HEAD; nothing reads a failure or error child) · Where: `:180-181` — `if (testCase.skipped) entry.skips += 1; else entry.passes += 1;` · Where: `:257` — `if (!process.env.CI) continue;` so the whole family loop is inert outside CI · Where: `:259` `if (skips > 0)`, `:265` `if (passes === 0)`, `:271` `} else if (skips > (family.maxSkips ?? 0))` — the only three failure branches · Where: `:281` — `${family.id}: ${family.passes} passed, …` published as evidence · Where: `:41` `/^\/\/ SKIP-FAMILY: ([a-z0-9-]+)$/mu` with `:68-71` family resolution (transcribed) · Where: `:221-230` — the inert-lane detector that fires when `entry.passes === 0 && entry.skips === 0` · Measured: the sibling ceiling candidate R9-L11b-2 is the denominator half of this same mechanism · third case state measured by R10 (V10): a childless `<testcase>` with no `<skipped/>` and no `<failure/>` — i.e. a case whose body `return`s before any assertion — is counted a pass and the junit reporter emits only `name`, `time`, `classname`, `file` per case, so no `tests=`/`checked=` attribute exists to expose it; probe output was `passes: 5` from five childless cases with no refusal. Correction to the census: **27** `// SKIP-FAMILY:` declarations in **27** files on node v24.15.0, and a genuinely skipped case DOES emit `<skipped/>`, which the gate does see
- Reproduction: a report of four `<testcase file="tests/m11-t1-postgres-sql-gate.test.mjs">` entries each carrying `<failure message="boom">` was written outside the repository and fed to the gate: with `CI` unset nothing can fail because `:257` skips the family loop outright, and with `CI=1` the gate exits FAIL only from the inert-lane detector firing on every other lane — the candidate's end-to-end "passes 4, failures [], PASS" was therefore NOT reproduced (the verifier could not isolate the postgres family's own line inside budget), while the failure-into-passes classification remains read directly from `:132` and `:180-181`.
- Acceptance criteria: `scripts/skip-expectation-gate.mjs` classifies `failure` and `error` children as a third state, exits FAIL on any case that is neither a pass nor a declared skippable, and excludes failed cases from `passes` before the must-run (`:265`) and ceiling (`:271`) comparisons — red for a synthetic report whose cases all carry `<failure>`, green for the same report with the failures removed. · widening added by R10 (V10): the gate must refuse all three non-executed states — skipped, failed/errored, and a childless zero-assertion case — and a case that adds a childless case to a `// SKIP-FAMILY:` lane must redden `tests/skip-expectation-gate.test.mjs`; the fix may not rely on an assertion counter, because node v24.15.0 exposes none (`t.diagnostics` is null)
- Related findings: carries the folded denominator insight of the sibling ceiling candidate R9-L11b-2 (that fix cannot close this row while the pass count is inflated) · the closed per-family-ceiling entry (CHANGELOG:1973) is present at HEAD and does not address failure classification · nguồn: R9 T11 — root-cause lane (test-validity shapes)

### F-493 — the collected-set floor and both sanctioned pytest invocations cover only apps/knowledge/tests, so the two tracked pdf-runtime contract tests are executed by nothing while a shipped docstring says the image runs them

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R9-T11-3`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI, Zink Knowledgebase, Build & runtime config
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — test-only / CI-invisible: at HEAD `git ls-files` shows `apps/knowledge/pdf-runtime/tests/test_ocr_contract.py` and `test_range_contract.py` tracked, and no executor references them — the two sanctioned invocations target `tests`, the image never copies `pdf-runtime/tests`, and the CI job is documented as deliberately not installing the closure
- Root cause: the floor is a count of the items the current run collected (`collected = len(session.items)` against the literals 245/161), so it only ever measures the suite the invocation names; because both invocations name `apps/knowledge/tests` and the Dockerfile copies only `pdf-runtime/pyproject.toml`/`uv.lock`/`requirements.txt` and `pdf-runtime/src`, the child suite is outside every denominator — and the docstring's claim that the floor "is a property of the WHOLE suite" is true only of the directory that was passed.
- Evidence: Where: `apps/knowledge/tests/conftest.py:27` `MIN_COLLECTED_WITH_TOKENIZER = 245`, `:30` `MIN_COLLECTED_WITHOUT_TOKENIZER = 161`, `:48` `collected = len(session.items)`, `:41-43` "The floor is a property of the WHOLE suite" (re-opened at HEAD 2026-10-23) · Where: `apps/knowledge/Dockerfile:74-76` — `COPY pdf-runtime/pyproject.toml pdf-runtime/uv.lock pdf-runtime/requirements.txt ./pdf-runtime/`, `COPY pdf-runtime/src ./pdf-runtime/src`, `COPY tests ./tests` (measured: `pdf-runtime/tests` is never copied) and `:85` `RUN uv sync --locked && uv run --no-sync python -m pytest tests -q` · Where: `package.json` `test:knowledge` = `uv run --directory apps/knowledge --locked python -m pytest tests -q` · Where: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:97-99` — "pdf-runtime is deliberately not installed here… That suite stays an explicit NOT RUN" and `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:640-656` — the job "must not install the pdf-runtime closure" (transcribed) · Where: `apps/knowledge/pdf-runtime/src/zink_pdf_runtime/runner.py:262-263` — the claim that the integration test runs this path inside the Linux image, which the Dockerfile copy list above contradicts · Measured: a glob of `apps/knowledge/pdf-runtime/**/*.py` returns `tests/test_ocr_contract.py` and `tests/test_range_contract.py` and no conftest anywhere under `pdf-runtime` · site added by R10 (V07, from R10-L08-3): Where: `apps/knowledge/pdf-runtime/src/zink_pdf_runtime/ocr.py:62-74` builds a four-key environment allowlist and a fixed argv and passes `env=environment`, while `apps/knowledge/pdf-runtime/tests/test_ocr_contract.py:33-42` patches `Popen` and asserts only `command[3:]` and never reads `popen.call_args.kwargs["env"]`; the executing suite `apps/knowledge/tests/test_pdf_ocr_runner_contract.py:53-97` spawns four fixture programs launched with `env` copied from `os.environ` and the whole file is `@unittest.skipUnless`-gated at `:100` (POSIX), so it is NOT RUN on this host · Measured: `package.json:25` roots pytest at `apps/knowledge/tests`, `ci.yml:97` states pdf-runtime is deliberately not installed, and `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:640-654` fails `repo:check` if the python-quality job names it — so no lane may execute it
- Reproduction: read-only measurements only — the Dockerfile copy list, the two sanctioned invocations, the CI comment, the policy rule and the glob above; no executor for the two files was found in any `*.mjs`, `*.yml` or `*.json` (the sole reference is `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:123-124` declaring a coverage scope for the path), and the suites were not executed because gates are forbidden for this band.
- Acceptance criteria: either one sanctioned executor names `apps/knowledge/pdf-runtime/tests` (Dockerfile copies it and a lane runs it) or the two files are removed from the tracked set; in both cases the floor in `apps/knowledge/tests/conftest.py` and the `runner.py:262-263` docstring state the same population, so a claim that the image runs the child suite reddens a lane that greps the Dockerfile for the copy line. · amendment added by R10 (V07): running the existing suite is not sufficient — the acceptance must bind the scrubbed environment itself, i.e. assert the `ocr.py:62-67` env allowlist and the argv tail, because the child's environment is asserted by NO suite at all including the dead one; and the row's impact stays a detection gap, not a live exposure, because HEAD does scrub
- Related findings: the deliberate NOT RUN is documented (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:97-99`, `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:640-656`), which is why the candidate's "a shipped gate makes executing that suite illegal" framing was dropped · R9 L09-3 is a symptom of this row per the round-2 flag · nguồn: R9 T11 — root-cause lane (test-validity shapes)

### F-494 — the 4 MiB tool-result envelope budget is enforced only on the guard's post-dispatch validation while a live tool seam returns stringified results past that site with no bound

- Category: F6 — Reliability & Performance
- Legacy register id: `R9-T12-3`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow contracts)
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `guard.beforeToolCall` (declared at `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/guard.mjs:1518`, re-opened 2026-10-23) is dispatched from four live plugin call sites — `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:8202`, `:8580`, `:8861`, `:8923` — measured by grep, and the envelope validator sits inside that method at `:2277-2286`
- Root cause: the published result budget is a single enforcement point on one dispatch path, so any tool seam that returns without passing through `validateContractSchema("tool-result", …)` is unbounded by construction; the live `zink_sql` seam stringifies its bridge result straight into the tool response, so an oversized envelope reaches the host from a path the budget was written to cover.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/schema.mjs:427-428` — `export const TOOL_CALL_ENVELOPE_MAX_BYTES = 65536;` / `export const TOOL_RESULT_ENVELOPE_MAX_BYTES = 4 * 1024 * 1024;` (re-opened at HEAD 2026-10-23) · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/guard.mjs:2277-2286` — "// 12. Post-Dispatch Result Validation & Correlation" and `validateContractSchema("tool-result", rawResult, TOOL_RESULT_ENVELOPE_MAX_BYTES)`, with the comment at `:2279-2281` naming the closed M12/T9 result-envelope fix (its 4 MiB constant is in place at `:2285`) — the only enforcement site · Where: `guard.mjs:2233` / `:2235` / `:2246` / `:2262` — the `rawResult` producers inside the same method (`async beforeToolCall(event, context, signalOrOptions) {` at `:1518`) · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:5050-5057` — `const result = await bridge.executeGovernedDuckDbQuery(…); return { content: [{ type: "text", text: JSON.stringify(result) }], };` (re-opened at HEAD) with no envelope validation on that seam, and the hook at `:7952` `async beforeToolCall(event, context, signal) {`
- Reproduction: read-only structural derivation — the enclosing method of `:2277` located by scanning to `:1518`, then every `beforeToolCall` reference in the plugin enumerated (`:8202`, `:8580`, `:8861`, `:8923`); the dispatch was not executed because running the live dispatcher requires the OpenClaw host. The round-2 premise that "the guard dispatch path is production-dead / has 0 production callers" is measured FALSE at HEAD by exactly those four sites and is dropped from this row; the surviving consequence is the result-envelope one, and the downstream card-clipping comparison was not re-opened within the verifier's budget so it is not asserted here.
- Acceptance criteria: the registered handler and every adapter return path validate the result against the same `tool-result` schema and `TOOL_RESULT_ENVELOPE_MAX_BYTES` before returning — including the `index.mjs:5050-5057` seam — with one contract test per output shape that reddens when the bound is removed from that seam.
- Related findings: the closed M12/T9 fix named in the guard comment at `guard.mjs:2279-2281` is present at HEAD and does not cover the non-guard seam, so this row is the surviving single-site half rather than a duplicate · nguồn: R9 T12 — root-cause lane (resource ceilings and side-effect idempotency)

### F-495 — the platforms operator walkthrough reads six response keys no Knowledgebase handler returns and a `COMPLETED` status absent from the enum, so an operator who follows it polls fields that are always null

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R9-L10-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Knowledgebase, Governance docs
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `platforms/knowledge-platform/README.md` is tracked at HEAD `cd41974` and its step 2 full-rebuild block (`:94-97`) posts the index run without ever assigning `$runId`, which step 3 (`:106`) then interpolates into the polling URL, so the rebuild path cannot be followed as written.
- Root cause: the walkthrough was written against an assumed response shape and only one of the two operator walkthroughs was ever corrected. The closed row `R7-OC2-02` (`CHANGELOG.md:1212-1213`) reads "both operator walkthroughs read index/search response fields the service never returns" and records `45aec4c` as closing it, but that commit touches `apps/knowledge/README.md` only (`git show --stat 45aec4c` → 1 file changed) and the regression guard resolves a single file (`apps/knowledge/tests/test_m11_api_attribution.py:322` reads `parents[1] / "README.md"`), so the platforms copy was never opened. It still reads `state`, `processed_files`, `total_files`, `hits`, `document_title` and `score`, expects a `connected` field on readyz, and declares a `COMPLETED` terminal status that `knowledge.index_run_status` does not contain. Category is F4 because this is a doc-to-API contract mismatch, not a wrong computation.
- Evidence: Where: `platforms/knowledge-platform/README.md:75` — the readyz step expects HTTP 200 carrying the database status `connected` · Where: `platforms/knowledge-platform/README.md:106-107` — polls `$status.state` and `$status.processed_files`/`$status.total_files` · Where: `platforms/knowledge-platform/README.md:110` — names the success status `COMPLETED` · Where: `platforms/knowledge-platform/README.md:127-132` — iterates `$response.hits` reading `$hit.document_title` and `$hit.score` · Where: `platforms/knowledge-platform/README.md:94-97` — the full-rebuild block issues the POST and sets no `$runId` · Where: `apps/knowledge/migrations/001_initial.sql:14` — `CREATE TYPE knowledge.index_run_status AS ENUM ('RUNNING', 'SUCCEEDED', 'FAILED', 'INTERRUPTED')` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:470` — readyz returns only `status` (`ready`), `active_index_id` and `embedding_model` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:973-986` — the index-run read returns `index_run_id`, `index_id`, `kind`, `status`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `sources_discovered`, `sources_indexed`, `sources_failed`, `failure_code`, `failure` and `source_counts` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:682-686` — search returns `total_count` and `results`, never `hits` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:607-627` — each result carries `title`, `text`, `rrf_score` and `citation`, so `$hit.citation`/`$hit.text` are real and the other names are not · Measured: `git grep -c -E "processed_files|total_files|document_title" -- apps/knowledge/app/main.py` returns zero matches.
- Reproduction: Read-only walk at HEAD `cd41974`: open `platforms/knowledge-platform/README.md:72-133` and diff every `$var.field` read against the return dicts at `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:470`, `:973-986` and `:682-686`, confirm the success code against `apps/knowledge/migrations/001_initial.sql:14`, then run `git show --stat 45aec4c` (one file: `apps/knowledge/README.md`) and read the guard's path expression at `apps/knowledge/tests/test_m11_api_attribution.py:322` to confirm no gate ever opened the platforms copy.
- Acceptance criteria: `platforms/knowledge-platform/README.md` steps 1–4 use only keys the handlers return (`status`, `sources_indexed`/`sources_discovered`, `total_count`, `results`, `title`, `rrf_score`, `citation`), name `SUCCEEDED` as the terminal success code, drop the `connected` expectation, and set `$runId` in the full-rebuild block at `:94-97`; the `R7-OC2-02` guard at `apps/knowledge/tests/test_m11_api_attribution.py:314-325` iterates every tracked operator walkthrough including `platforms/knowledge-platform/README.md` instead of one README, so reverting any field name turns it red.
- Related findings: completes the half of `R7-OC2-02` (`CHANGELOG.md:1212-1213`) that its closure commit never touched · same unguarded-doc family as `F-496` and `F-497` in this batch · nguồn: R9 L10 — platforms operator walkthrough against the Knowledgebase handler contract

### F-496 — docs/development/conventions.md documents `uv==0.12.5` while the workflow installs 0.12.0 and the shipped owner gate pins 0.12.0, and the gate-registry check compares names only so the stale line stays green

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R9-L12-1`
- Affected modules: Governance docs, Build & runtime config, Gates + CI
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: at HEAD `cd41974` `docs/development/conventions.md:98` states that the `CI / python-quality` job "installs `uv==0.12.5`" while `.github/workflows/ci.yml:65` runs `pip install --disable-pip-version-check "uv==0.12.0"`, so a reader who provisions the toolchain from the conventions doc gets a version no shipped surface uses.
- Root cause: the `uv` pin normalisation behind the owner gate shipped as `tests/agent-owner-F-338.test.mjs` aligned the three executable surfaces (image, CI, Windows driver) and left the prose in the same document family untouched. `checkGateRegistry` (`scripts/repository-policy.mjs:1322-1346`) parses the registry table by extracting only the first backticked cell of each row (`:1334`) and compares script names against `package.json`, so a documented version string can drift without any gate noticing. The missing assertion is a verification gap (F7-shaped), but the defect's nature is a doc-versus-shipped-config contract violation, hence F4.
- Evidence: Where: `docs/development/conventions.md:98` — asserts the python-quality job "installs `uv==0.12.5`" · Where: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:65` — `run: pip install --disable-pip-version-check "uv==0.12.0"` · Where: `tests/agent-owner-F-338.test.mjs:31` — `const CANONICAL_UV_VERSION = "0.12.0";` · Where: `tests/agent-owner-F-338.test.mjs:38-48` — the `pins` object extracts only `image`, `ci` and `windowsDriver` · Where: `tests/agent-owner-F-338.test.mjs:50-60` — the equality assertion covers those three surfaces and no documentation surface · Where: `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:1322-1346` — `checkGateRegistry` enumerates gate names only, never a column value · Measured: grepping `uv` version mentions in `docs/development/conventions.md` returns exactly two lines — `:74` (the registry row quoting `uv run --directory apps/knowledge --locked`, no version) and `:98` (`0.12.5`) — so `:98` is the doc's only version claim and it is the stale one.
- Reproduction: Read-only three-string comparison at HEAD `cd41974`: `docs/development/conventions.md:98` (`0.12.5`) against `.github/workflows/ci.yml:65` (`0.12.0`) and `tests/agent-owner-F-338.test.mjs:31` (`0.12.0`), plus `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:1334` to confirm the registry check cannot see the string. Round 3 recorded the owning lane green over exactly this tree (`pass 2 / fail 0`), which is the measurement that nothing reads the doc; this row re-ran no gate.
- Acceptance criteria: `docs/development/conventions.md:98` states the version the workflow actually installs (`0.12.0`), or `tests/agent-owner-F-338.test.mjs` gains a fourth pin extracted from the conventions doc and compared against `CANONICAL_UV_VERSION` (`:31`) alongside `image`/`ci`/`windowsDriver` (`:38-60`), so re-drifting the prose turns that assertion red.
- Related findings: shares the name-only registry check with the registry-columns candidate `R9-T07-4`, whose row is not on disk yet so it is cited by legacy id · nguồn: R9 L12 — development docs and runbooks against shipped gates (retry batch C)

### F-497 — README section 12 and the orchestration README present P1–P10 as implemented at `orchestration/patterns/index.mjs`, a path the shipped gate deliberately keeps nonexistent, so the documented entry point cannot be imported

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations

- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: both files are tracked at HEAD `cd41974` and the promised module resolves to nothing — `node -e import('./integrations/openclaw/orchestration/patterns/index.mjs')` printed `probe: ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, `git ls-files integrations/openclaw/orchestration` returns only `README.md`, and `ls integrations/openclaw/orchestration/patterns` shows an empty untracked directory — while `README.md:576` states the patterns are implemented at that path.

The result is two tracked operator docs presenting a specification as delivered code; the tree stays green precisely because the gate's contract is the opposite of the docs'. Category is F4 because the shipped contract and the documented contract contradict each other.
- Evidence: Where: `README.md:576` — points the reader to `integrations/openclaw/orchestration/patterns/index.mjs` as the implementation location · Where: `README.md:578-587` — enumerates P1 through P10 as delivered patterns · Where: `integrations/openclaw/orchestration/README.md:3` — states the guide follows the source logic of `patterns/index.mjs` in order · Where: `integrations/openclaw/orchestration/README.md:11` — names `integrations/openclaw/orchestration/patterns/index.mjs` as the source of the patterns · Where: `integrations/openclaw/orchestration/README.md:29` — a live sample statement `import { executePattern1_N8nToR2Landing } from "./patterns/index.mjs";` · Where: `tests/agent-topo-F-105.test.mjs:149-174` — the shipped gate that keeps the module deleted and reads no documentation file · Measured: `git ls-files integrations/openclaw/orchestration` → `integrations/openclaw/orchestration/README.md` only; the patterns directory exists but is empty and untracked; the import probe returned `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. · correction measured by R10 (V10, from R10-L11-2): this row credits `tests/agent-topo-F-105.test.mjs:170-174` as a second guarantee, but node:test stops the case body at the first failed assertion, so reaching `:170` already proves `existsSync(legacy) === false` (the same call is asserted at `:158-162`) — the clause is constant-true on every input and the assertion twelve lines above it is the only live one. This row's own claim stands; only its site count is overstated, and the standalone falsifiable version is filed as its own R10 row
- Reproduction: Read-only probe at HEAD `cd41974`: `node -e "import('./integrations/openclaw/orchestration/patterns/index.mjs').then(()=>console.log('IMPORTED')).catch(e=>console.log('probe:',e.code))"` printed `probe: ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, then `git ls-files integrations/openclaw/orchestration` (only `README.md`) and `ls integrations/openclaw/orchestration/patterns` (empty) beside the citations at `README.md:576-587` and `integrations/openclaw/orchestration/README.md:3,11,29`; no gate was run.
- Acceptance criteria: `README.md:574-587` and `integrations/openclaw/orchestration/README.md:3,11,29` stop asserting an implementation location — the `patterns/index.mjs` link and the executable `import` sample are removed or explicitly marked as specification rather than delivered code — and the gate shipped as `tests/agent-topo-F-105.test.mjs` gains an assertion that no tracked doc references `orchestration/patterns/index.mjs`, so reintroducing the reference turns it red; its tree half (`existsSync(legacy)`) and its index half (the `tracked.length === 0` assert that `F-526`/`5b317d2` split out of the old disjunction) are independent, so neither is credited as coverage of the other (was: the `:170-174` disjunction and the `:149-174` credit, which could not redden).
- Related findings: same unguarded-doc family as `F-495` and `F-496` in this batch — each is a tracked doc claim that no shipped gate opens · nguồn: R9 L12 — development docs and runbooks against shipped gates (retry batch C)

### F-500 — register() attaches the governed tool surface before config validation throws, so only the never-set governance store root keeps the half-registered surface inert

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R10-L01-3`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Low

- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:6016-6025` — `registerTools` then the throw · Where: `index.mjs:6032` — `configureGovernedStoreRoot(config.ledgerRoot)` after the throw · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:1272-1278` — `GOVERNANCE_STORE_ROOT_UNCONFIGURED` refusal · Where: `tests/m7-t2-openclaw.test.mjs:1446-1449` — the shipped test asserts the throw only, not registration absence · Measured: `git ls-files` confirms all four paths tracked.
- Reproduction: read-only walk at HEAD: with a relative `ledgerRoot`, `registerTools` executes, the throw aborts `register()` before any hook attaches, and any tool executor touching the governed store throws `GOVERNANCE_STORE_ROOT_UNCONFIGURED`; proving host-side exposure requires the OpenClaw host, outside this repo.
- Acceptance criteria: move the config validation above `registerTools` in `register()` so an invalid deployment registers neither tools nor hooks, and extend `tests/m7-t2-openclaw.test.mjs` to assert `registerTool` was never called when the throw fires; the assertion goes red while the current ordering stands.

### F-501 — a failed WORKFLOW_BRIEF_ACTIVATED durable write is swallowed into a warn with no named refusal, leaving the direct-session brief unactivated and any approval taken in the window bound to the empty node list

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R10-L01-4`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — needs fault injection: requires the journal write to throw at run-end (writer-lock busy or schema refusal); the ledger stays DRAFT and the next direct-session run-end re-drives the activation, so the split is transient-but-silent, not permanent
- Root cause: the run-end activation seam catches its only durable write into a warn log and falls through to completion governance, unlike its reconciling sibling at `index.mjs:3100-3121` which refuses by name on the same class of write failure; the reducer's BRIEF_ACTIVATED arm is the sole writer of `task.nodes`, so a lost write leaves the plan digest binding over an empty node list until a later activation lands.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:9092-9096` — catch into warn, no refusal · Where: `index.mjs:9048` — the DRAFT re-drive condition on every run-end · Where: `index.mjs:9104-9124` — execution continues to `resolveCoordinatorRun` and `guard.beforeRunEnd` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:2026` and `:2044-2048` — the reducer writes `task.nodes` and derives `primary_coordinator` only from this event · Measured: repo grep of `WORKFLOW_BRIEF_ACTIVATED` shows three production producers (`index.mjs:8776`, `index.mjs:9066`, `guard.mjs:1163`), disproving the lane-2 "sole producer" upgrade premise.
- Reproduction: read-only walk plus fault injection: make `recordWorkflowEvent` throw for `WORKFLOW_BRIEF_ACTIVATED` at a direct Main run-end; the handler logs a warn, the run-end completes, the snapshot keeps `brief_state DRAFT` and empty `nodes`, and no `BRIEF_ACTIVATION_FAILED` refusal exists.
- Acceptance criteria: the catch at `index.mjs:9092` returns a named refusal or defers the run-end like the `:3100-3121` sibling, and a shipped test with a faulted `recordWorkflowEvent` asserts the run-end does not proceed silently; the test turns red when the swallow is restored.
- Related findings: F-060 (closed — the COORDINATOR_NOT_EXECUTABLE fail-closure this window re-trips) · sibling shape of R10-T01-1 (durable write swallowed into a log) with a different producer and consequence · nguồn: R10 L01 — zink-governance plugin, R10 V01 — verdict

### F-502 — publishArtifactAtomic keeps the banned adopt-pattern ("w" open, no symlink guard, predictable temp) but is a declared-but-unwired export, so the divergence is LATENT

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R10-L02-1`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: repo-wide grep of `publishArtifactAtomic` returns exactly 1 hit, its own definition at `lib/artifact-validator.mjs:1445`; no importer, no test caller, so no governed publish can adopt a foreign temp file today
- Root cause: the helper truncates a predictable `.tmp-<pid>-<ms>` path with `"w"` and renames it onto the target without `assertNoSymlinkComponents`, the exact policy `queue-manager.mjs:393` and `decision-ledger.mjs:436` replaced with `"wx"` + symlink guard + residue cleanup; the export was never retired nor wired.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs:1456` — predictable temp name · :1458 — `openSync(tempPath, "w", 0o600)` · :1465 — rename onto target · contrast `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:375-393` — the corrected sibling pattern with its own docblock · Measured: predicate grep `publishArtifactAtomic` repo-wide = 1 hit (definition only); `git ls-files` confirms the path tracked
- Reproduction: static at HEAD: read `artifact-validator.mjs:1445-1486` and diff the flags against `queue-manager.mjs:393`; live firing is impossible because no caller exists — that unwired state is the row.
- Acceptance criteria: either delete the export or align it with the `"wx"` + `assertNoSymlinkComponents` + own-temp-removal discipline of `queue-manager.mjs:375-399`, with a test that pre-creates the predictable temp path and asserts refusal — red when the `"wx"` control is reverted.

### F-503 — the power-bi structural gate is ANY-of over a list containing [Content_Types].xml, so every OPC package (renamed xlsx/docx/pptx) passes as a valid Power BI artifact

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R10-L02-2`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: any governed result typed `powerbi` (extension-derived at `artifact-validator.mjs:364`, tier at `:742-778`) whose file is any ZIP/OOXML package records `powerbiStructureValid: true` and reaches the result gate with no error
- Root cause: the tier uses the ANY-of helper `inspectZipContainsEntries` (`:163-170`, `requiredKeywords.some`) and seeds its keyword list with `[Content_Types].xml` (`:768`), a part mandated by the OPC spec for every Office document, so the structural check is satisfied by the wrong-format container itself; the stricter ALL-of helper sits 10 lines below in the same file and is used by the docx tier instead.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs:763-778` — ANY-of call, keyword list, single-failure branch · :163-170 — `some` semantics with name/prefix matching · :172-174 — `inspectZipContainsAllEntries` unused by this tier · Measured: executed `validateTechnicalArtifact` read-only against a scratch zip in the temp dir containing only `[Content_Types].xml` → `status: PASS, powerbiStructureValid: true, errors: []`; the same list under ALL-of → false · Predicate grep `POWERBI|powerbi|Power BI` in `docs/execution/findings.md` = 0 hits · folded in from `R10-T08-2` (V11): the verdict is consumed as format identity, not just as a pass — `checks.format = "POWERBI"` is stamped at `integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs:751` and then digest-stamped — and V11 executed the repository's own workbook through the gate, which matches the ANY-of on `[Content_Types].xml` alone
- Reproduction: `node --input-type=module -e` importing the shipped validator and calling `validateTechnicalArtifact` with `expectedType` powerbi on any minimal zip holding a `[Content_Types].xml` entry (or a renamed xlsx fixture) — returns PASS; no live state touched.
- Acceptance criteria: the powerbi branch requires PBIX-specific parts conjunctively via `inspectZipContainsAllEntries` (e.g. DataMashup AND Report/Layout) with `[Content_Types].xml` removed from the accepted set, falsified by a test asserting a renamed OPC package now FAILs with the powerbi structure error — red when the conjunction is reverted to `some`. and, per the folded `R10-T08-2` (V11), the EVERY-of set must be over distinguishing parts (`Report/Layout` with `DataModel` or `DataMashup`), `[Content_Types].xml` must leave every existential list repo-wide, and the test must refuse the repository's own generated workbook as Power BI while a PBIX-shaped archive passes
- Related findings: F-469 (stale evidence digest on the same validator, different mechanism) · the xlsx content rows F-465/F-473 are content-of-parts, not required-parts · carries the merged candidate `R10-T08-2` (same mechanism, one fix site) · nguồn: R10 V02 — execution probe

### F-504 — the queue result record is generation-sticky (resultArtifacts never cleared, rebuildFromLedger override preserves it) but the only production finish caller cannot deliver the empty set that strands it

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R10-L02-3`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: the finish at `index.mjs:3037` only runs with a non-empty artifact set because the sole caller refuses `artifacts.length === 0` at `index.mjs:9907` and the derivation loop fails closed at `:2938-2971`, and the accept leg is separately blocked by F-402 at `queue-manager.mjs:1040-1046`
- Root cause: `finishExecution` writes `resultArtifacts`/`artifactSetDigest` only under a non-empty-array guard (`queue-manager.mjs:928-933`) and nothing in the module ever clears them; `rebuildFromLedger` (`:1267-1277`) overrides the projected status in place without invalidating the prior result record or bumping `attempt`, so the `resultAttempt !== attempt` fence at `:1022-1029` cannot fire for a superseded generation carried across a re-queue.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:928-933` — conditional-only write · :1267-1277 — status override touching no result field · :1022-1029 — the attempt fence that never sees a bumped attempt on this path · Measured: grep `resultArtifacts|artifactSetDigest` in queue-manager.mjs = 3 hits, all writes, zero clears · Measured: grep `handleExecutionFinished|finishExecution(` in index.mjs = definition :2832, call :3037, sole external call :9913 · Where: `index.mjs:9907` — the empty-set refusal guarding the seam
- Reproduction: static two-generation walk at HEAD: the strand needs a `finishExecution` with `artifactSet` empty or omitted after a `rebuildFromLedger` re-queue; traced every caller — the plugin finish path filters empty sets before the call, so the sequence cannot be produced without adding a second caller.
- Acceptance criteria: every result generation fully replaces or clears `resultArtifacts`/`artifactSetDigest` (write moved out of the length guard), and the `rebuildFromLedger` override invalidates the prior result record or bumps `attempt`; a two-generation test (finish, ledger re-queue, finish with empty set) asserting `resultArtifacts` is emptied — red when the clear is removed.
- Related findings: F-402 (accept-leg guard on this seam today) · F-405 (different split: ledger COMPLETE vs queue review) · F-406 (terminal vocabulary, adjacent not same) · F-469 (validator-side stamp order, different mechanism) · nguồn: R10 V02 — disposition settlement of the L02-1/L02-3 conflict pair

### F-505 — buildCoordinatorMessage byte-cuts the JSON bundle mid-instructions for oversized briefs, degrading coordinator prompt integrity with no test asserting parseability

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R10-L02-4`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: an approved brief longer than roughly 3.9 KB crosses the 4096-byte ceiling (`coordinator-pickup.mjs:556`) and the slice at `:627-632` emits unterminated JSON; identity fields are safe because the serializer orders `lease_id`/`attempt` before `brief` (`:613-621`)
- Root cause: the budget is applied by cutting raw bytes off the serialized string instead of truncating `brief` first and re-serializing, so the tail (end of `brief` plus all three `instructions` entries, including the anti-escalation line at `:624`) disappears silently; the module itself documents that nothing parses the text (`:595-598`), which caps the harm at prompt integrity rather than authority.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/coordinator-pickup.mjs:613-632` — field order and byte-slice truncation · :556 — 4096 ceiling · :595-598 — no-downstream-reader docblock · Where: `plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:3650` — advisory-text disclaimer · Measured: predicate grep `buildCoordinatorMessage` = 1 production caller (`:699`) + 1 definition + 2 test sites; read `tests/m10-t2-r19-artifact-and-message-limits.test.mjs:136-179` — asserts only byte length and surrogate safety, never JSON.parse
- Reproduction: read-only: `node --input-type=module -e` importing the module and calling `buildCoordinatorMessage` with a 5000-character brief — the returned string fails JSON.parse and no longer contains the instruction text, while `Buffer.byteLength` stays under the ceiling.
- Acceptance criteria: truncate `brief` to fit and re-serialize so the bundle always parses and always carries all three instructions; a test asserting `JSON.parse(buildCoordinatorMessage(...))` succeeds and contains every instruction for a 20 KB brief — red when the pre-serialize truncation is removed.
- Related findings: same declared-guarantee drift family as the R10-L06-3 candidate · no live register row names `buildCoordinatorMessage` or `COORDINATOR_MESSAGE_MAX` (verified 0 hits) · nguồn: R10 V02 — field-order verification of the T02 disposition

### F-506 — the preflight ingress predicate published to one scope-less process global is never retracted by the unregister closure, so a released or second-registered guard keeps deciding Discord admission for every ledger root

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R10-L03-1`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the patched host bundle calls the global on every preflight at `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/native-patch.mjs:532`, while registration, unregistration and the documented fallthrough all live in `native-boundary.mjs`
- Root cause: `registerNativeGuard` publishes one closure over the scoped entry into `globalThis[preflightIngressKey]` (`:75`, `:95-99`) but the returned unregister deletes only the registry map entry (`:76-78`), and because the published value carries no scope, a second `registerNativeGuard` for a different ledger root overwrites the first root's predicate instead of partitioning it.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/native-boundary.mjs:74-78` — set/publish then unregister-without-retraction · Where: `:95-99` — assignment-only publish, no clear branch anywhere · Where: `:90-93` — the contract the mechanism violates · Where: `:107`, `:111-114` — registry keyed by raw config string with a normalization fallback that the published global cannot use · Measured: grep for `preflightIngressKey` over all tracked `.mjs` in `integrations/` returns only the symbol definition and the single assignment; no `delete globalThis`, no null reassignment, at HEAD. · folded in from `R10-T04-4` (V04): the never-retracted-predicate half, with V04's corrected census 11/11/46 and the real stop caller `index.mjs:9198` (the `clearScopeLeases` site at `:6086-6090`)
- Reproduction: read-only static trace suffices; fault-injection variant: import `native-boundary`, `registerNativeGuard(rootA, guardA)` exposing `beforePreflightIngress`, run the returned unregister, then assert `globalThis[Symbol.for("zink.governance.native.preflightIngress")]` is undefined — it is still guardA's closure, and `native-patch.mjs:532` keeps invoking it.
- Acceptance criteria: the unregister closure must clear (or re-point to the surviving scope) the global it published, and the published value must be per-scope (a Map keyed by the resolved root, or the scope passed to the seam and checked against `registry()`); a two-scope test must show each root resolves its own guard and that after the last unregister the predicate is absent — red at HEAD.
- Related findings: R10-T04-4 (cross-module generalisation, same root cause, folded here) · R10-T04-3 (unscoped store-root global, sibling mechanism) · F-042 precedent at the lease seam · carries the merged candidate `R10-T04-4` (same mechanism, one fix site) · nguồn: R10 V03 — native-boundary lifecycle

### F-507 — r16-rehearsal.mjs waives its `--run`/`--confirm` gate on the plain environment variable `R16_INSIDE=1` and then recursively deletes the ambient `R16_REHEARSAL_ROOT`, so the confirmation that protects the delete has no effect in the mode that deletes

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R10-L04-1`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: High
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: `git grep` over 580 tracked files yields exactly four `R16_INSIDE` hits — the two reads at `:158`/`:213`, the driver's own child env at `:291`, and `tests/m8-t2.13-r16-apply-rehearsal.test.mjs:1345` which DELETES it — plus `R16-DRIVER-01` pinning that no `test:` script names the driver, so firing needs an operator-exported value no repo artifact produces
- Root cause: the namespace child's authority is a string the operator environment can carry (`:158`, `:213`), so when it is present the whole gate block `:168-194` is skipped and `teardownInsideNamespace()` reaches `rmSync(rehearsalRoot, { recursive: true, force: true })` at `:252` with no nonce, argv flag, parent-pid or namespace identity ever consulted; the target still resolves from `--rehearsal-root` or the ambient `R16_REHEARSAL_ROOT` (`:85`) and `:99-104` excludes only the filesystem root and the home directory itself.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/r16-rehearsal.mjs:154-195` — the gate comment and the `process.env.R16_INSIDE !== "1"` wrapper around the `--run` and `--confirm` refusals · Where: `…:213-231` — the child branch runs the suite then `teardownInsideNamespace()` and exits · Where: `…:251-252` — `rmSync(rehearsalRoot, { recursive: true, force: true, maxRetries: 5 })` · Where: `…:99-104` — the only two target exclusions, filesystem root and `path.resolve(os.homedir())` · Where: `…:85` — the delete target is taken from the ambient `R16_REHEARSAL_ROOT` · Where: `…:288-292` — the real parent relaunch passes `R16_INSIDE: "1"` with no argv and no nonce · Where: `tests/m8-t2.13-r16-apply-rehearsal.test.mjs:1339-1351` — `runDriver` deletes `R16_INSIDE` and `R16_REHEARSAL_ROOT` from the child env · Where: `tests/m8-t2.13-r16-apply-rehearsal.test.mjs:1353-1371` — `R16-DRIVER-01` asserts no `test:` script names the driver · Measured: `git grep -n "R16_INSIDE"` returned 4 tracked hits (2 reads, 1 writer, 1 test scrub); `package.json:27` is the only script naming the driver and it is named `rehearse:r16`; `tests/helpers/r16-fingerprint.mjs:20-24` puts the live state root inside the home directory, so it is an admissible target
- Reproduction: read-only proof at HEAD: no nonce, argv flag or parent identity appears anywhere between `:158` and `:252`, and `:99-104` excludes only two paths. Firing it on the real host needs `R16_INSIDE=1` exported plus the WSL and live-runtime preconditions at `:197-209`, so the fault-injection was not executed by this lane.
- Acceptance criteria: the exempt child mode is entered only on a capability the operator environment cannot carry — a per-run nonce passed by the parent that the child requires in addition to `R16_INSIDE`, and a child that deletes only a root whose `live-fingerprint.json` (`:128`) matches that nonce — so a bare `R16_INSIDE=1` invocation refuses, and the `R16-DRIVER-*` lane turns red if the nonce check is removed.
- Related findings: the same ambient-variable-outranks-intent family as F-486 (an exported variable silently outranking the intended root), inverted direction · `R16-DRIVER-01` (`tests/m8-t2.13-r16-apply-rehearsal.test.mjs:1353`) is the control that keeps it LATENT · nguồn: R10 V04 — operator drivers and the R16 rehearsal gate

### F-508 — both orphan-lock recovery drivers run their destructive `unlinkSync`/`rmdirSync` outside every error path, so a mid-delete failure emits no JSON record at all and prints a raw stack containing the absolute lock path

- Category: F6 — Reliability & Performance
- Legacy register id: `R10-L04-2`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), Zink Skill Store
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the same concurrent acquisition F-433 already declares reachable is what makes `rmdirSync` throw `ENOTEMPTY`, and no `try` encloses `operator-lock-recovery.mjs:175-176` or the call at `zink-skill-store/bin/skill-store-lock-recovery.mjs:215`
- Root cause: each driver's only report channel is a single JSON write reached after the removal succeeds (`operator-lock-recovery.mjs:98-101` and `:177-181`; `skill-store-lock-recovery.mjs:213-218`), while the destructive pair sits at top level after the last surrounding `try` (`:103-109` and `:125-134` in the OpenClaw driver, `:203-212` in the Skill Store CLI), so any filesystem error raised by `unlinkSync`/`rmdirSync` escapes as an uncaught exception: no record, non-zero exit with a Node stack naming the lock path, and no truthful `mutated` value.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-lock-recovery.mjs:175-181` — `unlinkSync(ownerPath); rmdirSync(lock); report.mutated = true; … finish(...)` with no `try` · Where: `…:98-101` — `finish()` is the only writer of the JSON record · Where: `…:103-109` and `…:125-134` — the only two `try` blocks in the flow, both before the gate · Where: `…:1-21` — the file's own contract: "Output is one JSON line of derived facts. The holder nonce is **omitted**, not masked" · Where: `zink-skill-store/bin/skill-store-lock-recovery.mjs:192-193` and `:203-218` — `unlinkSync`/`rmdirSync` inside `recoverSkillStoreLock`, whose CLI caller wraps only `parseArguments` in `try` · Measured: read the whole `90-181` window of the OpenClaw driver and the `140-218` window of the Skill Store driver at HEAD; the shape-check at `:137-146`, the liveness refusal at `:149-160` and the confirmation gate at `:161-173` all exist, so this row is about the failure path only
- Reproduction: read-only against a scratch `--state-dir`: start the driver with `--recover --confirm=ORPHAN_LOCK_RECOVERY` while a second process recreates `<root>/writer.lock/owner.json` immediately after the driver's `unlinkSync` returns, so `rmdirSync` raises `ENOTEMPTY`; observe a stack trace on stderr and an empty stdout where the contract promises exactly one JSON line.
- Acceptance criteria: wrap the removal in an error path that still calls the record writer, so a failed `unlinkSync`/`rmdirSync` emits exactly one JSON line with the true `mutated`/`recovered` values and no raw error text or absolute path, in both `operator-lock-recovery.mjs:175-181` and `skill-store-lock-recovery.mjs:192-193` plus its CLI caller; a lane reddens if the `try` around the removal is removed.
- Related findings: second site of F-433 for the read-once-then-delete half (that half is NOT filed here) · F-489 is this same driver's unrelated default-root mechanism · `tests/m12-t2-r4-orphan-lock-recovery.test.mjs:52` is the only lane that names the driver · nguồn: R10 V04 — operator drivers and the R16 rehearsal gate
