### F-515 — the embedding arms of search and readyz collapse the seven-code ModelProbeError vocabulary into one constant with no log record, so a permanent provider refusal is indistinguishable from a timeout

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R10-L07-4`
- Affected modules: Zink Knowledgebase
- Severity: Medium
- Status: PARTIAL — the service half is closed by `cb812ad`: both embedding arms carry the raised `ModelProbeError` code through `_embedding_error_code` (a code-shaped message only, so no upstream sentence is echoed) and the search arm records `code=` before raising. The criterion's companion proof is NOT shipped: `zink-tools/adapters/knowledge/knowledge-adapter.mjs:177` still accepts only an ARRAY `detail`, so the repaired object code is dropped one hop later, and the row it names for that seam (`F-551`, `R10-T06-3`) is open. The row stays until that adapter reader and its cross-module test land (2026-10-24).
- Root cause: the two embedding `except` arms name the caught error and then discard its `code`, emitting the constant `EMBEDDING_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE`; the same file already carries the correct pattern for the reranker seam. Nothing records the original code either, because the search route logs only inside its generic arm and `except HTTPException: raise` short-circuits ahead of it.
- Evidence: Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:528-530` — `code = error.code if error.code == "EMBEDDING_MODEL_DRIFT" else "EMBEDDING_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE"` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:531-532` — `except (httpx.HTTPError, ModelProbeError) as error:` raises 503 with the constant and never reads `error.code` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:399-400` — the same flattening on `/readyz` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/model_probe.py:63, 82, 85, 97, 101, 164, 187, 190, 194, 198, 200` — the raised codes · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:691-692` and `:699` — the HTTPException re-raise bypasses the only search-route logger · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:156-170` — the reranker seam that already carries the raised code, with its F-310 rationale comment at `:159` · Measured: `grep -o "EMBEDDING_[A-Z_]*" apps/knowledge/app/model_probe.py | sort -u` = 7 literals, 6 of them not the flattened constant · Measured: `grep -n "logger\." apps/knowledge/app/main.py` = 5 hits, none on an embedding arm
- Reproduction: Read-only: enumerate the `ModelProbeError(...)` raise sites and the two arms that swallow them. To force it: make Ollama answer the embeddings call with 400 and read the `/v1/search` body — `{"detail":{"code":"EMBEDDING_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE"}}` with no log line, identical to a timeout. The governed route additionally shows `error: HTTP_503` with no code at all, because `zink-tools/adapters/knowledge/knowledge-adapter.mjs:177` only summarises an ARRAY `detail`.
- Acceptance criteria: the 503 body carries the raised `ModelProbeError.code` (or the handler logs `code=<error.code>` before raising), by reusing the `_reranker_error_code` shape at `main.py:156-170`. A test that forces `EMBEDDING_PERMANENT_HTTP` and asserts the response or the log distinguishes it from `EMBEDDING_TRANSPORT` is red today; a companion test at the adapter must assert the code survives, since `knowledge-adapter.mjs:177` accepts only array `detail` and would drop any repaired code.
- Related findings: R10-T06-3 (the array-only reader that nullifies the code one hop later — same seam, must be fixed together) · reranker code-preservation root cause documented at `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:159` (the id that file cites is not resolvable in the register or CHANGELOG) · F-441 shares the "one code, several causes" shape · nguồn: R10 V06 — Knowledgebase embedding error attribution

### F-520 — the compose reconciliation loop drops unparseable overlay files without a failure and still reports the repository-policy check as passed

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R10-L09-3`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI, infrastructure scripts
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: every tracked compose overlay parses today, so no file is being dropped; the fire condition is one future overlay edit that breaks YAML in a file that declares no unique shared network
- Root cause: `checkSharedNetworkDeclaration` treats its own enumerated inputs as advisory (parse errors `continue`, throw becomes an empty network map) while treating the declaration file as mandatory, so a broken consumer file is silently removed from the denominator that `compareSharedNetworks` reconciles.
- Evidence: Where: `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:1622` — `if (document.errors.length > 0) continue;` inside the compose-directory loop · Where: `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:1624-1626` — `catch { composeNetworks[name] = {}; }`, the same silent treatment · Where: `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:1586-1591`, `:1593-1597`, `:1599-1604` — the fail-loud treatment of the declaration file in the same function · Where: `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:1538-1557` — the partial backstop: a dropped file loses its `consumed` entry, so only a sole-declared network turns the silence into a failure · Measured: predicate `git grep -In "parseDocument|readdirSync" -- tests` finds no lane that walks `infrastructure/docker/compose/`; `infrastructure/docker/scripts/preflight.ps1:101-105` runs `docker compose config --quiet` over core plus knowledge only
- Reproduction: read-only: feed `checkSharedNetworkDeclaration` a scratch repository whose `compose` directory holds a valid `compose.core.yml` consuming `platform` plus an overlay with a duplicate key or a stray tab; the returned failure list is empty because `platform` is still consumed by the core file.
- Acceptance criteria: `checkSharedNetworkDeclaration` pushes one failure per unparseable or throwing file in the enumerated directory, mirroring the wording at `:1586-1591`, and a case in `tests/repository-policy.test.mjs` feeding a broken overlay expects a non-empty failure list; removing the new push turns that case red.
- Related findings: family neighbour of OPEN F-477 and OPEN F-492 (a check that cannot see the state it claims to police); not the same root cause as either · nguồn: R10 V08 — infra, CI and gates

### F-521 — the tracked-file secret guard matches the basename .env exactly so the .env.* family that .gitignore declares secret-bearing passes repo:check by name

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R10-L09-5`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: the population is empty today, measured as 2 tracked files matching `.env` (`git ls-files | grep -iE '(^|/)\.env'` = `.env.example` and `infrastructure/docker/env/.env.example`, both template-allowlisted at `:830-838`), and reaching the gap needs a force-added `.env.production` whose content also misses every gitleaks rule
- Root cause: `isSensitivePath` is an exact-equality name test, so it covers only the basename `.env` while the repository's own ignore rules classify the wider `.env.*` family as machine-specific secret material; the guard and the ignore declaration were never reconciled.
- Evidence: Where: `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:843` — `name === ".env" ||`, the entire family clause · Where: `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:850-861` — `checkTrackedFilePolicies` filters only through `isSensitivePath`, so a false predicate means no failure · Where: `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:824-838` — the fixture and template allowlists that already anticipate a wider name set · Where: `.gitignore:2-5` — `.env`, `.env.*`, `**/.env`, `**/.env.*` under the comment "Secrets and machine-specific environment files" · Measured: `runGit … check-ignore` appears once in the whole file (`:1129`) against four hardcoded synthetic paths, so no other rule refuses a tracked-but-ignored file
- Reproduction: read-only: call the exported `checkTrackedFilePolicies([".env.production", "config/key.pem", "keys/id_ed25519"])` — the second is refused and the first returns no failure, which is the asymmetry, and staging is never attempted.
- Acceptance criteria: `isSensitivePath` accepts the `.env.` prefixed family except the names `isAllowedSensitiveTemplate` already allows, and the existing policy lane asserts that `.env.production` is refused while `.env.example` stays allowed; removing the family clause turns the new case red.
- Related findings: same "recorded control covers less than the prose says" shape as OPEN F-483; no live row names `isSensitivePath` · nguồn: R10 V08 — infra, CI and gates

### F-522 — start.ps1 creates the live stack with none of the credential-sentinel checks preflight.ps1 exists to make, so the guard holds only if the operator voluntarily runs the other script first

- Root cause: the sentinel grammar lives only in `preflight.ps1`, which no script, gate or CI lane calls, and `start.ps1` re-implements none of it, so the mutating path validates only env-file existence and vault shape while the documented order between the two scripts is prose with no enforcement.

- Reproduction: static read plus a documented-order probe: with an env file copied from `infrastructure/docker/env/.env.example` unchanged, walk `start.ps1:55-71` and observe that no clause tests a sentinel value, then walk `preflight.ps1:70-75` on the same file and observe the throw — the two scripts disagree on the same input. No script was executed.
- Acceptance criteria: `start.ps1`, or a helper it dot-sources, refuses a missing or sentinel `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` and the `N8N_*` set before the first `up`, and the docker contract lane `tests/m5-docker-contract.test.mjs` asserts that call exists so removing it turns that lane red.

### F-525 — the junction-escape case calls root-level `test.skip()` inside its own body, so the escape case is counted a pass while its four containment asserts never run

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R10-L11-1`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: the branch needs `fs.symlinkSync(..., "junction")` to throw, which measured false on this host (junction created in `%TEMP%`) and cannot fire on the ubuntu CI runner where Node ignores the `type` argument; the file's own contract at `tests/agent-lib-F-044.test.mjs:13-14` is nonetheless violated whenever it does fire
- Root cause: the case callback takes no `t` (`:39`), so the guard at `:47-53` calls the root-level `test.skip(name)` API, which node:test schedules as a NEW root case; the running case then hits the bare `return` at `:53` and is reported passed with `:69-83` unexecuted.
- Evidence: Where: `tests/agent-lib-F-044.test.mjs:39` — case body with no `t` parameter · `:47-53` — `if (!makeJunction(outside, junction)) { test.skip("the platform refuses to create a directory junction, so the escape case is NOT RUN here"); return; }` · `:69-83` — the four containment asserts behind that return · `:13-14` — the vacuous-pass prohibition the file writes for itself · Measured: probe in `%TEMP%` under `node --test` v24.15.0 printed `tests 5 / pass 3 / fail 1 / skipped 1` with `✔ phantom-skip-parent` and a separate `# SKIP` sibling · Measured: `node --test tests/agent-lib-F-044.test.mjs` on this host gives `pass 2 / fail 0 / skipped 0` (V04), and my own junction probe returned OK · Measured: `grep -c '^// SKIP-FAMILY: ' tests/agent-lib-F-044.test.mjs` = 0, and `skip-expectation-gate.mjs:191-199` refuses an unexplained skip on every host
- Reproduction: read-only: `node -e` in `%TEMP%` calling `fs.symlinkSync(target, link, "junction")` to show this host takes the other branch, then the four-case `node --test` probe above to show the runner credits a case that returns as a pass and registers the root `test.skip()` as an unrelated skipped sibling; the shipped lane must not be executed for this (it creates real junctions)
- Acceptance criteria: change `:39` to take `t` and `:50` to `t.skip(<reason>)` (or declare `{ skip: ... }`), so that forcing `makeJunction` to return false turns the report line for "a workbook target under a junction inside the sandbox is refused" from pass to skip and removes the phantom case name from the report
- Related findings: same skip-idiom class as the closed `R1-A12-02` (CHANGELOG:1743-1744, `5587bae`, which touched only `tests/m6-t1-skill-store.test.mjs`, so this is a new site and not a regression of that commit) · a zero-assert PASS site of live `F-492` · the register's `F-044` citation does not resolve; cite `tests/agent-lib-F-044.test.mjs:1` · nguồn: R10 L11 — test validity, agent-* half · confirmed by V10 with regraded severity

### F-529 — the approval card's only durable anchor is written best-effort, and all three guards that depend on it skip instead of refusing, so a delivered card can be permanently unbound and unclocked while publish reports PUBLISHED

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R10-T01-1`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow topology), Governance docs
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: any throw of the SURFACED write (writer-lock busy, schema maxLength refusal, digest-chain mismatch) or any journal read error in the unlocked `readFileSync` at `index.mjs:312-314` leaves a delivered, actionable card with no durable record, and both layers then skip their guards instead of refusing
- Root cause: three guards (plugin 24h clock, plugin thread-match, reducer thread-match) share one anchor field projected solely from `WORKFLOW_APPROVAL_SURFACED`, and every guard is written as "check if recorded" instead of "require the record", while the producer swallows the record's write into a log and still returns PUBLISHED; the reducer carries no second clock, so the plugin-side skip is not backstopped anywhere.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:7168-7179` — try/catch around the SURFACED write, error-only logging, then `return { status: "PUBLISHED" }` after confirmed delivery at `:7153-7155` · Where: `index.mjs:2426-2431` — clock skipped when `approvalExpiresAt === null` · Where: `index.mjs:2450-2452` — thread guard runs only when `approval_thread_id` is recorded · Where: `index.mjs:305-332` — `readSurfacedApprovalPostedAt` returns null on any read error or missing record · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:1158` and `:1174` — the reducer's mirror thread guard is gated on `Object.hasOwn(payload, "thread_id")` and on the same `task.approval_thread_id` slot · Where: `workflow.mjs:1332` — sole writer of `approval_thread_id`, reached only from the SURFACED arm `:1309-1333` · Measured: `workflow.mjs:1150-1229` read whole — no time predicate; `tests/agent-owner-F-123.test.mjs` writes SURFACED at :74 in every handler fixture, so no shipped test fails when the record is absent.
- Reproduction: static at HEAD plus the shipped harness: replay `tests/agent-owner-F-123.test.mjs` with the SURFACED record omitted from the ledger fixture (or with `data.threadId` pointing at a different thread) and the APPROVE is still admitted and written at `index.mjs:2486` — the clock guard is skipped at `:2429` and both thread guards at `:2452` and `workflow.mjs:1174` never enter.
- Acceptance criteria: make the record load-bearing — (a) a failed SURFACED write at `index.mjs:7168` returns `BLOCKED` instead of logging (mirror the reconcile at `:3100-3121`), and (b) APPROVE is refused when `approval_requested_at` exists but no readable surfaced record does, inverting `index.mjs:2452` and `workflow.mjs:1174` to require the record; the shipped test SURFACED-absent plus APPROVE must then deny, and turns red when either inversion is reverted.
- Related findings: F-399 (same violated invariant, first producer site — abandoned late-settlement returning BLOCKED; this is the second producer, confirmed delivery returning PUBLISHED) · F-398 (the reducer-side absence of any second clock — the backstop this row's skip lacks) · F-392 (same guard, opposite defect — trio-wide over-denial) · absorbs R10-L01-1 as its consumer half plus the read-error trigger · nguồn: R10 T01 — owner-verdict integrity, R10 V01 — verdict

### F-530 — the F-023 plan binding compares a digest to a fresh re-derivation of the same ledger record, and the surfaced card is an input to neither operand, so a stale or plan-divergent APPROVE always passes

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R10-T01-2`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow topology), Zink Tools (workflow contracts)
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the empty-nodes card renders model-authored `spawnInputs` text while binding a constant empty-list digest, and any node-graph change between surfacing and clicking re-targets the consent because both operands are re-derived at decision time; the text verdict path carries no `brief_revision`, so the one real staleness check is skipped
- Root cause: `scope_digest` is derived from the ledger task at verdict time (`index.mjs:2471-2476`) and compared against a re-derivation of that same projected task in the reducer (`workflow.mjs:1214-1222`), so the check is true by construction; no durable record of the bytes the owner was shown (the SURFACED/REQUESTED payload) participates in either operand, and the schema only requires the member's presence, never its provenance.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:2471-2476` and `:2498` — digest over the snapshot task, written as `scope_digest` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:1214-1222` — `RESULT_MISMATCH` comparison against the same projected task · Where: `index.mjs:1967-1977` — empty-steps card body renders `spawnInputs` (model-authored) outside any binding · Where: `index.mjs:10544-10559` — text-path `data` carries no `briefRevision`; repo grep of `briefRevision` finds button-card embedding at `:6995-7005` and nothing in the text path · Where: `workflow.mjs:1200-1213` — the only non-tautological check, skipped when the member is absent · Measured: all paths tracked via `git ls-files`.
- Reproduction: static at HEAD with a mutation probe: rewrite the snapshot task's `nodes` after the card is surfaced, then take the text-path APPROVE — `index.mjs:2471` re-derives over the new nodes, `workflow.mjs:1216` matches, and the decision is admitted against a plan the owner never saw; deleting the fallback at `index.mjs:1967-1977` does not turn any test red.
- Acceptance criteria: persist the surfaced plan digest in the `WORKFLOW_APPROVAL_SURFACED` (or REQUESTED) payload and require `payload.scope_digest` to equal that recorded digest instead of a fresh re-derivation, and require `brief_revision` on every new approval (drop the undefined escape at `workflow.mjs:1200-1203`); a test approving with a digest naming a plan other than the surfaced one turns red when the recorded-digest comparison is reverted.
- Related findings: F-396 (same advertised-binding-not-enforced family on the result arm — persist-digest-then-compare is the shared fix shape) · F-398 (the freshness dimension of the same consent binding) · F-392/F-043/F-123 (the guard this row's missing anchor shares with R10-T01-1) · nguồn: R10 T01 — owner-verdict integrity, R10 V01 — verdict

### F-532 — execution_finished presents the queue record's own fence fields to finishExecution, so the finish fence compares the queue to itself and a superseded run's result commits under the live attempt

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R10-T02-1`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: after stale recovery bumps `attempt` and re-issues `leaseId`, a zombie run of attempt N emits `execution_finished`; the handler copies attempt N+1's identity from `coordinated.queueItem` and `finishExecution` matches it against its own record, recording run N's artifacts as the live attempt's `RESULT_PENDING_REVIEW`
- Root cause: the finish seam takes `agentId`/`leaseId`/`attempt` from the queue item being verified instead of from the run's ledger binding, so the three-way M7-029 fence is a self-comparison; the intended backstop at the plugin (`activeClaims` guard) reads a property the queue manager never declares anywhere, making its refusals unreachable dead code.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:9913-9926` — the fence fields copied from `queueItem` (`:9889` `coordinated.queueItem`) · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:902-916` — `AGENT_MISMATCH`/`LEASE_MISMATCH`/`ATTEMPT_MISMATCH` compared against the same record inside the lock · Where: `index.mjs:2894-2915` — the `activeClaims` guard · Where: `index.mjs:9870-9872` — `resolveCoordinatorRun` with `requireBinding` proves the run's own binding is available and unused · Measured: repo-wide grep of `activeClaims` returned exactly 2 hits, both reads at `:2894-2895`, zero declarations; correct contrast pattern is the binding-sourced fence the topology guard uses.
- Reproduction: static at HEAD: read the two cited ranges and trace the values — the presented and the compared operands originate from the same `getTask` record; a live repro needs attempt N+1 re-claim plus a late `execution_finished` from run N, which F-401's unwired recovery currently makes hard to trigger, so the fence being vacuous is proven by construction, not by a firing denial.
- Acceptance criteria: pass the BINDING's `coordinator`/`lease_id`/`attempt` into `finishExecution` (the pattern the guard module already follows — the lease and the attempt come from the binding, not the caller), wrap the finish in the execution fence, delete the dead `activeClaims` block, and ship a case that claims at attempt N, recovers to N+1, delivers run N's finish, and asserts refusal — red while the self-comparison stands.
- Related findings: F-407 (same seam, expiry never checked — distinct mechanism) · F-401 (the unwired recovery that bumps the attempt this fence should deny) · F-405 (the accept-side split downstream) · nguồn: R10 T02 — execution lane after APPROVE, R10 V01 — verdict

### F-536 — the two shipped `default` keyword nodes make `zink_sql.max_rows` and `zink_knowledge.top_k` unsatisfiable, so every governed call that sends a published optional input is refused pre-dispatch

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R10-T03-1`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), Zink Tools (workflow contracts), OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `guard.mjs:1892` validates the live `tool-call` envelope in `beforeToolCall` (per the F-456 refutation, that seam is dispatched in production), and `index.mjs:4092` publishes each tool's `$defs.input` to the host as its `parameters`
- Root cause: the topology interpreter treats `KEYWORDS` as an admission list checked against every schema node (`schema.mjs:56-92`, `:275`), and `default` is JSON Schema vocabulary the host engine accepts but the repo engine rejects; because properties recurse only over present keys, the refusal fires exactly when a caller sends the documented optional field.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/schema.mjs:56-92` — frozen `KEYWORDS` with no `default` · Where: `:269-276` — unknown-keyword `invalid()` · Where: `zink-tools/contracts/tools/zink-sql.schema.json:70-74` and `zink-knowledge.schema.json:51` — the only 2 shipped `default` nodes (measured walk over all 25 tracked `*.schema.json`) · Where: `zink-tools/contracts/tool-call.schema.json:63-74` — oneOf arm `tool_id const zink_sql` with `input` bound to the sql `$defs/input` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:2783` — approval material-scope binds `input.max_rows`, a field that cannot be sent · Measured: executed probes — sql `$defs/input` without `max_rows` VALID, with `max_rows:10` INVALID SCHEMA_VALIDATION_FAILED; knowledge `$defs/input` without `top_k` VALID, with `top_k:5` INVALID.
- Reproduction: read-only, no state touched — `node --input-type=module -e` importing `./schema.mjs` from `topology/lib` and running `checkSchema(loadContractSchema("zink-sql").$defs.input, {query:"SELECT 1",target_database:"analytics_read",max_rows:10})` → throws SCHEMA_VALIDATION_FAILED while the identical object minus the key passes.

- Related findings: F-450 and F-455 (same effect class — published contract refuses the published shape — different mechanism, `$defs.parameter` contradiction vs `KEYWORDS` admission; cross-referenced, not merged) · F-456 (REFUTED row whose refutation is this row's reachability warrant) · R10-T03-3 (missing dialect gate is the shared root) · R10-L03-2 folded there · nguồn: R10 V03 — contract-vs-interpreter

### F-537 — three canonical, digest-pinned contracts have no validation call site anywhere and the published manifest-verifier wrapper is never called, so byte-pinning certifies content nothing checks

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R10-T03-2`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), OpenClaw governance plugin, deployment topology
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the three admission digests are maintained in the shipped `configure.mjs` today, so operators believe a governance check exists for artifacts that no code path validates
- Root cause: publication (membership in `CANONICAL_CONTRACTS`, an admission digest, a manifest hash) and validation (a `validateContract`/`validateContractSchema` call site or a `$ref` from a validated parent) are two disconnected registries with no gate relating them; a canonical name can therefore stay pinned while losing every consumer.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/schema.mjs:22-35` — the three names in the canonical list · Where: `integrations/openclaw/configure.mjs:331`, `:353`, `:436` — live admission digests for `contracts/governance-manifest.schema.json`, `contracts/incident.schema.json`, `contracts/result.schema.json` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:4119-4121` — the wrapper definition, its only in-repo occurrence · Measured: predicate `grep -rn 'validateContract("<name>"|validateContractSchema("<name>"'` plus a `$ref` walk over all 25 contracts → 0 hits each for the three names; enforced-half spot checks re-measured at `ledger.mjs:1159`, `:1538` (ledger-event) and `guard.mjs:821` (approval-request). · folded in from `R10-L04-3` (V04): the `governed-reference` transitive-enforcement datum, which is why the declared-validator trio counts 3 and not 4
- Reproduction: read-only — run the stated grep predicate: every validated contract (e.g. `ledger-event`) yields call-site hits and every member of the unenforced trio yields none, while all three still appear in the configure digests and in `generateSchemaManifest` output.
- Acceptance criteria: `CANONICAL_CONTRACTS` and `CANONICAL_TOOL_CONTRACTS` carry an explicit per-name disposition (`enforced` | `ref-only` | `spec-only` | `unenforced`), a shipped gate derives each name's actual status from a call-site/`$ref` walk and reddens on divergence — red at HEAD naming the trio — and the trio is then either wired to producers or deleted together with its digests.
- Related findings: F-498 (same digest-maintenance class at the README-pinned build digests) · F-456 (its refutation confirmed guard enforcement IS live, so the tool contracts are NOT in the unenforced set) · R10-L04-3 (round-1 half, 3/12, subsumed by this census) · carries the merged candidate `R10-L04-3` (same mechanism, one fix site) · nguồn: R10 V03 — 25-contract census

### F-538 — the topology schema interpreter's keyword set is an admission list rather than a feature matrix, imposing or dropping at least nine published-schema semantics with no gate that states which keywords are enforced

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R10-T03-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), Zink Tools (workflow contracts)
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the enforcing divergences bite live payloads — the hidden 1024 item ceiling applies to 57 of 86 shipped array nodes that declare none, `maxLength` compares UTF-8 bytes against character-counting authors, and the one shipped `uniqueItems` node (`ledger-event dependencies`) deduplicates with raw `JSON.stringify`, so key-order drift makes equal items distinct; the rejecting `format` half is latent at HEAD (24/24 shipped `format` nodes are `date-time`) and the `default` half is filed as R10-T03-1
- Root cause: `KEYWORDS` (`schema.mjs:56-92`) is used only to reject unknown keys (`:275`), never to declare which keywords are enforced, defaulted or ignored, so the published dialect and the executable dialect diverge in at least nine observable ways and nothing in the manifest or gates states the difference.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/schema.mjs:329-349` — string-only `format` branch that rejects every non-`date-time` value · Where: `:330-337` — `maxLength` as `Buffer.byteLength` with the deviation stated in-comment · Where: `:363`, `:378` — hidden `maxItems ?? 1024` / `maxProperties ?? 1024` · Where: `:368` vs `:300-310` — `uniqueItems` via raw `JSON.stringify` while `const`/`enum` use `stableJson` · Where: `:278-298` — `$ref` by filename+pointer, `$id` never read (25 shipped `$id` nodes) · Where: `:320-327` — `then`/`else` consumed only under `if` · Measured: my own walk of all 25 tracked `*.schema.json` — 86 array nodes, 57 without `maxItems`; 24 `format` nodes all `date-time`; 1 `uniqueItems` at `topology/contracts/ledger-event.schema.json:1282`; probes confirm `format:"uuid"` REJECT and `format` on an integer PASS.
- Reproduction: read-only — the walk script (recursive JSON visit counting array nodes lacking `maxItems`, and the format/value census) plus the two-line interpreter probes above; no gate performs any keyword-vs-contract comparison today (checked: `zink-tools-schema-only-capability-contracts.test.mjs` walks open-object nodes, `agent-lib-F-271.test.mjs` lists key names without validating a payload carrying them).
- Acceptance criteria: one exported keyword table `{keyword: enforced | annotation | default:<n> | unsupported}` consulted by the schema manifest and by a new gate that (a) walks every schema position of all 25 contracts against it (red on `default` per R10-T03-1), (b) reddens if the `uniqueItems`/`enum` comparisons become key-order dependent (stableJson), and (c) requires every array/object node to declare an explicit bound instead of inheriting 1024.
- Related findings: F-404 (NEW SITE at `schema.mjs:368` — the interpreter's `uniqueItems` shares its raw-`JSON.stringify` key-order mechanism; F-404's own fix at the decision ledger does not cover this site) · R10-T03-1 (the `default` refusal, filed as its own High row) · R10-L03-2 (the latent `format` half, folded here and not filed standalone) · F-450/F-455 (same family of unsatisfiable published shapes) · nguồn: R10 V03 — interpreter dialect census

- Root cause: the two published manifests guarantee byte identity only — `docs/contracts/knowledgebase-migration-digests.json` states adoption is keyed on filename in the runner and that the a4 test is what reddens — and the digest comparison at runtime exists on one side only (`zink-skill-store/src/migrations.mjs:134-143` against the `sha256` column at `:63-68`, versus `knowledge.schema_migrations` at `database.py:2430` which has no such column); neither the a4 gate (`tests/m12-t2.2-a4-migration-digest.test.mjs:94`, `:118`) nor either runner (`migrations.mjs:36` imports only the escape and execute helpers) ever inspects a migration's SQL or its own end-of-file postcondition
- Evidence: Where: `zink-skill-store/src/migrations.mjs:63-68` — ledger created with a `sha256 text NOT NULL` column · Where: `:134-143` — refuse-on-change at the write seam · Where: `:23-25` — the published rule · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:2430` — ledger without any digest column · Where: `:2434-2435` — adoption by `SELECT EXISTS` on version alone · Where: `:2442-2457` — the F-166 replay heuristic that records a migration as applied from catalog existence · Where: `:2458-2459` — the version-only INSERT · Measured: predicate over `tests/m12-t2.2-a4-migration-digest.test.mjs` for validateSql, pg_constraint and tokenize → 0 hits; only `existsSync` at `:94`, `:220`, `:306` and `sha256OfFile` at `:118`, `:221`, `:302`, `:343` · Measured: predicate `grep -c "run_state"` style re-runs on both runners show `migrations.mjs:36` imports only `escapeSqlLiteral, executePostgresQuery` · Where: `docs/contracts/knowledgebase-migration-digests.json` purpose text — discloses filename-keyed adoption and names the test as enforcement · Where: `R10-L05-2` is the measured instance of an applied migration whose own widening postcondition is never asserted
- Reproduction: read-only: open both ledgers' CREATE TABLE statements and diff their columns, then grep the digest-manifest test for any SQL-parsing symbol and record the zero hits, and read `004_skill_retirement_state.sql:40-55` to confirm the applied file's own check covers one of the two constraints it changes
- Acceptance criteria: the module that applies a migration also enforces the published immutability rule — `knowledge.schema_migrations` gains a digest column and a refuse-on-change path as a forward migration, or the Knowledgebase manifest is re-scoped in its purpose text to a repo-gate-only guarantee and says so explicitly — and one shipped non-skippable gate parses each migration in `apps/knowledge/migrations` and `zink-skill-store/migrations` for internal consistency, at minimum that every CHECK a file drops and re-adds is re-asserted by name in that file's own postcondition, so the gate reddens when the postcondition is deleted
- Related findings: cites F-481 as the reason the Postgres backstop cannot be counted on, does not re-file it · `R10-L05-2` is this row's measured instance and is its own row (disjoint fix site) · `R10-T05-4` owns the zero-runtime-reader half of the manifests · `R10-T12-4` belongs to F-431 · the moved-file half is guarded at `tests/m12-t2.2-a4-migration-digest.test.mjs:190` and is not claimed · nguồn: R10 V05 — Skill Store / Zink Tools verdict lane

### F-542 — no canonical ledger-root identity predicate guards the runtime write path: the plugin hand-builds ledger paths, bypasses `ledgerPaths()` entirely, and `buildConfig` re-implements the join, so a mis-set `ledgerRoot` mints a parallel empty governance root that every check accepts

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R10-T04-1`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow topology), Build & runtime config
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: any operator edit of `~/.openclaw-zink/openclaw.json` `ledgerRoot` (or a wrong-value deploy) reaches `index.mjs:6033` after only an `isAbsolute` test; the harm window runs until the next `verify.mjs` deep-compare, and `configure.mjs` refusing non-homedir state dirs does not constrain the value inside the homedir config
- Root cause: the only code that knows the canonical `operator/m7-t2/ledger` suffix (`ledgerPaths`, `ledger.mjs:186-199`) is a constructor with zero callers on the write path, while the validator every write actually runs (`validateLedgerPaths`, `ledger.mjs:245-254`) asserts only that sibling names match `join(root,…)` — consistency, never identity — and `lib/policy.mjs:1525-1530` `buildConfig` hand-joins the canonical segments a third time instead of calling the guarded constructor.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:6017-6037` — verbatim ledgerRoot test then the hand-built paths object · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/ledger.mjs:186-199` — `LEDGER_ROOT_ALREADY_JOINED` and its "would create a separate, empty governance root beside the real ledger" rationale, inside the bypassed constructor · Where: `ledger.mjs:245-254` — consistency-only validation followed by `assertNoLinks` on any accepted root, and `ensureLedger` `mkdirSync(recursive)` populating it · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:1525-1530` — the second unguarded hand-join · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/runtime-backup.mjs:111-113` — the guard-justification comment that is false at HEAD · Measured: `grep -n "ledgerPaths" plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs` → 0 hits; F-369/F-370 ids return 0 hits in the live register (closed family, artifact class re-mintable at a new seam).
- Reproduction: read-only — construct `{root:"/tmp/x", events:"/tmp/x/events.jsonl", snapshot:"/tmp/x/snapshot.json"}` and trace it through `validateLedgerPaths` (accepts: a root with no `operator/m7-t2/ledger` tail is never tested); do not run `ensureLedger` — it creates files. The static chain plus the 0-hits grep is sufficient.
- Acceptance criteria: one exported `assertCanonicalLedgerRoot(root, stateRoot)` (resolved suffix identity) called at construction AND validation — `ledgerPaths`, `buildConfig`, the plugin's `paths` build, `transactionRoot`, `validateLedgerPaths` — with a scratch-root test proving `ensureLedger` throws a named code for an already-joined or short root (red at HEAD) while the canonical state-root input still passes.
- Related findings: absorbs R10-L03-3 (WRONG-SITE) and R10-T04-2 (second site: `runtime-backup.mjs:119-131` `assertNoNestedLedgerRoot` checks exactly ONE double-join spelling `stateDir/operator/m7-t2/ledger/operator/m7-t2/ledger`, so `…/operator/m7-t2`, `…/operator` or the bare state root nest invisibly — predicate `grep -rn "assertNoNestedLedgerRoot|NESTED_LEDGER" --include=*.mjs` → 1 definition, 1 call `:288`, classifier and 2 tests, no general identity check anywhere) · closed F-369 family (same artifact, ceremony seam) · F-490 (distinct: state-dir refusal) · nguồn: R10 V03 — root-identity chain

### F-543 — `configureGovernedStoreRoot` overwrites one unscoped process global with no first-writer-wins guard, so a second governed root in the same process silently redirects `task-queue.json` and `decision-ledger.json`

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R10-T04-3`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: Medium
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: the predicate `git grep -n "configureGovernedStoreRoot"` returns exactly one production writer (`plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:6032`); the only multi-root callers are in `tests/agent-lib-F-002.test.mjs:60`, `:73`, `:111`, so firing needs two governed roots registered in one process and no HEAD path registers twice
- Root cause: the governance store root is a single module-level binding (`policy.mjs:1241`) and `configureGovernedStoreRoot` assigns `path.resolve(root)` unconditionally (`:1263`) without recording who configured it first, while leases and the native-guard registry in the same subsystem ARE partitioned by root, so a later root silently reroutes every fallback read in `resolveGovernedStoreRoot` (`:1272-1281`).
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:1241` — `let governedStoreRoot = null;` · Where: `…:1248-1265` — validation is shape-only (non-empty, absolute), then `governedStoreRoot = path.resolve(root);` with no prior-value comparison · Where: `…:1272-1281` — `resolveGovernedStoreRoot` answers the last write or throws `GOVERNANCE_STORE_ROOT_UNCONFIGURED` · Where: `…:1289`, `:1292` — the two store file names are `task-queue.json` and `decision-ledger.json` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:164` and `integrations/openclaw/lib/decision-ledger.mjs:62` — `const base = root === null ? resolveGovernedStoreRoot() : root;`, the fallback reads · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:6032` — the only production writer · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:1225-1239` — the docblock's own claim that the root "is configured once by whoever owns the configuration" · Measured: production writers of the global = 1; test writers = 3 in one file
- Reproduction: read-only proof at HEAD: `configureGovernedStoreRoot` has no branch that inspects an existing value, so calling it with a second absolute root returns the new root and every later `resolveGovernedStoreRoot()` read points at the new tree; a two-root process is reachable only through the test harness today (`tests/agent-lib-F-002.test.mjs`), which is what keeps this LATENT rather than OPEN.
- Acceptance criteria: `configureGovernedStoreRoot` either refuses a second, different root with a named code (`GOVERNANCE_STORE_ROOT_ALREADY_CONFIGURED`) or stores the root per scope the way the lease registry does (`index.mjs:6052-6053`), and a lane configures two roots in one process and turns red when the refusal or the partition is removed.
- Related findings: paired with R10-T04-4 as the same singleton family but kept separate because the fix sites differ (`policy.mjs:1248` versus `native-boundary.mjs:75-100`) · F-486 is the ambient-env sibling · `tests/agent-lib-F-002.test.mjs` is the lane that already re-configures roots · nguồn: R10 V04 — governance singletons and contract surface

### F-545 — the registry checksum gate is a 64-hex shape test over a digest recomputed from the contracts it claims to verify, and the verifier that compares against the committed pin has zero production callers

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R10-T05-1`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow contracts), Gates + CI
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: every governed plugin start, because `registerTools` runs this gate on its normal path
- Root cause: two separate mechanisms were built and never joined. `verifySchemaManifestChecksums()` compares a freshly generated manifest against `PINNED_SCHEMA_MANIFEST_CHECKSUM`, but its only wrapper `verifyToolSchemaManifest` is exported and never called, while `registerTools` gates on `isCanonicalChecksum(getToolRegistryChecksum())`, and `getToolRegistryChecksum` regenerates the manifest from the same files the pin was meant to attest. A mutated contract therefore yields a different but equally valid checksum and registration proceeds.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/schema.mjs:475-477` — the generator reads and hashes each contract file's live bytes · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/schema.mjs:556-557` — the committed expectation literal · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/schema.mjs:559-570` — the only comparison, with the F-322 comment at `:560-562` stating "the generated manifest is compared against the COMMITTED expectation, not against itself" · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:4119-4121` — `verifyToolSchemaManifest` wrapping it, zero call sites · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:4287-4288` — the running gate: regenerate then shape-test · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:4132-4133` — `isCanonicalChecksum` is `/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/u` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:4294-4295` — the M8-059 comment claiming integrity binding · Where: `tests/m7-lane-h5-persistence-deps.test.mjs:199-203` — the assertion that pins a value against a recomputation of itself · Measured: predicate over `--include=*.mjs` for the four symbols: production hits are the import at `index.mjs:31` and the definitions/`getToolRegistryChecksum` uses; all pin readers are in `tests/`
- Reproduction: Read-only symbol census (above) plus the two operand reads; measuring it live would require mutating a published contract, which is out of bounds. Static proof is sufficient: no code path from `registerTools` reaches `verifySchemaManifestChecksums`.
- Acceptance criteria: the production registration gate calls `verifySchemaManifestChecksums()` and refuses registration on `valid: false`, with `isCanonicalChecksum` retained as shape hygiene, and `tests/m7-lane-h5-persistence-deps.test.mjs:199-203` is replaced by an assertion against `PINNED_SCHEMA_MANIFEST_CHECKSUM`. Red today: editing any `contracts/*.schema.json` byte leaves the gate green; deleting the pin comparison inside `verifySchemaManifestChecksums` must turn the new assertion red.
- Related findings: R10-T03-2 (the 25-contract census naming `verifyToolSchemaManifest` as one of the zero-caller validators — this row is the mechanism, not the symptom) · R10-L01-2 (the same declared-validator-with-zero-call-sites family: `verifyToolSchemaManifest`, `isMutatingStage`, `assertRealExecution`) · R10-T05-4 (the sibling self-derived pin store, `zink-skill-store/src/migrations.mjs:127-129`) · the fix comment cited by the code as `F-322` does not resolve as a register row · nguồn: R10 V06 — contract manifest integrity binding

### F-546 — the packaged adapter copies under the plugin root are unreachable from the entrypoint loader because the repo-depth rewrite covers only topology/lib, so governed tools import ZINK_REPO_ROOT or cwd bytes instead of the digest-gated ones

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R10-T05-2`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (SQL/n8n/Python guards), deployment topology
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the packaged copies cannot be resolved by any loader candidate on any host, proven by path arithmetic; which of the two harms fires (live repo bytes run, or the adapter import fails into an empty catch) is decided by the OpenClaw gateway process working directory, which no tracked artifact sets
- Root cause: the packaging layer rewrites repository-depth literals only for the `topology/lib` target, while the deployed entrypoint keeps its own inline four-levels-up and cwd-based loaders. `findRepoRoot` compounds it: its ancestor walk tests the parent of the plugin directory first and so never tests the plugin directory where the packager has materialised the walk's own marker file.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:1747-1764` — `PACKAGED_RUNTIME_MODULES` names the knowledge adapter, the skill adapter, the DuckDB bridge and the SQL validator · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:1827-1828` — each is emitted as `assets[relative]` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/configure.mjs:1701-1712` — written to `path.join(output.governancePluginDirectory, relative)` and gated by `ALLOWED_GOVERNANCE_ASSET_SHA256[relative]` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/configure.mjs:1606-1609` — that directory is the state dir's `extensions/zink-governance` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:1128-1145` — the `REPOSITORY_ROOT_PREFIX` rewrite is registered only under the `topology/lib` target · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:1156-1159` — the entrypoint rewrite covers only the two import-specifier prefixes, not the repo-depth literal · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:684-688` — `findRepoRoot` candidates are `ZINK_REPO_ROOT`, `path.resolve(module, "../../../..")`, `process.cwd()` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:693-699` — the walk tests `path.dirname(current)`, never `current` itself · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:703-742`, `:744-780`, `:1184-1210` — the three loaders with the same shape and an empty final catch · Measured: `node -e` with `path.posix` on the deployed shape gives `/home/hoang_vinh` for the `path.resolve` branch and `/home/zink-tools/adapters/knowledge/knowledge-adapter.mjs` for the `new URL` branch, while the packaged copy is `/home/hoang_vinh/.openclaw-zink/extensions/zink-governance/zink-tools/adapters/knowledge/knowledge-adapter.mjs` · Measured: predicate `grep -rn "ZINK_REPO_ROOT"` over tracked files = 1 hit, the read at `index.mjs:685`, no setter; `grep -rn "WorkingDirectory"` = 0 hits, and `policy.mjs:1979` shows the drop-in writes only `Environment=` and `ExecStartPre=`
- Reproduction: Read-only, executed: `node -e` path arithmetic above, plus the symbol census of the rewrite target set. The live branch needs the host gateway cwd (or a shell-exported `ZINK_REPO_ROOT`) to be a repository checkout — that fact is outside this repo and was not asserted.
- Acceptance criteria: one resolver anchored on the importing module's own directory with no cwd and no `ZINK_REPO_ROOT` candidate, or the resolved path is asserted to be inside the digest-gated plugin directory before `import()`; `findRepoRoot` tests `current` before its parent; the entrypoint joins the repo-depth rewrite target. A packaging test that materialises the plugin tree in a temp root, runs with a cwd that is not a repository, and asserts the knowledge and skill adapters load from the plugin root is red today; restoring the un-rewritten entrypoint literal turns it red again.
- Related findings: F-022-family `/mnt/c` premise stays closed at HEAD (the literal is gone from `index.mjs:653-668`; this row is the surviving depth half, not a repeat) · R10-T04 residual ("`findRepoRoot` still admits ZINK_REPO_ROOT/cwd") is the same site without the packaged-copy mechanism · R10-T02-2 (two copies of one module, production binding the wrong one) is the same failure class one layer up · nguồn: R10 V06 — packaged runtime module resolution depth

### F-547 — both published migration-digest stores have zero runtime readers, so migration immutability is trust-on-first-use over an unpinned pin file

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R10-T05-4`
- Affected modules: Zink Skill Store, Zink Knowledgebase, Governance docs
- Severity: Medium
- Status: PARTIAL — the Skill Store half is closed by `f6e504c`: `applySkillStoreMigrations` reads `docs/contracts/skill-store/skill-store-migration-digests.json` at the apply seam and refuses a listed file whose bytes do not hash to the published digest (an absent or unreadable store is `SKILL_STORE_MIGRATION_MANIFEST_UNREADABLE`), and the store itself is digest-gated by the A-4 lane. The criterion's "each runner" is only half met: the Knowledgebase runner (`apps/knowledge/app/database.py` `_apply_migrations`, ledger `knowledge.schema_migrations`) still records a filename with no digest column and adopts an edited file silently. Owner decision needed: add the digest column and a refuse-on-change forward migration, or re-scope the Knowledgebase manifest's `purpose` text to a repo-gate-only guarantee (the alternative F-541 names) (2026-10-24).
- Root cause: the Skill Store runner compares each migration against the digest it inserted on its own first run and the Knowledgebase runner keys migrations by filename with no digest at all, while the two `docs/contracts/*-migration-digests.json` stores that publish the expected values are read only by a test, so the contract's refusal exists on paper and the pin file can be re-legitimised by the same edit that mutates a migration.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-skill-store/src/migrations.mjs:126-129` — the runner digests the file and selects the recorded digest from its own ledger · Where: `zink-skill-store/src/migrations.mjs:134-142` — the mismatch refusal, whose expected value is the one `:148-151` inserted · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:2432-2436` — adoption keyed on `migration_path.name` with no digest · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:2447-2448` — the comment that cites "the digest manifest forbids" an in-place edit the runner never consults · Where: `docs/contracts/knowledgebase-migration-digests.json` and `docs/contracts/skill-store/skill-store-migration-digests.json` — the published stores, tracked per `git ls-files` · Measured: `grep -rln "migration-digests"` (excluding temp, backups, node_modules) returns 4 files, only one of them code, and it is a test · Measured: `grep -rn "migration_digests|migration-digests" apps/knowledge/app/database.py zink-skill-store/src/migrations.mjs scripts/*.mjs` returns 0 hits · Measured: no manifest, `ALLOWED_GOVERNANCE_ASSET_SHA256` entry or `PACKAGE_REWRITES` target names either JSON.
- Reproduction: static read-only at HEAD plus the two predicates above; firing it needs a database — edit a tracked migration file in place, roll the published JSON alongside, and both runners accept it: the Skill Store because a fresh ledger has no recorded row, the Knowledgebase because the version name already matches. No write to any database or migration file was performed.
- Acceptance criteria: each runner resolves its expected digest from its published JSON at the apply seam and refuses a file whose hash is not the published one, and both JSONs enter a digest-gated manifest — deleting the JSON from `docs/contracts` or editing a migration without editing the JSON must turn a non-skippable lane red (the `m12-t2.2-a4` comparator alone cannot, because it is satisfied by rolling the JSON).
- Related findings: adds the unread-pin mechanism to R10-T03-6's two-runner divergence · cites F-481 for why the Postgres backstop cannot be counted on · distinct from F-431 and F-436, which own the ledger INSERT's idempotency and lock window · nguồn: R10 V07 — migration digest pin stores

### F-548 — pin literals are re-declared per carrier with no agreement check and the image-side manifest guard is bound by no lane, so any one copy can be edited or the refusal deleted with every gate green

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R10-T05-5` and `R10-L08-2`
- Affected modules: Zink Knowledgebase, Zink Skill Store, OpenClaw governance plugin, Gates + CI, Build & runtime config
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — test-only: no lane executes the shipped image-side digest refusal and no gate compares the several text copies of one pin, so a drifted copy is only observable in the one place that happens to import the constant
- Root cause: each carrier of a model or artifact identity holds its own literal and each test asserts the literal it embeds, so the copies are individually self-satisfying; the one guard that refuses a mismatched artifact lives in `zink_pdf_runtime.manifest`, which no test imports, and the CI drift case asserts only that the tokenizer step is still present, not that its digest still equals the constant the runtime compares.
- Evidence: Where: `apps/knowledge/pdf-runtime/src/zink_pdf_runtime/manifest.py:20-21` and `apps/knowledge/tests/test_pdf_runtime.py:73-75` — two carriers of the same `9ec325c8…` literal, the test never imports the shipped constant · Where: `apps/knowledge/pdf-runtime/src/zink_pdf_runtime/manifest.py:57-70` — the digest comparison and its `raise` · Where: `apps/knowledge/Dockerfile:120` — the module's only executor, inside an image build · Where: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:92-93` and `apps/knowledge/Dockerfile` and `tests/m3-t2-contract.test.mjs:40-47` and `tests/repository-policy.test.mjs:131,960` — four further carriers of `69564b69…` next to `apps/knowledge/app/markdown_adapter.py:41-43` · Where: `tests/repository-policy.test.mjs:996-1006` — the shipped-workflow drift case asserts presence only · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/markdown_adapter.py:174`, `apps/knowledge/app/reranker.py:113-115` — the correct import-and-compare shape that the carriers above do not use · Measured: `grep -rn "zink_pdf_runtime\.manifest|_requirements_digest|EXPECTED_HASHED_REQUIREMENTS"` over `apps/knowledge`, `tests`, `scripts`, `docs`, `.github` returns 0 test importers of the module · Measured: `grep -rn 9ec325c8…` returns exactly 2 carriers · Measured: `git ls-files` walk of 64-hex literals gives 719 distinct literals in 53 tracked files; restricted to executable and config extensions it gives 293 in 52; the largest family is `555f37dae8ee…` in 7 tracked carriers.
- Reproduction: read-only — delete the raise at `manifest.py:67-70` or change `69564b69…` in `ci.yml:93` alone and no lane can notice: the first is executed only inside a Docker build that no test imports, the second is covered only by a presence assertion, and the runtime comparison reads the Python constant. No mutation was performed; the conclusion is the predicate result above.
- Acceptance criteria: one pin module per family that every other carrier imports (the `reranker_contract.py` shape already in-repo), plus a gate that asserts each re-declaring file — `ci.yml`, `apps/knowledge/Dockerfile`, the pinning tests — still contains the imported value, and a case that executes `zink_pdf_runtime.manifest` on a mutated artifact and expects the refusal; deleting the raise or editing any single carrier's literal must turn a non-skippable lane red.
- Related findings: the OpenClaw-side members of the same root cause are R10-T05-1 and R10-T03-2 — file them as one family, not three rows · value-binding of the PDF pins is R10-L08-1 · F-134 stays REFUTED: its premise was re-measured and its replacement tests exist at `tests/repository-policy.test.mjs:939-994` and `:996-1006` · adjacent to F-496 (docs-vs-CI `uv` version drift, a different field) · nguồn: R10 V07 — pin carrier agreement and image-side guard coverage

### F-549 — no shared error-code carrier: three throw conventions consumed by five private discriminators, so a governed code crosses a seam only by accident

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R10-T06-1`
- Affected modules: Zink Skill Store, Zink Knowledgebase, Zink Tools (workflow topology), Zink Tools (workflow contracts), OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the Skill Store CLI flattens every code thrown without a `details` array, and the queue-claim seam classifies only by message prose, both on live routes
- Root cause: Errors are raised in three mutually invisible conventions — (A) `error.code` set on the object, (B) a bare code as the whole message with no `code` member, (C) `"CODE: prose"` inside the message — and each consumer independently tests one of five discriminators (details-array shape, a code-shaped regex over the message text, `message.startsWith`, a `/CODE/u.test(message)` regex, or the HTTP status integer), so whichever convention the producer did not use the code is destroyed at that hop.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-skill-store/src/catalog.mjs:971-972` — the only producer that sets both `code` and `details` outside migrations · Where: `zink-skill-store/index.mjs:66,85` and `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/workflow.mjs:280-283` — `code` with no `details` · Where: `zink-skill-store/bin/skill-store.mjs:45` — gate is `Array.isArray(error?.details)`, a foreign module's private shape · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/reranker.py:20,66,108,145,174,221,253` plus 13 further raise sites — bare code as message, class carries no `code` member · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:607-609` and `integrations/openclaw/lib/discord-native-send.mjs:114-152` — prefix-in-message, no `code` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:168` and `apps/knowledge/app/run_errors.py:9,129` — the same code regex re-implemented in two modules · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:1385-1392` — thrower and matcher both key on message text · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/coordinator-pickup.mjs:273-281` — `/CONCURRENT_CLAIM_DENIED/u.test(String(claimErr?.message))` · Measured: `git ls-files '*.mjs' | grep -v '^temp/' | xargs grep -n "\.code = "` = 58 sites in 30 files (53 outside `tests/`; 38 onto an Error-like binding; `error.code =` alone = 33); exactly 2 files attach a `details` member (`catalog.mjs:972`, `zink-skill-store/src/migrations.mjs:80`); node walk of 47 `catch` blocks in 8 audited files found 0 leaving the binding unread in the next 12 lines · Measured: the archived id in `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:301` resolves in neither the live register nor `CHANGELOG.md`, so it is cited here as an in-source comment only
- Reproduction: static, re-runnable: run the three predicates above from the repo root and diff the producer set (58 sites) against the two `details`-attaching files and the five discriminator sites; then read `skill-store.mjs:44-50` — a thrown error with `code` and no `details` array returns `OPERATIONAL_FAILURE`, which is the executable proof the code is destroyed at the hop
- Acceptance criteria: one `codeFrom(error)` helper (the union of `main.py:168`, `run_errors.py:129` and `error.code`) replaces the five private discriminators, every refusal site sets `error.code`, and a gate asserts that each `CODE:`-prefixed message literal also sets `code` — the gate must go red when any of the 58 producer sites stops setting `code`
- Related findings: `R10-L05-1` folded (V05 confirmed at `catalog.mjs:1052-1062`) · `R10-L07-4` folded (V06 → site of T06-2/T06-3) · in-source fix comment `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:301` · sources for `R10-T06-2`, `R10-T06-3`, `R10-T06-4`, `R10-T06-5` · same error-code vocabulary family seen from the other side — that row counts the disagreeing DECLARATIONS of the code set, this one the disagreeing CARRIERS and discriminators; neither fix closes the other · nguồn: R10 V11 — T06/T08 candidate verification

### F-551 — the Knowledge HTTP reader accepts `detail` only as an array while every producer sends an object, so all 44 governed codes are replaced by an HTTP-status constant

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R10-T06-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow contracts), Zink Knowledgebase, Zink Skill Store
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: any `/v1/search` refusal (503 `RERANKER_UNAVAILABLE`, 503 `EMBEDDING_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE`, 409 `NO_ACTIVE_INDEX`) arrives at `knowledge-adapter.mjs:749` and yields an empty projection
- Root cause: `summarizeKnowledgeFailure` was written for FastAPI's `RequestValidationError` array body and never reconciled with the hand-raised `HTTPException` bodies the same routes produce, so the only body the reader can project is the one the governed vocabulary is not in; the envelope then names the failure by status integer.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-tools/adapters/knowledge/knowledge-adapter.mjs:177` — `if (!body || typeof body !== "object" || !Array.isArray(body.detail)) { return ""; }` · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/knowledge/knowledge-adapter.mjs:160-165` — the comment scoping the projection to caller-request field names and calling a free-text `detail` worthless · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/knowledge/knowledge-adapter.mjs:749,751-760` — the single call site, then `governed_status: "CONTROLLED_FAILURE"`, the status-derived error constant, and "its response body is not forwarded" · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py` — 44 sites of `detail={"code": …}` including `:530,532,534,892,896` · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:301-307` — the producer deliberately recovers the raised reranker code and reads it in the readiness route, so the vocabulary exists and is lost only at the transport hop · Measured: `grep -c 'detail={"code"' apps/knowledge/app/main.py` = 44 (the candidate said ~33); `grep -rn "summarizeKnowledgeFailure" zink-tools tests` = 2 hits (1 definition, 1 call); `grep -n "detail"` in the adapter = 3 hits all inside that one function; `grep -rln` of three governed codes in `tests/` = 3 files, none asserting the adapter envelope
- Reproduction: read-only — apply the reader predicate to a produced body: `node -e 'const b=JSON.parse("{\"detail\":{\"code\":\"RERANKER_UNAVAILABLE\"}}");console.log(Array.isArray(b.detail))'` prints false, and `summarizeKnowledgeFailure`'s first `if` returns the empty string for that shape, which is exactly the 503 body `main.py:530` emits.
- Acceptance criteria: the reader accepts both shapes — an object `detail` with a code member is forwarded verbatim through a code-shaped allowlist (the predicate exported from `main.py:168`), an array `detail` keeps the existing field projection, and the adapter's `error` field becomes the upstream code with the status constant as fallback; a cross-language test asserts each of the 44 producer codes reaches the envelope and goes red when the object branch is removed
- Related findings: nullifies the fix recorded in the comment at `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:301` one hop later · makes `R10-L07-4` (V06: site of this row) a symptom · related live `F-017` (status attribution, different mechanism) · root cause is the carrier split in `R10-T06-1` · nguồn: R10 V11 — T06/T08 candidate verification

### F-552 — one governed code maps to two HTTP statuses on two routes and retry is classified only by the status integer

- Category: F1 — Logic & Functional Bugs
- Legacy register id: `R10-T06-4`
- Affected modules: Zink Knowledgebase, Zink Tools (workflow contracts), Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: a model swap during `/v1/search` yields 503 and three adapter retries, while the identical drift on `/v1/index-runs` yields 409 and none
- Root cause: there is no code-to-status or code-to-retry table; each route picks its own status for the same `LifecycleError` code, and the caller's retry decision is a status-integer set, so the retryable answer is derived from the transport rather than from the cause. A second module owns retry by attempt count on a different subject, so no single place answers "is this retryable".
- Evidence: Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:528-530` — the drift code is raised with `status_code=503` on the search route · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:896` — the same code is raised with `status_code=409` on the index-runs route · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/index_lifecycle.py:127` — `raise LifecycleError("EMBEDDING_MODEL_DRIFT")`, the single producer of both · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/knowledge/knowledge-adapter.mjs:588-594` — `retryableOutcome` is `status === 0 || 429 || 503 || 504`, then backoff `attempt * 1500` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/runtime.mjs:274-276` — the second classifier, keyed on attempt count for a `SELF_VALIDATION_FAILED` claim · Where: `docs/runbooks/governed-outage-classes.md:69,100` — the operator contract says retry only after the refusal is understood and that some classes must be reported, not retried · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/file-lock.mjs:50,55` — a third, errno-keyed retry table exists in-process · Measured: both producer lines and both classifier lines re-read at HEAD; no code-level retry table found in `apps/knowledge` or `zink-tools`
- Reproduction: static plus arithmetic — at `main.py:530` the drift code is 503, and `knowledge-adapter.mjs:588-592` marks 503 retryable, so a permanent drift is retried `maxAttempts` times; the same code at `:896` is 409 and is not retried. No fault injection is needed to show the divergence, only the two lines.
- Acceptance criteria: one code-to-status and code-to-retry table owned beside the vocabulary that `main.py:168` already validates, used by both routes, and the adapter retries by code with the status as fallback; a test pairs `EMBEDDING_MODEL_DRIFT` with the single status and the single retry verdict and goes red when either route re-diverges
- Related findings: depends on `R10-T06-3` (the code never reaches the retry decision today, which is why the status-only rule is currently all the adapter can act on) · extends the declared-list rows `R10-L06-3`/`T03-4` with the behavioural half · carried by `R10-T06-1` · nguồn: R10 V11 — T06/T08 candidate verification

### F-553 — refusal classifiers match producer prose instead of a code, and one known-refusal entry can never fire because an earlier entry is its substring

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R10-T06-5`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow topology), Zink Knowledgebase, Gates + CI
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the queue-claim seam classifies only by regex over `claimErr.message`, and a reword of `queue-manager.mjs:608` silently changes the owner-visible class
- Root cause: the producers behind these classifiers throw plain `Error` objects whose code lives only in the message prefix, so each consumer re-implements matching as substring or regex tests over message text (plus a concatenated `stderr`+`message`+`code` blob), and the ordered substring scan makes the vocabulary order-sensitive: an entry that contains an earlier entry is unreachable.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/coordinator-pickup.mjs:272-293` — `String(claimErr?.message ?? "")` then two `/…/u.test(message)` arms then a fallback that publishes `cause: message` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:607-609,613-614` — both refusals are message prefixes with no `code` member · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/refusal-classifier.mjs:76-78,111-116` — `refusalOutput` concatenates `stderr`, `message`, `code`, then an ordered `find` over 31 literals including lowercase words such as `permission`, `symlink`, `mode drift` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-preflight.mjs:112-118` — a duplicated inline list and a duplicated matching rule, with `assertSameRefusalClasses` pinning only the list · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs:189` — `stderr.includes("SyntaxError")` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:1385-1392` — the thrower and the matcher both key on the same message prefix · Measured: the list is 31 entries; a substring-order scan found exactly one shadowed entry, `"Agent governance instructions drift"` (index 1) shadowed by `"governance instructions drift"` (index 0); executing `classifyRefusal` with that exact message returned `"governance instructions drift"`; `grep -n "\.code = " integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs` = 0 hits; 5 prose classifier sites counted (the candidate said 4)
- Reproduction: executed and read-only — run `node --input-type=module` importing `classifyRefusal` from `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/refusal-classifier.mjs`, call it with an error whose message is the second list entry verbatim, and observe the first entry returned; static for the claim seam by reading `coordinator-pickup.mjs:272-293` against `queue-manager.mjs:607-614`.
- Acceptance criteria: every producer of a classifiable refusal sets `error.code`; `classifyRefusal` and `coordinator-pickup` test `code` first and keep substring matching only for foreign errors; the ordered list is asserted to contain no entry that includes an earlier entry, and each entry is asserted to be a `code` some module sets — both assertions go red today (shadowed entry count 1, `queue-manager` code count 0)
- Related findings: carried by `R10-T06-1` · the redundancy note replaces the lane's `INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION` unreachable-branch claim (that arm is reachable at `queue-manager.mjs:613-614`) · `R10-T07-4` owns the list-split/unfalsifiable-assertion half · `F-383` is closed in `CHANGELOG.md`; the id the lane cited as `F-159` does not resolve live · nguồn: R10 V11 — T06/T08 candidate verification

### F-554 — a swallowed child exit status makes the Python artifact validator return valid true when the interpreter never answered, and the verdict is digest-stamped as evidence

- Category: F1 — Logic & Functional Bugs
- Legacy register id: `R10-T06-6`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — needs fault injection: reproduced by emptying `process.env.PATH` in-process so `python` resolves to nothing; the same path fires for real on any host without a `python` executable, which `docs/contracts/ci-skip-expectations.json:67-71` documents as the CI quality-gate runner's state, while this host does have Python 3.13 and therefore takes the correct branch
- Root cause: the catch at `artifact-validator.mjs:187-196` distinguishes only "stderr said SyntaxError" from "status was a non-zero number"; an ENOENT or a `timeout` overrun arrives with `status: null` and an empty stderr, so control falls through to the structural brace counter, which reports `valid: true` at `:275` and is recorded as an executed Python check by the caller at `:731-732`.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs:182-185` — `execFileSync("python", …, { stdio: ["ignore","pipe","pipe"], timeout: 5000 })` · `:189` — `if (stderr.includes("SyntaxError") || (err.status && err.status !== 0))`, no `err.signal`/`err.code`/null-status arm · `:199-275` — the brace counter and its `{ valid: true, method: "structural" }` · `:730-736` — the only consumer, which sets `checks.pythonSyntax` and forces FAIL only when `valid` is false · `:290-293` — `evidenceDigest` over `status:sortedChecks:errors.length` · Measured: `x = 1 +` file → `{"valid":false, …, "method":"py_compile"}` with python on PATH, `{"valid":true,"method":"structural"}` with `process.env.PATH` emptied · Measured: timeout error is `code=ETIMEDOUT status=null signal="SIGTERM" stderr.len=0`, ENOENT is `code=ENOENT status=null signal=null`; `:189` evaluates false for both · Measured: `validatePythonSyntax` has 0 hits in `tests/` and `execFileSync("python"` has exactly 1 repo hit
- Reproduction: read-only and outside the repository: write `x = 1 +\ny = 2\n` into `%TEMP%`, import the shipped `validatePythonSyntax`, call it once with a normal `PATH` (returns valid false, method `py_compile`) and once after `process.env.PATH = ""` (returns valid true, method `structural`); a timeout variant is the same call against a `python` that hangs, which the 5000 ms `timeout` converts to `status: null`
- Acceptance criteria: separate "the interpreter refused" (fall through to the structural counter) from "the interpreter never answered" (`err.signal`, `err.code === "ENOENT"`, or `err.status === null` → return `valid: false` with a new code naming the unavailable validator, and never stamp `checks.pythonSyntax` true), and a shipped test injects both a missing binary and a timeout and asserts neither yields `valid: true`; deleting the null-status arm must turn that test red
- Related findings: `F-469` (same file, digest stamped over a verdict that later mutates — different mechanism, cross-referenced not a parent) · `F-310` closed (the flattened error-constant sibling: five distinct causes here share the literal `INVALID_PYTHON_SYNTAX` at `:192,240,254,264,271`) · `R10-T06-5` cites the same `:189` line for its prose-matching classifier · no live row covers `INVALID_PYTHON_SYNTAX` or `py_compile` (grep = 0) · nguồn: R10 T06 — error truth · reproduced by V10 with the host premise corrected and the category moved from F7 to F1

### F-555 — the Skill Store adapter forwards raw `error.message` into the agent-facing envelope while the sibling Knowledge adapter was fixed to return a fixed sentence

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R10-T06-7`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow contracts), Zink Skill Store, OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: any unexpected throw inside the Skill Store adapter's dispatched action (including a `fs` error whose message interpolates an absolute state path) is returned verbatim in the envelope `message`
- Root cause: the top-level catch builds its envelope from `error.message`, and the sibling seam already documents why that is unsafe (a parsed-body `SyntaxError` quotes upstream bytes and a socket error names host and port) — the control exists in the repository and was applied to one adapter only, so layout and host detail leak on the other.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-tools/adapters/skill-store/skill-adapter.mjs:1169-1178` — `catch (error)` returns `error: error.code || "SKILL_ADAPTER_ERROR"` and `message: error.message` · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/knowledge/knowledge-adapter.mjs:775-780` — the sibling returns a fixed sentence, with the comment recording that raw messages reached the model and the cards · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/queue-manager.mjs:242,285,343,429` — `${err?.message}` interpolated into the storage-failure messages, surfaced at `integrations/openclaw/lib/coordinator-pickup.mjs:288-293` as `cause: message` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/decision-ledger.mjs:350-352` — `${err?.code ?? err?.message}` inside the ledger-corrupt message · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs:192` — raw child stderr into the validated report · Measured: `grep -rn "message: error.message" zink-tools/adapters` = 1 hit, `skill-adapter.mjs:1177`; no sanitiser between the catch and the returned envelope; no secret or path value was printed or copied in this lane
- Reproduction: read-only — open `skill-adapter.mjs:1169-1178` and confirm the envelope is built from `error.message` with no allowlist, then open `knowledge-adapter.mjs:775-780` and read the stated reason the sibling does not do this; the shapes named by the `fs` error class (absolute path in the message) are the disclosure, and none is reproduced here.
- Acceptance criteria: adapters return fixed sentences plus a code only; a gate asserts zero `message: error.message` occurrences under `zink-tools/adapters/**` (predicate `grep -rn "message: error.message" zink-tools/adapters`, 1 hit today) and a test asserts the envelope for a thrown error whose message contains an absolute path exposes no path characters
- Related findings: carried by `R10-T06-1` (the same seam also loses the code whenever `error.code` is unset) · the archived id in the comment at `knowledge-adapter.mjs:776` does not resolve in the live register or `CHANGELOG.md` · `R10-L09-5` is unrelated (env basename matching) · nguồn: R10 V11 — T06/T08 candidate verification

### F-556 — operator-rollback refuses the state root it documents as valid input and no runbook names the driver, so the most destructive operator command is unreachable by its own instructions

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R10-T07-1`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), Governance docs
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the driver is the documented historical-recovery action (`CHANGELOG.md:460`), the runbook promise is in the operator's face at `docs/runbooks/deploying-the-m12-t9-build.md:69-72`, and reaching the recovery path is by definition an outage event, where the operator has no procedure to follow
- Root cause: the driver resolves the root from env-then-positional at `:54` but then hands the whole `argv.slice(2)` to `parseRecoveryArguments`, which whitelists only the two flag forms and returns `UNSUPPORTED_ARGUMENT` on any positional, so the promised first-argument form refuses before any check runs; separately, no tracked runbook or README names the driver, so the one form that does work is documented nowhere.
