- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-rollback.mjs:50-54` — "the operator names it (ZINK_OPERATOR_STATE_DIR or the first argument)" with the env-then-argv resolve · Where: `:117` — `parseRecoveryArguments(process.argv.slice(2))` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/recovery-authorization.mjs:80-93` — only `--confirm=` and `--target=` accepted, `:91` returns `UNSUPPORTED_ARGUMENT` for the positional · Where: `docs/runbooks/deploying-the-m12-t9-build.md:69-72` — the same promise, for the plural "operator drivers" · Measured: `git grep -In "operator-rollback" -- '*.md'` returns only `CHANGELOG.md`, `PLAN.md` and `docs/execution/findings.md`, zero hits in `README.md` and zero in `docs/runbooks/`; `git grep -In "RECOVER-T1-BASELINE"` = 1 hit, the definition at `recovery-authorization.mjs:50`; comparator `operator-lock-recovery` is documented at `README.md:263`, `:265`, `:267` and `docs/runbooks/governed-outage-classes.md:37`; `git grep -n "argv.slice(2)"` over the fixture dir = 3 drivers, and the two non-mutating ones take no strict parser, which is why the uniform promise never got challenged
- Reproduction: static read of the two argument paths against the documented command line: substituting the documented positional into `parseRecoveryArguments` returns the `UNSUPPORTED_ARGUMENT` refusal at `:91` before `readRecoveryAuthorization` runs, and `refuse()` at `operator-rollback.mjs:93-106` exits 1; no driver was executed and no `--recover`/`--apply` flag was passed anywhere.
- Acceptance criteria: a runbook section states the exact working invocation, the command that mints `operator/m7-t2/recovery-authorization.json` and both literals; `parseRecoveryArguments` excludes a leading positional (or the driver consumes argv[2] before parsing) so the documented form reaches the gate, and a lane runs the documented form and asserts the `DISCLOSED` report instead of `UNSUPPORTED_ARGUMENT`.
- Related findings: mirror image of OPEN F-489 (same `STATE_DIR_NOT_NAMED` promise, opposite failure in `operator-lock-recovery.mjs`) and of OPEN F-490 (same runbook promise, refused by `configure.mjs`); adjacent to OPEN F-492 in category only · nguồn: R10 V08 — infra, CI and gates plus round-2 destructive ops

### F-557 — r16-rehearsal compares its own (unavailable) gateway sentinel to itself and certifies LIVE UNCHANGED having observed no gateway state

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R10-T07-2`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `rehearse:r16` is a declared npm script (`package.json:27`) run by the operator before a deploy decision, and the sentinel path is the ordinary one on any host without a reachable user systemd manager or without the `openclaw-gateway-zink.service` unit installed
- Root cause: `gateway()` collapses every failure into the same string it can also return on success-with-no-output, and the only gateway control in the report is an equality test between the two returned values, so an unobserved gateway and an unchanged gateway produce the identical verdict and the pass line is emitted from `differences.length === 0` without knowing which arm contributed nothing.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/r16-rehearsal.mjs:130-150` — the single `catch` at `:147-148` returning `(unavailable)` as the only failure value · Where: `:262` and `:322` — the two reads · Where: `:336-343` — `if (gatewayAfter !== gatewayBefore)`, the only place `kind: "gateway"` can enter `differences` · Where: `:356-373` — empty `differences` prints LIVE UNCHANGED, REHEARSAL PASSED and "this run changed nothing live" · Measured: the sentinel is also printed at `:272` and `:334`, so an operator reading the body can see the non-observation even though the verdict says PASSED; a successful-but-empty `systemctl show` for an absent unit collapses the same way; the correct unknown-vs-empty idiom exists in the same file at `:161-166`
- Reproduction: read-only: substitute the two read sites — with `systemctl` absent, `execFileSync` throws ENOENT on both calls, `gatewayBefore` and `gatewayAfter` are both the literal `(unavailable)`, the branch at `:336` is not taken, `differences` stays empty and `:370` prints REHEARSAL PASSED; the driver was never executed.
- Acceptance criteria: `gateway()` returns a record with an observation flag, the gateway difference is pushed as an unobserved state whenever either read is unobserved, the pass line is suppressed in that case in favour of `GATEWAY_STATE_NOT_OBSERVED` with a non-zero exit, and `tests/m8-t2.13-r16-apply-rehearsal.test.mjs` gains a case stubbing an unreachable `systemctl` that goes red when the flag test is removed.
- Related findings: sentinel-as-evidence family with the R10-T07-3 row and OPEN F-421's existsSync-only `backupPresent` (`docs/execution/findings.md:891-895`); R16 rows at `docs/execution/findings.md:386-389` are a different mechanism, closed by `703fec4` · nguồn: R10 V08 — infra, CI and gates plus round-2 destructive ops

### F-558 — an absent or unreadable measurement is coerced to 0 or empty or true and published as a positive operator certification in the backup manifest and the resource window

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R10-T07-3` (absorbs `R10-L09-1` and `R10-L09-4`)
- Affected modules: infrastructure scripts, deployment topology
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: the backup arm runs on the documented operator command at `docs/runbooks/knowledge-platform.md:55` and its empty-inventory input is the ordinary state after `docker compose down --volumes` plus `start.ps1` before migration; the cgroup-v1 arm is code-proven and needs a v1 engine, which no read-only probe can observe, so it is stated as the row's second site rather than as an independently fired defect
- Root cause: these scripts express "nothing was measured" as a value instead of a refusal, so a downstream gate reads a default or an empty collection as an observation; the producer excludes what the paired assertion then looks for, and one branch throws on a missing counter while its sibling defaults the same counter to zero.
- Evidence: Where: `infrastructure/docker/scripts/backup-inspection.ps1:115` — `plaintext_secrets_excluded = ($carriers.Count -eq 0)` · Where: `:112` — `n8n_home_entries_inspected = @($HomeArchiveEntries).Count`, recorded and never gated by either script · Where: `:89` — a null inventory returns `@()` · Where: `infrastructure/docker/scripts/backup.ps1:108` — existence plus a non-zero byte length is the whole dump-content assertion · Where: `backup.ps1:116` — the same shell line that excludes `config` then greps for `config`, so its `exit 1` branch is unreachable · Where: `backup.ps1:53`, `:162` — stderr discarded during capture and the claim printed unconditionally · Where: `infrastructure/docker/scripts/knowledge-t5-common.ps1:515` — the v1 default `0`, against `:504` which throws for v2, with `:513` an untested exit status and `:532`, `:536`, `:544-546` publishing the delta · Measured: `grep -c "Count -eq 0"` = 0 in `backup.ps1` and its one hit in `backup-inspection.ps1` is the coercion site; the correct idiom exists in-repo at `knowledge-t5-live.ps1:287`; `tests/agent-setup-F-265.test.mjs:63-99` executes the helper and `:154-196` asserts it on non-empty listings only, with `:157` skipping when no shell is present
- Reproduction: read-only: walk the decision chain from `backup.ps1:108` (a schema-less dump has a header, so it passes) through `:134` (`Get-BackupArchiveObjectNames` yields an empty array) to `backup-inspection.ps1:115` and `backup.ps1:162`, which print the claim as true; the equivalent fault injection for the helper is already expressible in `tests/agent-setup-F-265.test.mjs` by calling its `inspect` function with an empty entry list, which today is no case at all.
- Acceptance criteria: one inventory-then-assert idiom — a measurement returns an observation flag and the caller refuses without it: `backup.ps1` throws when a named database yields zero objects or the home listing is empty before any manifest field is written; the new `BACKUP_INVENTORY_UNREADABLE` throw sits ahead of `plaintext_secrets_excluded`; `knowledge-t5-common.ps1:515` becomes the `:504` throw and each `docker exec` exit status is tested separately; `backup.ps1:116` asserts over the pre-exclusion listing; and `tests/agent-setup-F-265.test.mjs` gains the empty-listing case that goes red the moment the refusal is removed, with its existing `:154-168` case unchanged.
- Related findings: one root cause with OPEN F-492 and the sentinel family of OPEN F-421 (`docs/execution/findings.md:891`, existsSync-only evidence) and R10-T07-2 above; descendant of CLOSED rows `R7-D13-02` (`CHANGELOG.md:1779-1780`, commit `4307f19`, the unhedged `secrets_included` constant) and `R7-D3-01` (`CHANGELOG.md:1758-1759`, commit `d211dd4`, the missing database), whose delivered fixes are present at HEAD and neither added a non-empty floor; `F-196` (`CHANGELOG.md:1948`) and `F-266` (`CHANGELOG.md:1781-1782`) are unrelated mechanisms · nguồn: R10 V08 — infra, CI and gates plus round-2 destructive ops

### F-559 — the F-159 refusal-class anti-drift assertion runs only on one driver's failure branch and no test compares the two real class lists

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R10-T07-4`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), Gates + CI
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — test-only: the assertion is code-proven unreachable on the success path at HEAD (`operator-preflight.mjs:61-63` returns before `:115`), and both class lists measure 31 identical entries today, so the drift itself is untriggered while the falsification machinery is already absent
- Root cause: a load-time invariant was written as a statement inside a catch block, so it only evaluates after the child has already failed, and the shared classifier's documented promise was never wired to a module-scope call; the only test that touches the invariant calls the comparator with the same list as both operands, which cannot fail.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-preflight.mjs:67-69` — the comment "the class SET is asserted equal to the shared set at load time" · Where: `:64` and `:115` — the call sits inside the catch, after the success write at `:61-63` · Where: `:82-114` — the driver's own 31-entry list · Where: `refusal-classifier.mjs:80-89` — "A drift is a hard failure at load time", and `:90-100` the comparator that throws `REFUSAL_CLASS_SET_DRIFT` · Where: `refusal-classifier.mjs:26-65` — the shared 31-entry list · Where: `tests/m7-t4-orchestration.test.mjs:1147` — the identity call, with `:1151` and `:1156` testing the comparator against a differing and a reordered list, and `:1189` asserting only that the driver's source text names the call · Measured: `git grep -In "assertSameRefusalClasses"` = 6 hits with one production call site, inside the catch; entry counts of both lists = 31 and 31, so HEAD is green and unfalsified
- Reproduction: read-only: add one literal to the driver's `known` list at `:82-114` and no lane turns red, because `tests/m7-t4-orchestration.test.mjs` never compares those two exports and the driver only evaluates the assertion after a child refusal; symmetrically, a green preflight returns at `:61-63` without ever evaluating it.
- Acceptance criteria: the assertion moves to module scope in both drivers so every invocation evaluates it, and `tests/m7-t4-orchestration.test.mjs:1147` is replaced by a case importing the driver's exported list and the classifier list and asserting the comparator throws on an added entry and on a reorder; removing the module-scope call turns that case red.
- Related findings: explains why OPEN F-490's unclassified refusal went uncaught; member of the published-but-unenforced family of R10-L04-3 and R10-T03-2; closed F-159 is the row whose fix this residual sits inside (no row is filed by that id in `docs/execution/findings.md` or `CHANGELOG.md`; the delivered artifact is `refusal-classifier.mjs`) · nguồn: R10 V08 — infra, CI and gates plus round-2 destructive ops

### F-560 — the three mutating operator drivers let an inherited `ZINK_OPERATOR_STATE_DIR` outrank the positional root the operator names and never disclose the root they resolved

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R10-T07-6`
- Affected modules: deployment topology, infrastructure scripts, Governance docs
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: an operator whose shell profile exports `ZINK_OPERATOR_STATE_DIR` (README.md:252 documents exporting it) and who then names a different root positionally gets the env root silently; the refusal path and every report field describe the env root while the typed argument is discarded
- Root cause: all three mutating drivers read the root as `process.env.ZINK_OPERATOR_STATE_DIR ?? process.argv[2] ?? null`, so the ambient value wins over the explicit argument, and neither the pre-mutation disclosure nor the printed report contains the resolved root, so the divergence is invisible to the operator and to the evidence trail.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-apply.mjs:46`, `operator-preflight.mjs:23`, `operator-rollback.mjs:54` — identical env-then-argv precedence · Where: `integrations/openclaw/configure.mjs:78-81` — `GOVERNED_STATE_TREE` = `ZINK_OPERATOR_STATE_DIR ?? ZINK_STATE_DIR ?? "/home/hoang_vinh/.openclaw-zink"` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/recovery-authorization.mjs:211-222` — the disclosure object names action/target/willInstall/willRestoreNative/willRemoveGovernanceExtension and no root; printed at `operator-rollback.mjs:131` · Measured: predicate `grep -rn "process\.env\.ZINK_OPERATOR_STATE_DIR" --include=*.mjs integrations` = 5 hits (3 drivers + `configure.mjs:79` + `verify.mjs:70`); census of env-then-argv resolvers = 6 (3 operator drivers + `live-governance-probe.mjs:59`, `operator-token-probe.mjs:13`, `runtime-preflight.mjs:11`); `README.md:252`/`runbook:91` export the same value they then pass at `README.md:256,275`.
- Reproduction: read-only, nothing executed: export `ZINK_OPERATOR_STATE_DIR=/root/A`, then inspect what `node integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-rollback.mjs /root/B` resolves and prints — line `:54` selects `/root/A` and the disclosure printed at `:131` (`recovery-authorization.mjs:211-222`) contains no field naming `/root/A`, so stdout cannot distinguish the two roots.
- Acceptance criteria: named-argument-first precedence in all three drivers (or a hard refusal when env and argv both name a root and disagree), plus a `resolved_root_fingerprint` digest field in the pre-mutation disclosure and in the apply report before the first write; a lane reddens if any mutating driver resolves a root different from the one it names in its own output.
- Related findings: F-486 (verified live `findings.md:1743`, same precedence root cause but scoped to the probe spawn and a different variable — this row is outside its acceptance text, not an addendum) · F-423 (`:923`, runtime root not assertable by any shipped command) · F-490 (`:1795`, promised named root refused by the builder) · F-489 (`:1782`, `operator-lock-recovery.mjs` reads neither) · nguồn: R10 V12 — T07 destructive-ops lane (verdict)

### F-561 — operator-apply.mjs recomputes the derived ledger snapshot in the live state root before applyRuntime starts, a mutation no stage of the ceremony enumerates and no preimage records

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R10-T07-7`
- Affected modules: deployment topology, infrastructure scripts
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: every documented apply run executes `operator-apply.mjs:237` before `:244`, and a refusal at `apply-orchestration.mjs:119-120` returns `mutationStarted:false` for a run that already wrote
- Root cause: the ledger recompute lives at driver module scope instead of in `APPLY_STAGES`, so it runs outside the mutation boundary the orchestration exists to enforce, and `SNAPSHOT_TARGETS.state` enumerates only the config/extension/builder files, so the ledger tree it writes is structurally unpreimaged and unrestorable by rollback.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/operator-apply.mjs:237` — `migrateLedgerProjection(ledgerPaths(stateDir))` at module scope, ahead of `await applyRuntime({…})` at `:244`, with the owner-ruling comment `:232-236` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/apply-orchestration.mjs:5` — the invariant `PRE-FLIGHT FAILURE = ZERO RUNTIME MUTATION`, `:82` `FIRST_MUTATING_STAGE = "builder-apply"`, `:182` where `mutationStarted` flips, `:186` where the builder is invoked · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:64-67` — "Every live path the apply can mutate and rollback must restore", against `SNAPSHOT_TARGETS.state:78-93` which contains no ledger path · Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/ledger.mjs:614` — the only writer reachable from `:237` · Measured: `git grep -n migrateLedgerProjection` = 3 hits (definition, import, the single call); `git grep -n isMutatingStage` = 1 hit (definition only, no consumer anywhere including tests).
- Reproduction: read-only static sequence at HEAD — open `operator-apply.mjs:237` and `:244` and `transaction-snapshot.mjs:78-93` together; the recompute line executes before the ceremony's first stage and its target is absent from the preimage list, so no `--apply` run can report a zero-mutation state that is true of the whole tree. Fault injection (a `builder-preflight` refusal) was not executed because running any driver is forbidden for this lane.
- Acceptance criteria: either move the recompute into `APPLY_STAGES` after `transaction-snapshot` with its tree added to `SNAPSHOT_TARGETS.state` (so rollback can restore it), or amend `apply-orchestration.mjs:5` and `transaction-snapshot.mjs:64-67` to state the surface the ceremony does not cover; a lane reddens when a run reports `mutationStarted:false` while any file under `operator/m7-t2/ledger/` changed.
- Related findings: F-424 (`findings.md:937`) and F-425 (`:950`) — same "two subsystems enumerate different governed sets" family, neither names `migrateLedgerProjection`, the ledger tree, or pre-`applyRuntime` ordering · R10-T12-2 (verified separate: unlocked orphan-check and the late receipt, different lines) · F-414 (`:807`, `migrateLedgerProjection` re-derives but never rewrites the checkpoint) is the same function and the reason this write is convergent rather than corrupting · nguồn: R10 V12 — T07 destructive-ops lane (verdict)

### F-562 — `artifact.logical_path` is validated by two guards with disjoint accept sets and the delivery gate's path tier is stamped PASSED unevaluated whenever the set has one member

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R10-T08-1`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (SQL/n8n/Python guards), Zink Tools (workflow contracts)
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: every `finishExecution` delivery with a one-member artifact set reaches `artifact-validator.mjs:1405-1407` and records `artifactSetPathSecurity: true` without evaluating any member; the spelling contradiction between the two guards is live in both code paths, but no HEAD line is measured to carry one string through both, so the delivery-failure half is contract-level not proven per-flow
- Root cause: one field is governed twice with opposite requirements — `path-safety.mjs` rejects leading slash, leading backslash and any drive prefix, while `artifact-validator.mjs` requires `path.isAbsolute` and only rejects UNC — and the artifact-set tier is written as an `else if` that defaults the check to true when `orderedSet.length` is 1, contradicting its own comment that every member must pass.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/guard.mjs:2290-2299` — result ingress validates every `artifact.logical_path` with `validateArtifactLogicalPath`, failing with `SCHEMA_VALIDATION_FAILED` · Where: `zink-tools/common/path-safety.mjs:29-36` — the three rejections that define the relative-only accept set · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs:416-424` — `INVALID_PATH: Artifact path must be an absolute path` · Where: `:1370-1372` comment versus `:1386-1407` code — the member tier is gated on `orderedSet.length > 1` and `:1405-1407` assigns `artifactSetPathSecurity = true` unevaluated · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:3007-3009` — the delivery maps `artifactSet.map(a => a.logical_path)` into that gate, and `:2945` does `existsSync(logicalPath)` on the same value · Where: `index.mjs:1103` `validateLiveArtifactPath` — 0 production callers (measured 8 hits: definition, self-comment at `:3695`, `PLAN.md:4780`, 4 test sites) · Measured: importing both modules and applying both predicates to 5 spellings gives disjoint accept sets; no input is admitted by both.
- Reproduction: read-only, executed: `node --input-type=module` importing `zink-tools/common/path-safety.mjs` and `integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs` — `artifacts/report.xlsx` passes `validateArtifactLogicalPath` and fails the gate's absolute tier with `INVALID_PATH`; `/workspace/artifacts/report.xlsx` fails `validateArtifactLogicalPath` and passes the absolute tier. Separately, `evaluateResultGate({artifactPath:"<abs>", artifactSet:["<abs>"]})` returns `checks.artifactSetPathSecurity === true` while no member predicate ran, which the single-member branch at `:1405` guarantees.
- Acceptance criteria: one path policy for `artifact.logical_path`, decided at the root: either it is logical (then `validateTechnicalArtifact`/`evaluateResultGate` resolve it against `resolveArtifactBoundary` and stop requiring `path.isAbsolute`) or it is absolute (then `path-safety.mjs:29-36` accepts the rooted form and the logical-path contract text is reworded); the set tier must be evaluated for the primary member too, so `:1405-1407` cannot default to true; `validateLiveArtifactPath` is wired or deleted together with the `index.mjs:3693-3696` claim, and a lane turns red when a one-member set reports the tier without evaluating it.
- Related findings: F-469 (verified live `findings.md:1522` — same `:1386` mutation but the digest-ordering defect, not the predicate contradiction) · F-458 (`:1379`) unrelated · cross-reference to R10-T08-2 (V11's lane) via the shared `validateTechnicalArtifact` container gates · nguồn: R10 V12 — T08 guard lane (verdict)

### F-563 — renderExpectedResultPreview guards its output directory by string equality against path.normalize, refuses POSIX-shaped roots on the Windows host, and writes the PNG before the realpath containment that the same route performs later

- Category: F2 — Security & Authorization
- Legacy register id: `R10-T08-4`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD for the write-anywhere half: the only production caller passes `previewRoot = resolveAgentSandboxDir("main", context)` (`index.mjs:8357`), an internally derived sandbox root the model cannot name, so an arbitrary host directory is reachable only through a future caller; the over-rejection half is live but degrades silently into the `catch` at `:8395`
- Root cause: the guard is lexical — `path.isAbsolute` plus an identity test against `path.normalize` — and performs no containment of its own, while the containment that exists on the route (`containOutboundAttachment`) runs after `mkdirSync`/`writeFileSync`; the identity test is also platform-dependent, so a POSIX-shaped absolute root is refused on a win32 host even though it is absolute.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/discord-result-preview.mjs:123-132` — the two lexical tests and their error codes · Where: `:150-151` — `mkdirSync(normalized, { recursive: true })` then `writeFileSync(filePath, png)` · Where: `:109-113` — the docstring's own deferral to the transport layer · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:3705` — the realpath containment, called at `:8497`/`:10118`, i.e. after the render at `:8387` · Where: `index.mjs:8357`+`:8393` — `previewRoot` is derived from `resolveAgentSandboxDir("main", context)`, not from the model · Measured: predicate probe — `/workspace/artifacts` → UNSAFE, `/home/u/.openclaw-zink` → UNSAFE, `C:\Windows\Temp` → ACCEPT, no write performed.
- Reproduction: read-only and executed — copy `:123-132`'s two predicates into `node -e` on this host and feed the three spellings above; the refusal of POSIX-shaped roots and the acceptance of an arbitrary absolute Windows directory both reproduce without touching the filesystem. The write-outside-governance path additionally needs a caller that names a free directory, which HEAD does not have.
- Acceptance criteria: resolve `outputDirectory` through the same realpath containment the route already uses (`containOutboundAttachment`'s root logic or an exported `assertInsideWorkspace`) and refuse before `mkdirSync`; make the shape test platform-aware (`path.resolve`/`path.win32` comparison instead of string equality on the caller's spelling); a lane turns red when a POSIX-shaped governed root is refused on win32 or when a directory outside the sandbox is accepted.
- Related findings: F-468 (verified live `findings.md:1509`, same function, filename-collision subject) · sibling of the T04-5 lexical-vs-real family · R10-L02-1 (round-1 file, LATENT for the same 0-caller reason per the wave-2 disposition) · nguồn: R10 V12 — T08 guard lane (verdict)

### F-564 — the operator preview arm asserts validity from magic bytes alone and is computed after the machine verdict, so a file the technical tier refuses is reported as valid to the human

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R10-T08-5`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow contracts)
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `evaluateResultGate` always attaches `preview` (`artifact-validator.mjs:1426,1437`) on the live result path at `index.mjs:3007`, so every delivered artifact carries the weaker human verdict beside the machine verdict
- Root cause: `generateArtifactPreview` re-implements per-type validity checks at a lower bar than `validateTechnicalArtifact` (magic bytes only, no parts, no trailer), and the gate computes it after `overallStatus` is decided, so the two verdicts over one artifact are never reconciled and only the stronger one is actionable.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs:1214-1234` — the preview branch for `.pbix`/`.pbit` derives `validity` from `isPkZip` alone and reports a container-valid string · Where: `:1291-1308` — the same magic-bytes-only validity string for `.docx`/`.doc` · Where: `:1265-1282` — PDF validity from the `%PDF-` header and a version regex only · Where: `:877-880` — the DOCX technical tier requires BOTH `[Content_Types].xml` and `word/document.xml` through `inspectZipContainsAllEntries` · Where: `:833-853` — the PDF technical tier additionally requires a `%%EOF` trailer and an `xref`/`startxref` pointer · Where: `:636-643` — the Excel technical tier requires the content-types part and a worksheet part · Where: `:1414-1424` then `:1426` and `:1437` — `overallStatus` is fixed from `technical.status`/`business.status` before the preview is generated, and the preview is only attached to the payload · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:3007` — the live gate call site · Measured: a scan of `evaluateResultGate` body lines 1363-1439 for the preview identifier returned exactly those two occurrences; `grep -c "pptx" integrations/openclaw/lib/artifact-validator.mjs` = 0, so the candidate's `.pptx` site does not exist and its `:1224-1240` range is the Power BI branch
- Reproduction: static and read-only — compare `:1223-1225`/`:1300-1301`/`:1274-1276` against `:877-880`/`:833-853`: a zip carrying only `[Content_Types].xml` is called container-valid by the preview and refused by the DOCX tier, and any PDF whose last 1024 bytes lack `%%EOF` is refused at `:844-848` while `:1274` still prints valid for a `%PDF-` header.
- Acceptance criteria: the preview arm calls the technical tier's per-type helpers (or prints "not validated by this arm") and a test asserts a DOCX-shaped zip without `word/document.xml` and a header-only PDF are reported as NOT valid in the preview string — both go red today because the preview reads magic bytes only, and the gate must stop attaching an unevaluated verdict after `overallStatus`.
- Related findings: same effect class as the published-but-unread rows in `R10-T03-2`, different mechanism · sibling of `R10-T08-2` (the same tiers, the stronger machine verdict being the one that is wrong there) · nguồn: R10 V11 — T06/T08 candidate verification

### F-565 — the server-side statement timeout is applied only on the docker-exec arm, so the injected-executor arm runs the same SQL with no database-side deadline

- Category: F6 — Reliability & Performance
- Legacy register id: `R10-T08-6`
- Affected modules: Zink Skill Store, Zink Tools (SQL/n8n/Python guards), Gates + CI
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: any caller that injects `options.dbExecutor` reaches `executePostgresQuery`'s early return at `vector-sync.mjs:292-293` and the advertised ceiling is never sent to the server
- Root cause: `withServerSideTimeout` was added in commit `131c768` inside the child-process arm only, so the bound lives in the transport branch instead of in `executePostgresQuery` before the arm split; the injected-executor branch therefore hands raw SQL to whatever executor the caller owns, and no test can observe the bound because the only arm that applies it is the one the suites bypass.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-skill-store/src/vector-sync.mjs:282-286` — the helper prepends the server-side timeout statement · Where: `:391` — its only production call, `child.stdin.write(withServerSideTimeout(sql, dbTimeoutMs))` in the docker-exec arm · Where: `:291-294` — `executePostgresQuery` returns `options.dbExecutor(sql)` directly, without the bound · Where: `:283-284` — a non-safe-integer or non-positive ceiling falls back to 30000, which `tests/m11-t1-skill-vector-sql.test.mjs:811-815` pins as the documented default (so it is intent, not a silent bug; the disclosure gap is the residue) · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:1139` — the plugin loads the store's public door, which is how an injected executor reaches this seam · Measured: `grep -rn "withServerSideTimeout" --include=*.mjs` = 1 definition, 1 production call, 2 test references; `grep -rn "dbExecutor"` = 12+ test call sites and no production injector; `grep -rn "statement_timeout"` touches only these two files; the `allowRemote` dead-hatch datum in the candidate is REFUTED (13 hits, live readers at `vector-sync.mjs:52` via `:414` and `knowledge-adapter.mjs:103`, plus `tests/m7-t5-remediation.test.mjs:1223-1265`)
- Reproduction: static and read-only — read `:291-294` and `:391` and note the bound is in the arm body, not before the split; then run `node --test tests/m11-t1-postgres-sql-gate.test.mjs` (which injects `dbExecutor` at `:301,337,464,470,488,605,611,648`) and observe that no assertion can see a `statement_timeout` because the injected arm never receives one.
- Acceptance criteria: `executePostgresQuery` applies the server-side bound once before the arm split (so the injected executor also receives it, or the arm refuses explicitly), the fallback is disclosed rather than invisible, and a test asserting the executor arm receives the bounded statement goes red when the bound is moved back into the child arm.
- Related findings: the fix that introduced the helper is commit `131c768` (its message names the legacy id `F-054`, which resolves in neither the live register nor `CHANGELOG.md` — cite the sha) · `R10-L05-3` disposition (PARTLY CORRECT-AS-IS) already narrowed `validateOllamaUrl` as sound · the `allowRemote` half of the candidate is struck · nguồn: R10 V11 — T06/T08 candidate verification

### F-568 — the dependency-audit baseline compares aggregate severity counts only and the bare audit step it is ordered after feeds it nothing, so an equal-severity advisory swap stays green

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R10-T09-3`
- Affected modules: Gates + CI, Build & runtime config
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `security.yml:50-51` runs `node scripts/audit-baseline.mjs` on every Security workflow run and the verdict is the count comparison in `compareAuditToBaseline`, so a dependency change that replaces recorded advisories with different advisories of the same severity prints the baseline-held answer
- Root cause: the baseline record carries six integers and the reader extracts six integers (`audit-baseline.mjs:64-75`), so the compared value is an aggregate of the advisory set rather than the set; and `readAuditReport:120-131` re-runs the audit itself while the earlier bare step (`security.yml:44-49`) is non-blocking with discarded stdout, so `checkAuditBaselineWorkflow`'s ordering assertion at `repository-policy.mjs:1896-1903` enforces an order between two steps that share no artefact.
- Evidence: Where: `scripts/audit-baseline.mjs:18-20` — the five severities plus total as the only keys · `:64-75` — counts extracted, nothing else · `:84-112` — the per-severity growth test plus the total test, `:100` skipping unrecorded severities · `:120-131` — the script's own spawn of the audit command · Where: `.github/security/npm-audit-baseline.json:5-13` — the six counts and a prose note, no package, range or advisory identity · Where: `.github/workflows/security.yml:44-49` — the bare audit, `continue-on-error: true`, stdout unwritten · `:50-51` — the comparison step · Where: `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:1887-1903` — the shape check requiring the non-blocking flag and the ordering · Measured: static read of all four files; the audit command was not run.
- Reproduction: read-only — compare the baseline's six keys against the comparison's only predicates; two different packages at the same severity produce identical counts, so the ok flag stays true. Note `:66` falls back to the per-package map and `:71` returns null for it, so an unreadable shape fails closed — this is not a fail-open claim.
- Acceptance criteria: the baseline records the advisory identity set (package name, vulnerable range, severity, advisory id) taken from the same json report the comparison already parses, and `compareAuditToBaseline` fails on any observed member absent from the record with the count ceiling kept as a secondary check — a case that swaps two recorded highs for two different highs must redden; alternatively the unshared bare-audit step and the `:1896-1903` ordering sentence are removed together.
- Related findings: legacy `R5-E09-06`, named in `audit-baseline.mjs:1` as `F-188` which resolves in neither register file, bounded the bare audit's exit status · F-458 and F-481 are adjacent gate-coverage rows with different mechanisms · nguồn: R10 V09 — audit baseline operand set at cd41974

### F-569 — the shared-network driver comparison needs both operands present, so the two compose files that consume platform without a driver are never reconciled and the gate prints pass

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R10-T09-4`
- Affected modules: deployment topology, Gates + CI, infrastructure scripts
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: `repo:check` runs this walk in the CI quality-gate at `ci.yml:46`, and at HEAD the driver comparison is inert for 2 of the 3 compose files that declare the shared network, so a driver drift introduced by dropping the key is invisible
- Root cause: `compareSharedNetworks:1544` fires only when the shared driver and the declared driver are both truthy, so a compose declaration that omits the driver key — the ordinary case, since the shared file is meant to be the single definition — never mismatches anything; the only other asymmetry the function can raise (`:1552-1557`) is a declared network no file consumes. The reconciliation also reads only the driver key (`:1542-1543`), so other shared attributes are outside its operand set.
- Evidence: Where: `scripts/repository-policy.mjs:1538-1548` — the per-file walk, the both-truthy guard and its single failure sentence · `:1552-1558` — the consume-only asymmetry · Where: `infrastructure/networking/networks.yml:23-25` — platform declared with `driver: bridge` · Where: `infrastructure/docker/compose/compose.core.yml:221-222` — the only compared file, repeating `driver: bridge` · `compose.knowledge.yml:20-22` — platform with labels only · `compose.m3-t5.acceptance.yml:53-57` — platform with `internal: true` and labels · Measured: the grep above returns 3 declaring files of which 1 carries the driver key; `ls infrastructure/docker/compose/` returns 7 tracked compose files.
- Reproduction: read-only — run the grep above; adding a `driver: overlay` line to `compose.knowledge.yml` makes the comparison fire, whereas the current omission of the key produces no failure at all, which is the unreachable half of the predicate. No compose file was edited.
- Acceptance criteria: a compose file that consumes a shared network without declaring a driver is reported, either as a finding or as an explicit inherited-driver assertion, so removing the both-truthy guard reddens a new case in `tests/repository-policy.test.mjs` for each of `compose.knowledge.yml` and `compose.m3-t5.acceptance.yml`; the silently skipped parse-error path is not this row's fix site because legacy `R10-L09-3` owns it.
- Related findings: same walker as legacy `R10-L09-3` (unparseable compose files dropped at `:1622` and `:1624-1626`), filed separately · `tests/m5-docker-contract.test.mjs` parses the same files and is the partial backstop · nguồn: R10 V09 — compose driver operand census

### F-570 — the agent-facing knowledge contract forbids every provenance field the Knowledgebase publishes, so a governed agent cannot re-verify a citation it was just given

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R10-T10-1`
- Affected modules: Zink Knowledgebase, Zink Tools (workflow contracts)
- Severity: Medium
- Status: PARTIAL — the service half is closed by `d010a7a` (span keys restored by `4ebd0ce`): the search, chunk and citation items now publish `line_start`/`line_end`/`page_start`/`page_end` on the ITEM, so a consumer that rebuilds its own item has values to forward. The acceptance criterion's contract half is NOT shipped and is the RED the row itself flags: `zink-tools/contracts/tools/zink-knowledge.schema.json` still closes the item and the five-key metadata object, and the adapter still rebuilds without the span, so a governed agent still cannot see them. Owner decision needed: change the published tool contract (item + metadata) and forward the span, or re-scope the criterion to the service route (2026-10-24).
- Root cause: the service grew source identity (the F-072-era projection of `sources.sha256` into both the result and the citation object) but the published tool contract kept a closed item and a closed five-key metadata object, and the adapter rebuilds its item from scratch instead of forwarding. A moved or edited source file therefore still yields an identical agent-visible citation string with no digest anywhere in the envelope.
- Evidence: Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:616-617` and `:627-643` — `source_id`, `source_sha256`, `line_start`, `line_end`, `page_start`, `page_end`, `bbox`, `markdown` published per result · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/knowledge/knowledge-adapter.mjs:704-731` — the item is rebuilt as `chunk_id`, `title`, `text`, `score`, `citation` string, `metadata` of `source_path`, `heading_path`, `source_type`, `page_start`, `page_end` only · Where: `zink-tools/contracts/tools/zink-knowledge.schema.json:95-111` — the metadata object, five keys, `"additionalProperties": false` · Where: `zink-tools/contracts/tools/zink-knowledge.schema.json:108-111` and `:130` — the closed item and closed envelope · Measured: predicate `grep -rn "source_sha256" zink-tools/` = 0 hits; the only `docs/contracts/` hits are `execution/archive/2026-10-15-M12-T2.1-R3.md:241`, `.../2026-10-17-M12-T2.2-R3.md:43` and `:165`
- Reproduction: Read-only at HEAD: the union of keys the envelope may carry is the contract's, and the digest is outside it, so no service-side field can survive. Note the fix is a contract change and therefore RED under `AGENTS.md` §7 — it needs an owner decision, not a patch.
- Acceptance criteria: the contract the agent reads carries the same identity the service publishes — `source_id`, `source_sha256` and the line span declared on the result item, forwarded by the adapter — plus a cross-module test that pipes a service search envelope through `knowledge-adapter.mjs` and asserts `source_sha256` survives. Red today at the schema level (`additionalProperties: false`) and at the adapter (`:719-730`); removing either the declaration or the forwarding turns it red again.
- Related findings: R10-L07-1 (for a PDF the field the contract omits is itself the wrong quantity, so both must be fixed for re-verification to work) · R10-T10-4 (citations are not re-derivable after an incremental reindex because `segment_id` is not in the citation string) · nguồn: R10 V06 — provenance reachability at the agent boundary

### F-571 — the knowledge adapter recomputes total_count from the formatted page and discards has_more, returned_count and candidate_pool_count, so the agent sees the pre-F-132 truncated semantics one hop after the service fix

- Category: F4 — Integration & Contract Violations
- Legacy register id: `R10-T10-2`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow contracts), Zink Knowledgebase
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: every successful governed `zink-knowledge` search or retrieval call
- Root cause: the service was corrected to report pool and page separately, but the adapter builds its envelope independently and sets `total_count` to the number of rows it formatted, and the published contract declares one integer with `additionalProperties: false`, so the three other signals have nowhere to go. The agent-facing route therefore loses the distinction between "N matches exist" and "N rows were returned".
- Evidence: Where: `apps/knowledge/app/main.py:682-685` — the four fields the service emits, with the F-132/F-140 comment at `:674-681` · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/knowledge/knowledge-adapter.mjs:734-741` — `// M14: total_count matches formattedResults length` then `total_count: formattedResults.length` · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/knowledge/knowledge-adapter.mjs:371-373` — `Math.max(1, Math.min(20, Number(limit ?? top_k ?? 5)))`, so the adapter requests the same ceiling the service pools at · Where: `zink-tools/contracts/tools/zink-knowledge.schema.json:113-116` and `:130` — one `total_count` integer, closed envelope · Measured: predicate `grep -n "has_more|candidate_pool_count|returned_count"` against the adapter = 0 hits; the only `total_count` writers are `:674`, `:741`, `:755`, `:773`
- Reproduction: Read-only: trace a `limit: 5` search — the service returns `total_count` up to 20 with `has_more: true`, the adapter returns `total_count: 5` with no other count field. Executing it needs the live service, so the claim is code-proven at both seams rather than measured on the wire. Surfacing the signals through Zink Tools is a contract change and therefore RED under `AGENTS.md` §7.
- Acceptance criteria: the adapter forwards the service's pool and page signals instead of recomputing them, with `truncated` or `has_more` declared on the envelope item, and one cross-module test that feeds a service envelope with `total_count` greater than the page length through `knowledge-adapter.mjs` and asserts the envelope does not report the page length as the total. Red today; reverting `:741` to a forward of the service value turns it green and removing the forward turns it red again.
- Related findings: F-140 (`findings.md:275-286`) — **amend only**: line drift `main.py:658-669` → `:670-687`, `database.py:1060`/`:1079` → `:1521`/`:1542`, adapter `:441` → `:734-741`; its `Status: REFUTED` measured the service operand and still holds, so the consumer seam gets this row instead of a status flip · R10-L07-3 (the same field at the service, where the four signals collapse at the ceiling) · R10-T10-1 (the closed item shape that blocks the provenance fix at the same seam) · nguồn: R10 V06 — count reporting at the consumer seam

### F-572 — embedding identity is a stored checked column on the Knowledge side and a hardcoded constant on the Skill side, so no row can say which model produced it

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R10-T10-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Skill Store, Zink Knowledgebase, Zink Tools (workflow contracts)
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: a sync interrupted mid-loop, or a derived row the catalog scan no longer produces while the tombstone pass is refused, leaves rows marked ACTIVE and READY whose vectors came from a different model than the ones re-embedded in the same pass, and the read path scores them together
- Root cause: `skills.catalog_index` and `skills.segments` carry no model name, model digest or dimension column, so model identity exists only as the caller's constants; the write side can mark a row READY from a previous run and never revisit it, and the read filter selects on recency and status rather than identity. The Knowledgebase already demonstrates the shape the Skill Store lacks.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-skill-store/migrations/003_skills_vector_schema.sql:9-25` — catalog_index columns, none of them model identity · Where: `zink-skill-store/migrations/003_skills_vector_schema.sql:27-36` — segments columns, `embedding vector(1024) NOT NULL` with no provenance · Where: `apps/knowledge/migrations/001_initial.sql:27-29` — `embedding_model_name`, `embedding_model_digest` with a 64-hex CHECK, `embedding_dimensions` pinned to 1024 · Where: `zink-skill-store/src/vector-sync.mjs:1019-1027` — the code concedes a stored per-row model identity is required and was deferred as a schema migration · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/skill-store/skill-adapter.mjs:896-905` — `sync_status: "READY"`, `embedding_model: EXPECTED_MODEL_NAME`, `embedding_version: "1.0.0"` are constants, not projections · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/skill-store/skill-adapter.mjs:639` — the serving filter is `status = 'ACTIVE'` and `sync_status = 'READY'` only · Where: `zink-skill-store/src/vector-sync.mjs:14,471,485-489` — model identity is checked per HTTP call, never per stored row · Measured: the candidate's High harm path is narrowed by two guards it did not cite — `vector-sync.mjs:1121-1152` marks a failed skill FAILED and deletes its segments, and `:950-957` names a REFUSED tombstone pass, which is where stale ACTIVE rows can survive.
- Reproduction: requires PostgreSQL, so it is not executed here: roll `EXPECTED_MODEL_NAME` or the endpoint's model, run a sync that dies after some skills commit, then issue a retrieval — the served set is exactly `status = 'ACTIVE' AND sync_status = 'READY'` and mixes vectors from both passes while the envelope reports one model. Read-only evidence is the absence of any identity column in `003` and the constants at `skill-adapter.mjs:896-905`.
- Acceptance criteria: identity travels with the vector — a model name and digest column on `skills.catalog_index` (or `skills.segments`) written by the sync and projected plus compared by the read path, and the three constants removed from the provenance block; a row written under one model and read under another must be refused rather than scored, and an assertion over a mixed-identity table must turn a non-skippable lane red.
- Related findings: F-133 is EXCLUDED and does not carry a model-identity mechanism · the same read-side tautology class as R10-T10-5's `sync_status` rollup and R10-T01-2 · the Knowledge-side shape to copy is `001_initial.sql:27-29` · nguồn: R10 V07 — embedding identity storage asymmetry

### F-575 — knowledge.segments re-declares the citation columns of knowledge.sources with no trigger, CHECK or per-segment content digest, so search reports derived copies against a canonical digest nothing ties them to

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R10-T10-6`
- Affected modules: Zink Knowledgebase
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD: every writer of `knowledge.segments` is inside `apps/knowledge/app/database.py` and transactional, so no HEAD path can desynchronise the copies; the gap is the absent schema constraint and the absent agreement assert, not a reachable wrong citation
- Root cause: the schema denormalises `source_path`, `title`, `primary_topic` and `tags` into `knowledge.segments` for retrieval speed but declares no foreign-key-checked equality, no trigger and no per-segment content digest, so the derived rows' agreement with the canonical row holds by writer convention only while the search and citation paths read the copies and take the digest from `sources` in a separate statement.
- Evidence: Where: `apps/knowledge/migrations/001_initial.sql:71-81` — sources declares `source_path`, `sha256` with the 64-hex CHECK, `title`, `tags`, `UNIQUE (index_id, source_path)` · Where: `:100-138` — segments re-declares `source_path`, `title`, `tags`, declares no digest column, and only `UNIQUE (index_id, source_id, chunk_ordinal)` plus the cascade FK tie it to the parent · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/database.py:1514-1518` and `:1534-1539` — the citation and search reads project the copied columns · Where: `:1552` and `:1573` `_source_digests`, plus `:1129` `SELECT s.sha256 AS content_identity_digest` — identity is always taken from `sources`, never re-derived from the segment · Measured: `grep -c -i "create trigger" apps/knowledge/migrations/001_initial.sql` = 0; `grep -n "sha256" 001_initial.sql` yields the sources column only.
- Reproduction: read-only, executed: the two greps above plus the column listing; firing the harm needs an UPDATE to `knowledge.sources.source_path` or `title` outside `database.py` (a repair script or a manual statement), which no HEAD code performs and which cannot be demonstrated without touching a real database — forbidden for this lane.
- Acceptance criteria: either project the citation fields through the FK (`JOIN knowledge.sources` in the citation and search reads at `database.py:1514-1518`/`:1534-1539`) or store a per-segment content digest, and extend the existing invariant family (the `assert_searchable_segment_type_domain` shape) with a `segments`-to-`sources` agreement assert bound in a non-skippable lane rather than in the may-skip Postgres lane F-481 covers.
- Related findings: F-481 (verified live `findings.md:1678`, why a Postgres-lane assert can skip) · R10-T10-4 (same read seam, the missing ACTIVE predicate) · R10-T10-3/V06-V07's provenance family — not merged: those rows are about embedding identity and envelope fields, this one about un-constrained denormalisation · nguồn: R10 V12 — T10 provenance lane (verdict)

### F-576 — the search filter NFC-normalises a path prefix while sources stores the raw writer form, so a non-NFC path silently matches nothing

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R10-T10-7`
- Affected modules: Zink Knowledgebase
- Severity: Low
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable: a source path stored in non-NFC form is returned by no prefix filter, so retrieval answers empty while the row is indexed and citable by id
- Root cause: the read seam normalises the operator's prefix to NFC before matching, but the stored column carries the writer's bytes with no normalisation constraint and no writer-side normalisation, so the two forms are compared without being brought to one form.
- Evidence: Where: `apps/knowledge/app/retrieval.py:214-222` — `_normalize_path_prefix` applies `unicodedata.normalize("NFC", …)` and rejects absolute or dot segments · Where: `apps/knowledge/migrations/001_initial.sql:71` — `source_path text NOT NULL CHECK (source_path !~ '(^|/)\.\.(/|$)')`, the only constraint, plus `UNIQUE (index_id, source_path)` at `:81` · Measured: `grep -rn "NFC|unicodedata" apps/knowledge/app/*.py` returns 9 NFC call sites — tags, titles, stems, content and every filter — none on a stored source path · Measured: the same census is why this is asymmetry rather than duplicate rows.
- Reproduction: read-only static; firing it needs a vault path whose name is stored in NFD (combining diacritics) and a query prefix typed in NFC, which then matches no row — no PostgreSQL and no repository write were used.
- Acceptance criteria: one form on both sides — either normalise `source_path` to NFC at ingest and add a CHECK, or drop the read-side normalisation — and a case that indexes a decomposed-form path and asserts the NFC prefix filter returns it; removing the normalisation from either side must turn `npm run test:knowledge` red.
- Related findings: the tombstone-key half of this census adds nothing to F-428 and its grammar is enforced at `zink-skill-store/src/catalog.mjs:31,341-343` · the empty-digest half is guarded at `zink-tools/adapters/skill-store/skill-adapter.mjs:967-971` · nguồn: R10 V07 — path normalisation asymmetry in retrieval

### F-577 — the ledger commits the journal append before the snapshot it derives, names that snapshot temp by pid alone with no sweeper, and re-charges BUDGET_RESERVED on retry because the event carries no idempotency key

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R10-T12-1`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), OpenClaw governance plugin, infrastructure scripts
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — needs fault injection: no gate injects a fault between `ledger.mjs:1560` and `:1570` and none pre-creates `snapshot.json.tmp-<pid>`, but the append-then-snapshot order and the pid-only exclusive open are unconditional on every write, and the double-charge needs only that the caller retry after any snapshot failure
- Root cause: one commit seam has three durable artefacts written in sequence (journal, snapshot, checkpoint) with no rollback or fencing, the snapshot temp is derived from `process.pid` alone so a recycled pid makes the exclusive `openSync(...,"wx")` fail permanently and that failure happens outside the handler that cleans residue, and `reserveBudgetAtomically` records no caller-supplied key, so replay cannot tell a repeated reservation from a new one.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/ledger.mjs:1557-1581` — append+fsync at `:1559-1560`, `writeSnapshotAtomic` at `:1570`, `writeCheckpointAtomic` at `:1575` · Where: `:1603-1624` — `:1605` pid-only temp, `:1607` `openSync(temp,"wx")` before the `try` at `:1609`, cleanup only in the `writeFault` branch `:1617-1621`, with the module's own blocking warning at `:1588-1590` · Where: `:1437-1450` — `withWriterLock` maps only `FILE_LOCK_BUSY` to `EXECUTION_STATE_UNKNOWN` and rethrows everything else raw · Where: `:1675-1692` and `:1362-1374` — `BUDGET_RESERVED` payload has no key and replay recomputes counters from the live `BUDGETS` constants · Measured: `grep -nE "readdirSync|sweep|[Rr]esidue" ledger.mjs` = 4 hits, 0 `readdirSync`; temp-name census over `integrations zink-skill-store zink-tools` = 9 expressions (3 nonce, 4 pid+ms, 2 pid-only).
- Reproduction: read-only static sequence plus a predicate-only probe: run the `.tmp-` census above and confirm `ledger.mjs:386`/`:1605` are the only two names with no entropy and no directory sweep; `tests/m11-t3-durable-state-integrity.test.mjs:839-870` exercises `unlinkWriterResidue` standalone and concedes at `:872` that nothing on the host makes `writeFileSync` fail, so no gate covers the window. Firing the double-charge for real needs a fault injected between `:1560` and `:1570`, which this lane was forbidden to do.
- Acceptance criteria: the commit seam returns a distinguishable COMMITTED / NOT_ATTEMPTED / UNKNOWN so `EXECUTION_STATE_UNKNOWN` stops covering the never-attempted lock-busy case (`ledger.mjs:1443-1448`), the snapshot and checkpoint temp names carry the `pid-Date.now()-randomBytes` entropy already used at `catalog.mjs:978`, the exclusive open moves inside the fault handler with residue cleanup, `BUDGET_RESERVED` carries a caller-supplied idempotency key the reducer can discard (the `discarded` pattern in the same module), and a fault-injection lane turns red when a retry appends the same reservation twice.
- Related findings: F-417 (verified live `findings.md:846`, the queue's post-rename rethrow of a committed write — same ordering family, retry-idempotent store) · F-401 (`:638`) and F-407 (`:716`) unrelated to budget · F-090 (`:222`, same counters, different mechanism) · F-408 (`:729`) and F-414 (`:807`) are the checkpoint-repair lanes that cannot clear this state · coupled with `R10-T12-3` (V12): that row's ceiling re-derivation can only fire on this row's un-pruned journal residue · nguồn: R10 V12 — T12 ordering lane (verdict)

### F-578 — the apply ceremony's mutual exclusion is an unlocked read-then-write and its spend-once receipt lands at the far end of the mutation window, contradicting its own exclusivity docblock

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R10-T12-2`
- Affected modules: deployment topology, infrastructure scripts
- Severity: High
- Status: OPEN — production-reachable, conditional on interleaving: two overlapping `operator-apply` invocations (or a retry while a prior run hangs mid-mutation) both read zero orphans at `apply-orchestration.mjs:128`, both persist separate transactions, and both mutate — no lock spans the window; single-operator clean runs do not fire it
- Root cause: `findOrphanedTransaction` is a pure read, so orphan-check → persist-snapshot → mutate is a classic unlocked read-then-write, and the builder receipt is checked at `configure.mjs:2042` but marked only at `:2323` after backup, config, workspace and systemd mutations; the exclusive receipt write protects one attemptId from double-spend, which is not the race the orphan-check gate actually admits, yet its docblock (`transaction-snapshot.mjs:1020-1022`) claims two racing applies "cannot both believe they own it".
- Evidence: Where: `topology/fixtures/runtime-regression/apply-orchestration.mjs:127-186` — unlocked check → persist → mutate sequence · Where: `topology/lib/transaction-snapshot.mjs:1006-1017,1024-1029,1020-1022` — existsSync check, exclusive mark, over-claiming docblock · Where: `configure.mjs:2042` vs `:2323` — assert/mark split across ~280 mutation lines · Measured: predicate grep `from ".*file-lock` repo-wide — the deploy lane (configure.mjs, apply-orchestration.mjs, transaction-snapshot.mjs, operator-apply.mjs) imports the lock primitive nowhere, while ledger/guard/queue/decision-ledger/catalog all do · Measured: findings.md grep `findOrphaned|APPLY_ALREADY_SPENT|two applies` → only row F-416 (different mechanism)
- Reproduction: static order proof at HEAD (running any driver is forbidden for audit lanes): read the 128→186 span and the 2042/2323 pair — between them nothing serializes; the concurrency harm needs two processes and is claimed only as interleaving, exactly like the live F-433 precedent.
- Acceptance criteria: one lock (the shipped `withFileLockSync`/`acquire`) held across orphan-check → persist → complete, keyed on the transaction root, plus consume the receipt BEFORE `runBuilder("--apply")` so `APPLY_ALREADY_SPENT` becomes the pre-mutation refusal, and the docblock at `:1020-1022` states the guarantee actually delivered; a two-process test on a scratch state dir asserting the second apply refuses before mutating — red when the lock or the pre-mutation mark is removed.
- Related findings: F-416 (same ceremony, residue mechanism) · F-419/F-422 (verdicts and mixed state this race makes reachable) · F-433 (interleaving-class reachability precedent) · round-1 candidate `R10-L04-1` R16_INSIDE waiver note · nguồn: R10 V02 — cross-module lock-absence census

### F-579 — per-task budget ceilings are re-derived from the BUDGETS constants at replay time, so tightening a ceiling turns any ledger with unanchored budget events in its tail into permanent EXECUTION_STATE_UNKNOWN while the journal carries an era flag for the other class of such change

- Category: F6 — Reliability & Performance
- Legacy register id: `R10-T12-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (workflow topology), Build & runtime config
- Severity: Low
- Status: LATENT — code-proven, untriggerable at HEAD for a settled ledger: replay reaches `reserveBudget` (`ledger.mjs:1363`) only for events after the checkpoint anchor (`:1330-1331`, `:1376-1377`), so a lowered ceiling only fires when the tail still holds budget events — the state R10-T12-1 produces — and no HEAD-side change to `BUDGETS` (`:62`) is in flight
- Root cause: the ceiling is a live code constant rather than a value committed with the reservation, so the reducer's arithmetic depends on the build that reads the journal instead of the build that wrote it, and the fault any replay raises is mapped to `EXECUTION_STATE_UNKNOWN` at `:1428-1433`.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/ledger.mjs:1651-1655` — `limit` re-evaluated from `BUDGETS.maxBriefRevisionsPerTask` / `maxStackTransitionsPerTask` / `maxTotalDelegatedRunsPerTask` per call · Where: `:1656-1664` — `task[key] > limit - amount` raises `WORKFLOW_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` · Where: `:1362-1368` — replay calls `reserveBudget` and compares the recomputed counters · Where: `:1428-1433` — any replay fault becomes `EXECUTION_STATE_UNKNOWN` · Where: `:1548-1555`, `:95`, `:1060`, `:1578` — the `has_pre_verdict_stamp` era precedent with no budget counterpart · Measured: `grep -n "BUDGETS\." ledger.mjs` = only the declaration at `:62` region and the three reads at `:1652-1654`; no persisted ceiling exists anywhere in the module.
- Reproduction: read-only, static at HEAD: open `:1363` and `:1651-1664` together and note the replay call site uses the live constant; firing the fault requires a journal whose checkpoint does not cover its tail plus a build in which one ceiling is lowered — the fault-injection itself needs a scratch state root and was not executed because the live ledger and full gates are forbidden for this lane.
- Acceptance criteria: the ceiling in force is committed into the `BUDGET_RESERVED` payload (or an era bit is stamped the way `has_pre_verdict_stamp` is at `:1548-1555`) and replayed against the recorded value instead of `BUDGETS` at `:1651-1655`; a lane turns red when lowering any `BUDGETS` constant in a scratch checkout makes an existing journal unreadable with `EXECUTION_STATE_UNKNOWN`.
- Related findings: R10-T12-1 (this row is downstream of that ordering defect: it supplies the unanchored tail) · F-090 (verified live `findings.md:222`) · F-408 (`:729`) and F-414 (`:807`) — the repair lanes that report this code and do not recover it · F-481 (`:1678`) why no gate counts the Postgres/lane coverage · coupled with `R10-T12-1` (V12): a settled journal survives a ceiling edit, so this row needs that row's residue before it can produce EXECUTION_STATE_UNKNOWN · nguồn: R10 V12 — T12 ordering lane (verdict)

### F-580 — a promoted Skill Store version that fails re-validation while the rollback removal also fails strands a canonical directory no sweeper can match and takes every governed skill read dark

- Category: F3 — State & Lifecycle Integrity
- Legacy register id: `R10-T12-5`
- Affected modules: Zink Skill Store, Zink Tools (workflow contracts)
- Severity: High
- Status: PARTIAL — the stranded-state half is closed by `a7c8239`: `quarantineInvalidVersion` renames a canonical pair that fails validation into the residue grammar under the store-wide writer lock, `scanCatalog` treats `.stranded-*` as residue, and `tests/agent-skill-F-580.test.mjs` proves the dark read arms, the repair and its refusals. The promotion-path fallback is NOT proven: `promoteVersion` now quarantines when its rollback removal fails (`catalog.mjs:1920-1928`), but disabling that branch leaves the lane 3/3 green — the shipped case builds the stranded directory directly instead of promoting a record that fails re-validation with the removal forced to fail, which is what the acceptance criterion asks for. Owner decision not needed: the missing case can be written; the row stays until it is (2026-10-24).
- Root cause: `catalog.mjs:1694-1716` catches a failed `rm(versionDir)` into `removalError`, reports it in prose plus a code list and returns the refusal, leaving the directory canonical-named and complete; the residue machinery then cannot see it, because `isStagingResidue:1002-1003` matches only the `.pending-` grammar produced by `stagingName():990`, the scan pushes residue only through that predicate at `:775-781`, `sweepWriterResidue:1284-1291` removes only what that list names, and `healWriterResidue:1253-1265` removes the target directory only when one of `manifest.json` or `SKILL.md` is missing, which a content-invalid complete pair is not
- Evidence: Where: `zink-skill-store/src/catalog.mjs:1694-1716` — revalidation, swallowed `rm` failure, `PROMOTED_REVALIDATION_FAILED` refusal · Where: `:1582-1596` — any catalog error refuses the next promotion with `PREEXISTING_CATALOG_ERRORS` · Where: `:1002-1003`, `:775-781`, `:1253-1265`, `:1284-1291`, `:1602` — the three residue grammars, none of which matches a complete canonical pair that fails content validation · Where: `:313`, `:497`, `:542-556`, `:595-619`, `:654-655` — the pair-level error codes that land in `errors` rather than `residue` · Where: `zink-tools/adapters/skill-store/skill-adapter.mjs:135-148` — `loadVerifiedRecord` throws `SKILL_CATALOG_INVALID` on any catalog error before it looks at the target id, called by the two production read arms at `:485` and `:556` · Measured: predicate `git grep -n "quarantine\|stranded\|\.stranded-"` over `zink-skill-store` and `zink-tools` returns 3 hits, all comments, so no shipped repair mechanism exists · Measured: predicate `git grep -n loadVerifiedRecord` returns the definition, the two production call sites and two documentation mentions · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/file-lock.mjs:51-62` — the retryable removal errno set that makes a failed `rm` a host-normal condition · Corrected by this lane: the read outage is the adapter's error gate, not a blanked record set — F-222 removed that collapse at `catalog.mjs:919-927`, and the collapse quoted at `:766-774` is historical F-150 rationale
- Reproduction: read-only geometry proof: run `scanCatalog` over a store root containing one complete version pair whose manifest fails validation and observe the path appears in `errors` and never in `residue`, then read the four sweeper predicates to confirm none can remove it; the strand itself needs the rename plus a forced `rm` failure, which requires the live state root and is not reproducible read-only
- Acceptance criteria: a lock-held shipped repair for the canonical-but-failing case — quarantine by rename into a directory the scanner treats as residue, reusing the same grammar so `sweepWriterResidue` clears it — plus the stranded and `*.tmp-*` shapes added to the residue grammar so this and F-410's residue are covered by one mechanism; a shipped case promotes a record that fails re-validation with the removal forced to fail, and asserts the next `scanCatalog` is error-free and both read arms still serve, so the case reddens if the quarantine or the grammar is removed
- Related findings: F-410 (LATENT, scoped to `manifest.json.tmp-*`, does not cover a canonical directory) · F-427 is the re-validation-failure class its first leg needs · F-133/F-429/F-430 are derived-index states, not this state · `R10-T04-5` (isSymbolicLink-only link guards) is the same file's other unwritten boundary · promotes `R10-L05`'s write-only residue claim to a read-path outage · nguồn: R10 V05 — Skill Store / Zink Tools verdict lane

### F-581 — outbound call budgets and the file-lock deadline are computed from the wall clock while the repo's own Python child uses a monotonic clock, so a clock step truncates or silently extends a governed budget

- Category: F6 — Reliability & Performance
- Legacy register id: `R10-T12-6`
- Affected modules: Zink Tools (SQL/n8n/Python guards), OpenClaw governance plugin
- Severity: Medium
- Status: OPEN — needs fault injection: a wall-clock step (forward → spurious `KNOWLEDGE_SERVICE_TIMEOUT` after one attempt; backward → one call exceeds `MAX_KNOWLEDGE_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS` and the 300 s run budget F-184 protects); credible on this WSL2↔Windows host topology but not observed in-repo
- Root cause: the JS seams express their budgets as `Date.now()` deltas (`knowledge-adapter.mjs:438/441/616`, `file-lock.mjs:323`, and the `busyWaitMs` spin at `:64-70`), while wall time is not monotonic; the repo already treats monotonic time as the correct source in its Python child (`ocr.py` deadline arms, corroborated by live row F-441's evidence), and `AbortSignal.timeout` (`zink-tools/n8n/trigger.mjs:207`) shows the monotonic-safe arm exists in JS too.
- Evidence: Where: `zink-tools/adapters/knowledge/knowledge-adapter.mjs:438-441` — deadline set and remaining-budget reader · Where: `:616` — the retry loop consumes it · Where: `:34-43` — the F-184 run-budget relationship the ceiling encodes · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/file-lock.mjs:323` — acquire-loop deadline · Where: `:64-70` — Date.now busy spin · Measured: predicate `grep -rn "performance.now|monotonic" integrations zink-tools zink-skill-store --include=*.mjs` → 0 JS call sites (2 comments only); `grep -rEn "Date\.now\(\) \+" integrations/openclaw zink-tools` → 7 sites, the 3 quoted among them.
- Reproduction: static read (quoted lines are literal-comparable); a behavioural probe needs a clock-step or an injectable time source, and `MAX_KNOWLEDGE_RETRY_AFTER_MS` clamping at `:595-605` was re-checked clean so the only lever is the clock.
- Acceptance criteria: one monotonic convention (`performance.now()`) for every deadline/backoff in the JS seams, with a gate that refuses `Date.now()` inside a deadline/remaining/until expression (scoped, since 7 legitimate wall-clock arithmetic sites exist for expiry timestamps) and a test that a simulated forward step does not truncate a knowledge call whose monotonic budget remains.
- Related findings: F-398 (different mechanism: durable freshness, not clock source) · F-441 (different seam: expired-deadline misattribution in the Python child) · F-184 (the ceiling this clock depends on) · nguồn: R10 V12 — outbound budgets (adjudicated by V03)

### F-582 — the component-walk link guards were filed as junction-blind because their only junction test was read as a no-op, and both halves measure false at HEAD, so no symlink defence is bypassed

- Category: F5 — Data Integrity & Result Correctness
- Legacy register id: `R10-T04-5`
- Affected modules: OpenClaw governance plugin, Zink Tools (workflow topology)
- Severity: Medium
- Status: REFUTED — premise measured false at HEAD: libuv reports a Windows mount-point reparse point as `lstatSync(…).isSymbolicLink() === true`, so the guards at `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/ledger.mjs:236` and `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:2262-2276` do refuse a junction, and the "its only junction test is a no-op" half died when the suite was run at HEAD `cd41974` — `node --test tests/agent-lib-F-044.test.mjs` reports `tests 2 / pass 2 / fail 0 / cancelled 0 / skipped 0`, so the case executes its assertions on this host
- Root cause: the candidate inferred runtime behaviour from the shape of the predicate (`isSymbolicLink()` only, versus `realpathSync` in the containment guards) without measuring what the platform reports for a reparse point, then read the test's skip idiom as proof the case never runs; on this host that branch is not even reached, so the inference had no observation under it.
- Evidence: Where: `integrations/openclaw/topology/lib/ledger.mjs:234-238` — `if (lstatSync(cursor).isSymbolicLink()) throw new Error("LEDGER_SYMLINK_DENIED");` inside the component walk · Where: `integrations/openclaw/lib/policy.mjs:2262-2276` — `assertNoSymlinkComponents` walks segments and throws on `existsSync(current) && lstatSync(current).isSymbolicLink()` · Where: `integrations/openclaw/plugins/zink-governance/index.mjs:2815-2816` — the `realpathSync` containment guards the candidate contrasted them with · Measured: `node --test tests/agent-lib-F-044.test.mjs` at HEAD returns 2 tests, 2 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped
- Reproduction: read-only — run `node --test tests/agent-lib-F-044.test.mjs` (output quoted above) and open `ledger.mjs:234-238` plus `policy.mjs:2262-2276`: both call `isSymbolicLink()` on the exact path form a junction presents, so the value the candidate claimed slips through is refused by the shipped guard
- Acceptance criteria: no code change is licensed by this row — the refutation is recorded so a later band does not re-file the "a junction bypasses the link guards" premise; the only residual the candidate produced is the vacuous-pass skip idiom at `tests/agent-lib-F-044.test.mjs:46-54`, and that is filed on its own merits as `R10-L11-1`, not as a guard bypass
- Related findings: `R10-L11-1` (the surviving skip-idiom half — different mechanism, different fix, its own row) · `F-044` resolves only as the in-source test-name prefix at `tests/agent-lib-F-044.test.mjs:1`, not as a register id · nguồn: R10 V04 — operator drivers, the R16 rehearsal gate and the state-root guards

### F-583 — the PDF child requires and shape-checks `expected_processing_identity_digest` without comparing it, filed as unverified identity while the parent recomputes that identity per operation at the same seam

- Category: F7 — Testing & Verification Gaps
- Legacy register id: `R10-T05-3`
- Affected modules: Zink Knowledgebase
- Severity: Medium
- Status: REFUTED — premise measured false at HEAD: the operand is vestigial by design and the identity is verified per operation — `apps/knowledge/app/pdf_runtime.py:146-147` calls `_verify_locked_distributions(manifest, probe)` and `_verify_hashed_requirements(manifest, runtime_lock_path)` from inside `processing_identity_for_runtime` (`:82`), so the values the child would echo are recomputed by the parent rather than trusted
- Root cause: the candidate read the child's `_probe` (`apps/knowledge/pdf-runtime/src/zink_pdf_runtime/runner.py:81-109`, which requires the key, tests it with `re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]{64}", expected)` and never compares it) and stopped there; the docblock at `runner.py:99-104` states that this is the correction recorded under legacy id `R7-C3-05`, made because the child used to echo the value and the parent compared its own echo — so the claim describes the pre-fix design it was written away from.
- Evidence: Where: `apps/knowledge/pdf-runtime/src/zink_pdf_runtime/runner.py:81-109` — the shape-only check, and `:99-104` — the in-source note naming the earlier correction · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/pdf_runtime.py:82`, `:146-147`, `:150`, `:180` — the parent's per-operation recomputation (`_verify_locked_distributions`, `_verify_hashed_requirements`) · Where: `apps/knowledge/app/pdf_runtime.py:228`, `:271`, `:291` — the three operations, all funnelled through the same probe · Measured: `apps/knowledge/app/pdf_runtime.py:140-190` re-read at HEAD — both verify calls sit inside the identity path, not beside it
- Reproduction: read-only — open `runner.py:81-109` and then `app/pdf_runtime.py:140-190`: the digest the child receives is produced by the parent's own recomputation of the locked distributions and the hashed requirements, so "accepts any 64-hex string → extraction proceeds" does not describe an unverified-identity gap; the direction of the comparison is what `R7-C3-05` changed and it is non-tautological at HEAD
- Acceptance criteria: no code change is licensed by this row — the refutation is recorded so a later band does not re-file the unverified-identity premise or re-open the closed `R7-C3-05` fix; the only residual is that the operand is vestigial, which the in-source comment at `runner.py:99-104` already documents
- Related findings: `R10-T05-1`, `R10-T05-2`, `R10-T05-4` and Row B (`R10-L08-2` + `R10-T05-5`) are the pin/manifest family this candidate was filed inside, and none of them depends on this premise · the id `F-286` resolves neither as a live heading nor in `CHANGELOG.md`, so the closed fix is cited here by its legacy id `R7-C3-05` · nguồn: R10 V07 — PDF runtime pin and identity seam verification
